Rejected: none.

### Deviations recorded while implementing PR1 (WP0.1–WP0.3)

Live tests on `rahulnakmol/agentic-ayudha` (a public repo on a personal account, one human collaborator) on 2026-10-11. Each row is the closest enforceable alternative to a plan assumption that GitHub does not support.

| # | Plan assumption | Live result | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | WP0.1: `ledger` is pushable only by the bot identity and CI | **Partly enforceable.** A ruleset `update` restriction works on a personal repo: the founder's own token (repo admin) and Amp's user-to-server App token were both rejected (`GH013 … Cannot update this protected ref`), and a deploy key in the bypass list pushed (rule-suite `result: bypass`). But the GitHub Actions app (15368) cannot be a bypass actor on a personal repo (`422 Actor GitHub Actions integration must be part of the ruleset source or owner organization`), so CI's built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` can never be a ledger writer. Only a GitHub App installed on the owner account is accepted, and only its installation tokens bypass; user-to-server tokens act as the user. Individual users (a machine user) cannot be bypass actors; only repository roles can. The rule-suite list shows no actor for the deploy-key push while the single-suite endpoint shows the repo owner's login, so `audit` must identify deploy-key writes from the ledger commit, not from the actor name | Ruleset `ledger-writers`: deletion, non-fast-forward and update restricted; bypass = repository admin role (the founder, day-one rule). WP1.4 `init --identity` replaces the admin bypass with the bot App (Integration actor), and CI writes `ledger` with that App's installation token (`actions/create-github-app-token`), never `GITHUB_TOKEN`. The machine-user fallback becomes a deploy key (or a repository-role bypass, which covers every collaborator in that role) |
| D2 | WP0.1 solo mode: the founder approves their own protected-path PR on a commit other than the last push | **Not enforceable.** GitHub refuses self-approval (`Review Can not approve your own pull request`). A ruleset with `require_code_owner_review: true` and 0 required approvals did not block a `skills/` PR authored by the sole code owner (scratch PR #2 merged, rule-suite `result: pass`). Requiring ≥ 1 approval would block every PR to `dev`, not only protected paths, and turn every merge into a bypass | Workflow `protected-paths` (`pull_request_target`, so GitHub runs the default branch's copy and a PR cannot weaken it; it never checks out PR code) fails when a PR changes any path in the base branch's `.github/CODEOWNERS`. Ruleset `protected-paths-codeowner-review` requires that check (and keeps native CODEOWNER review switched on for when approvals become ≥ 1); its only bypass is the repository admin role in `pull_request` mode. A protected-path PR therefore merges only through an explicit admin bypass, which GitHub records in the rule-suite history with `result: bypass`; the hand-run reconciliation lists these until `audit` automates it (WP7.6). Because the workflow must be on the default branch, the gate covers `dev` from the PR after PR1; PR1 itself, which creates it, merged on green CI. WP1.4 extends the gate to pass on an approving review of the head SHA by a human CODEOWNER who is not the author (bot-authored PRs); WP5.1 switches to native CODEOWNER review with ≥ 1 approval when a second human seat is added. CODEOWNERS also covers `.agents/` and `.claude/` (host execution surfaces) and the whole of `.aayudha/config.toml`, because CODEOWNERS cannot protect the `[models]`/`[autonomy]` sections alone |
| D3 | WP0.1: linear history on `rolling`/`main` | Linear history forbids merge commits, but promotions are non-fast-forward merge commits (`Rolling snapshot: …`, `Release: v…`); and any rule placed in a ruleset whose bypass actor does the promotion is bypassed anyway | Ruleset `rolling-main-promotion`: deletion, non-fast-forward and update restricted; only the repository admin (bypass, always) can promote. No PRs target `rolling` or `main` |
| D4 | Rule 8: tag ruleset `v*` from `main` only | Rulesets cannot check which commit a tag points to | Ruleset `release-tags`: creation, update, deletion and non-fast-forward of `refs/tags/v*` restricted to the repository admin. WP0.4 `release.yml` must refuse a `v*` tag whose commit is not on `main` |
| D5 | WP0.2: Go 1.25 | `go.mod` says `go 1.25.0`, and CI builds and vets with Go 1.25.x. The orb setup already on `dev` (`.agents/setup`) pins Go 1.27.2, a supported toolchain that builds a `go 1.25` module unchanged; Go 1.25 itself no longer receives upstream fixes now that 1.27 is out | Keep `go 1.25.0` as the language floor and the 1.27.2 orb toolchain; `go vet`'s stdversion check stops newer standard-library APIs from creeping in |

### Deviations and decisions recorded while implementing PR2 (WP0.4–WP0.5)

Rows are named by WP so that parallel work does not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-0.4a | Promotions `dev` → `rolling` → `main` follow a deliberate human decision (stable only after an explicit decision; WP8.2 "brew/Scoop/winget after the stable decision") | Founder policy, 2026-10-11: for this repository promotions need **no human approval**; the gates and the commit/tag conventions are unchanged | The gates are CI jobs: `ci.yml` dispatched with `gate=rolling` (PR suite + `integration` + `benchmarks`; `release-snapshot` is the production validation) or `gate=stable` (rolling + `extended-integration` + cross-OS `smoke`; `security` always runs). They run on a `promote/…` branch holding the exact `Rolling snapshot: …` / `Release: vX.Y.Z` merge commit, which is then pushed to `rolling`/`main` as a fast-forward and tagged. An annotated `v*` tag push runs `release.yml` end to end. The stable decision is now "an explicit stable-release request plus a green stable gate". Procedure: CONTRIBUTING.md, AGENTS.md |
| D-0.4b | WP0.4: CI checks DCO and Conventional Commits | GitHub writes the squash commit on `dev` itself, and promotion merges (`Rolling snapshot: …`, `Release: v…`) are not Conventional Commits by design | The `commits` job runs on pull requests: every non-merge PR commit needs a Conventional Commit subject and a `Signed-off-by` matching its author, and the PR title (the squash subject) must be a Conventional Commit. Pushes are not re-checked |
| D-0.4c | WP0.4: path filters | A workflow-level `paths:` filter leaves a required check pending forever; a job skipped by `if:` reports success | Path filters are job conditions fed by a `changes` job (`lint`, `docs`, `release-snapshot`). `build`, `generate-drift`, `security`, and `commits` always run on PRs; pushes and dispatches run everything. Fork PRs skip `release-snapshot` because they cannot mint an OIDC token for keyless signing |
| D-0.4d | WP0.4: brew tap | GoReleaser v2 deprecates Homebrew formulae for prebuilt binaries in favour of casks | The tap receives `Casks/aayudha.rb` (macOS and Linux). The tap, the Scoop bucket, the winget fork, and their environment-scoped tokens are founder actions; until they exist the `homebrew`, `scoop`, and `winget` jobs fail with a message naming the missing secret, after the GitHub release is already published |
| D-0.4e | WP0.4: skill lint in CI | The full lint (hooks, scripts, Mobbin-origin images) is WP3.1 | `TestSkillLint` (`embed_test.go`, run by `build`) checks the embedded `skills/*/SKILL.md`: frontmatter present, `name` equals the directory, ≤ 500 lines, every `aayu <command>` resolves in the registry. WP3.1 extends it |
| D-0.4f | WP0.4: the stable gate's `extended-integration` job runs the live tests (`AAYU_LIVE=1`) against scratch repositories | The first stable gate (run 38733786670, 2026-10-15) failed every live test with "no GitHub token": no workflow ever gave the job a token that can create and delete `aayu-scratch-*` repositories, and the default `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot | Founder, 2026-10-15: the founder's classic token is stored as `AAYU_GITHUB_TOKEN` in a `live-tests` environment whose deployment branches are `dev` and `promote/*`; only `extended-integration` uses that environment. The same tests passed locally with that token. Replace it with a bot App or a narrower token when one exists |
| D-0.5a | WP0.5: `LAYERS.md` seat column reads "n/a until WP5.1" | WP0.3's registry already assigns each layer one owner seat | `LAYERS.md` keeps naming the owner seat; seat charters remain n/a until WP5.1 |
| D-0.5b | WP0.5: `CONSTRAINTS.md` generated from the embedded default config | The plan fixes some defaults (coverage 90 %, zero critical/high SAST and dependency findings, zero secrets, axe zero serious/critical at WCAG 2.2 AA, 44 px targets, 200 % text, streak 20 from WP5.7's 19/20/21 test, DAST opt-in) and names others without a value (`autonomy.never`, `autonomy.budget`, `[budget]` amounts, proof retention). WP1.2 lists `[verify]` but no `[floors]` section | `internal/config/defaults.toml` (embedded, the base layer WP1.2 adopts) holds floors under `[verify.floors.<floor>]`, plus `[autonomy]`, `[budget]`, `[proof]`. Values the plan does not fix are marked "proposed (WP0.5)" in `CONSTRAINTS.md`: never-layers L04, L07, L09; per-story build budget 8 h, 25 USD, 3 attempts; 25 USD per story, 100 USD per day, 5 USD per PR for `inspect`, 2000 CI minutes per month; 90 days retention (`retention_days`). `defaults.toml` is a protected path. `internal/config/gen` fails when a key and its documentation row disagree |

### Deviations and decisions recorded while implementing WP6.1–WP6.3

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-6.1a | WP0.4: npm licence scan (`security` job) with the allow list Apache-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, ISC, MIT | The design system is a pnpm workspace with one root `pnpm-lock.yaml` (`.agents/setup` installs from it), so the scan's `npm ci` per `package.json` fails for lack of a `package-lock.json`. ### Deviations recorded while implementing M1 (PR3–PR6)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-1.1a | WP1.1: `self-update` verifies the cosign signature and provenance | The sigstore Go libraries are not on the §2 dependency list, and verifying a keyless bundle (Fulcio chain, Rekor inclusion, certificate identity) by hand is not something to reimplement | `self-update` shells out, as `install.sh` does: `cosign verify-blob --bundle checksums.txt.sigstore.json` pinned to `…/.github/workflows/release.yml@refs/tags/<the exact tag>` and the Actions OIDC issuer, then the archive's SHA-256 against the signed `checksums.txt`, then `gh attestation verify --signer-workflow …/release.yml`. A missing tool is exit 5 with a pointer to `install.sh`; a missing or bad signature, checksum, or attestation is exit 4 and the binary is untouched. Release asset names are the WP0.4 GoReleaser names (`aayudha_<version>_<os>_<arch>.tar.gz`, `.zip` on Windows) |
| D-1.2a | WP1.2 accept: "a 24 h SLA started Friday 17:00 London time expires the following Monday 16:00" | With the `[time]` default of Mon–Fri 09:00–18:00 that instant is 8 business hours away (Friday 17–18 plus Monday 09–16). 24 business hours land on Wednesday 14:00. No consistent reading of "business hours" gives Monday 16:00 for 24 h | `[decisions] sla_hours` counts business hours literally (`config.Clock.Add`, local wall-clock windows in the IANA zone, so DST never shifts them). The table test pins both: 8 h from Friday 17:00 → Monday 16:00, and 24 h → Wednesday 14:00, each also across the 25 October 2026 clock change (16:00 and 14:00 local, now GMT) |
| D-1.2b | WP1.2 layer list "`AAYU_*` → flags" and section keys | The plan names no environment-variable or flag syntax, and gives keys without units | Env layer: `AAYU_<SECTION>__<KEY>` (double underscore between levels, e.g. `AAYU_BUDGET__PER_DAY_USD`); other `AAYU_*` variables (`AAYU_AGENT`, `AAYU_LIVE`, `AAYU_SECRET_*`, `AAYU_GITHUB_TOKEN`) are not configuration. Flags layer: a new global `--set key=value` (repeatable). Keys carry units: `[decisions] sla_hours provisional_after_hours` (business hours), `[proof] retention_days`, `[autonomy.budget] wall_hours cost_usd attempts`, `[lfs] quota_warn_percent`. The defaults layer is WP0.5's `internal/config/defaults.toml`, extended with `[lfs]`, `[decisions]`, and `[time]` defaults (Europe/London, Mon–Fri 09:00–18:00), which `CONSTRAINTS.md` does not render. A file without `version` is v0 (plan v0.1); v0→v1 drops `[lfs] server = "github"` and refuses any other server (V6). `[models.gateway]` (founder request) routes model calls through one AI gateway; see WP1.6 |
| D-1.3a | WP1.3: "after every secret-handling command" and a headless-Linux CI job for the env and file backends | V7 fixes the plumbing list, so there is no `secret` command; the plan does not say where the age key lives; provider scope APIs belong to the WP5.2 adapters | Secrets are handled by `aayu auth set-secret <name>` (value from stdin only, refused for agents), `auth delete-secret`, `auth list-secrets` (names and backends, never values), and `auth status`. File backend: an X25519 identity at `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/aayudha/age-identity.txt` and the ciphertext at `$XDG_DATA_HOME/aayudha/secrets.age`, both 0600. The canary test runs each command against a store whose keychain is unavailable (as on the `build` job's headless Ubuntu runner, which is the headless-Linux CI job) and scans home, repo, temp, and output. `[integrations.<name>] scope` is enforced before any call by `secrets.CheckScope` (a read verb refuses a `write` key); verifying the key's real scope with the provider is n/a until WP5.2 |
| D-1.4a | WP1.4: token broker "returns [the token] encrypted to the caller's ephemeral age key" | A `workflow_dispatch` run has no return channel, and `actions/create-github-app-token` revokes its token when the job ends | `aayudha-token.yml` (embedded template, installed by `init --gates`, WP2.6) validates the inputs, mints with `skip-token-revoke: true`, encrypts `{token, expires_at = now + 55 min}` to the caller's one-time age key, and uploads it as a one-day artifact named by the request ID; the client finds the run by `run-name`, downloads and deletes the artifact, and refuses a token that is expired or outlives 1 h. Tested against a fake GitHub; the live round-trip is `TestBrokerLive` behind `AAYU_LIVE=1` and `AAYU_LIVE_BROKER_REPO`, which needs the founder to create the App. CI uses `AAYU_BOT_TOKEN` from `actions/create-github-app-token`; with `--ci`, bot and ledger writes are refused without it or when it equals `GITHUB_TOKEN` (D1) |
| D-1.4b | Appendix B D2: extend `protected-paths` to pass on a human CODEOWNER's approval of the head commit | `pull_request_target` does not fire on reviews, and `pull_request_review` runs the PR's own workflow file | The gate logic is Go (`internal/identity.ProtectedPathsGate`, fixture-tested) run from a checkout of the base branch (the `pull_request_target` default; never the PR). A review fires `protected-paths-review.yml` (no token, no checkout), whose completion fires `protected-paths-rerun.yml` from the default branch, which re-runs the base-branch `protected-paths` run for that head SHA. Latest decisive review per reviewer counts, as on GitHub; every protected path needs one of its own owners; `[identity] bot_logins` is read from the base branch's `.aayudha/config.toml`. Only root-anchored literal CODEOWNERS patterns are supported; any other pattern fails the gate closed. Team owners are not resolved |
| D-1.5a | WP1.5 accept: "dry-run `init` records planned actions and performs none" | `init` lands in WP2.5 | The test drives `init`'s action plan (labels, issue type, form, `ledger` ref) through `actions.Do` with `--dry-run` against a fake GitHub: every record is `planned`, the server sees zero requests, and the chain verifies; WP2.5 reuses the same path. The log is `.aayudha/telemetry/actions.jsonl` in a checkout (XDG state dir elsewhere), git-ignored; segments go to `.aayudha/ledger-outbox/actions/<date>/<host>-<run>.jsonl` until the ledger writer exists (WP2.4). A capability is enforced twice: `Do` checks the action's declared request before calling anything, and `actions.Guard` wraps the HTTP transport so a perform function cannot exceed it. `aayu actions verify-chain` exits 4 on a broken chain |
| D-1.6a | WP1.6: one client per provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, xAI) | Founder, 2026-10-11: models are reached through one AI gateway (repo environment `ai-gateway`: secret `AAYU_SECRET_AI_GATEWAY`, variable `AAYU_AI_GATEWAY_URL`) that speaks Anthropic Messages, OpenAI Responses, and OpenAI Chat Completions on one base URL with a Bearer key. Model IDs verified there: `claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `claude-haiku-5-5`, `claude-fable-5-1` (Anthropic, not Google), `gpt-6.1-sol`, `gpt-6-astra`, `grok-4.7`, and only `gemini-3.1-pro-low`. No JSON Schema validator is on the §2 list | `[models.gateway] base_url secret api_style styles` routes every lane through the gateway; without it each family uses its direct provider (Anthropic Messages, OpenAI Responses, Google and xAI Chat Completions; key secret named after the family). Wire format per model: a `styles` entry for the model ID (`"grok-4.7=chat"`; a list because IDs contain dots), then for its family, then `api_style`, then the family default (anthropic → messages, openai → responses, others → chat). Family is inferred from the model ID, never the endpoint, so reviewer ≠ builder holds through one gateway. The registry carries the IDs (Gemini note: `gemini-3.1-pro-low` substitutes for Gemini 3.1 Pro; Fable is an anthropic alternative, valid only when the builder is not anthropic); heavy reviews use `gpt-6-astra` at high effort; Cursor excludes the openai family, so a GPT lane on Cursor falls back to Grok and logs it; a direct call where the host default is host-managed (Amp) uses the Claude Code default. Cost: the endpoint's reported `usage.cost`, else `[[models.prices]]`, else a deliberately high fallback rate (15/75 USD per million tokens) so an unpriced model trips the breaker early. The breaker trips when spend reaches the limit (the boundary trips), writes `.aayudha/state/paused` (agents' verbs exit 6 until a human clears it; `aayu resume` is WP5.7), and pages once per scope per day. `[notify]` gains `issue`, `smtp_addr`, `from`, `smtp_secret`. Structured output validates against an in-house JSON Schema subset (`models.Schema`). Live calls: `TestGatewayLive` behind `AAYU_LIVE=1`, run by `models-live.yml` on pushes to `dev`, daily, and on dispatch with `environment: ai-gateway`, never on PRs, green-skipping while the secret is absent |
| D-1.7a | WP1.7 check list and "nonzero on floor-blocking failures" | Several checks depend on later WPs or have no GitHub API (LFS quota on personal accounts); the plan does not name the exit code or how hosts are detected | `internal/doctor` runs 20 checks as pure functions of a probe, each golden-tested on a healthy and a failing fixture (`testdata/checks.golden`). Floor-blocking (exit 3): git < 2.40 or missing, git-lfs missing, invalid config, a repository schema newer than the binary. Warnings never fail: development build or newer release, missing `gh`, no or over-scoped token, bot identity not configured (day-one path), no detected host (`AMP_THREAD_ID`, `CLAUDECODE`, `CURSOR_AGENT`/`CURSOR_TRACE_ID`, or `--host`), no writable secrets backend, missing optional tools (one row), leaked worktrees, integration secrets not set. Not yet available: skill copies vs `lock.toml` (WP3.2), MCP registrations (WP3.3), LFS quota (WP4.2), stale leases (WP5.5), provider reachability and real key scope (WP5.2). Coexistence: local workflow files and their check names, branches other than dev/rolling/main/ledger, the default branch and the `[branches]` mapping `init` will write, rulesets through the API, and collisions with `aayudha-*.yml` or `aayudha/` checks. `--fix` reports nothing to re-sync until WP3.2. `--all` checks each `[fleet]` repo through the API |
| D-1.8a | WP1.8: OTel traces to a file, OTLP opt-in | `go.opentelemetry.io/otel` v1.47 requires Go 1.26 (D5 keeps the 1.25 floor), and the upstream OTLP exporters pull in gRPC and protobuf | `go.opentelemetry.io/otel` and `otel/sdk` v1.46.0. Both exporters are in `internal/telemetry` and write OTLP JSON: one request per line in `.aayudha/telemetry/traces.jsonl`, and, only when `[telemetry] otlp_endpoint` is set, a POST to `<endpoint>/v1/traces`. The `OTEL_*` variables are ignored so nothing is sent by accident; `[telemetry] disabled = true` writes nothing. Each command run is one span with its exit code |
| D-1.9a | WP1.9 accept: v1 → v2 byte-exact diff; v1 pack passes `proof verify` on v2; v3 repo refused by a v2 binary | This binary is the first with versioned files: config is v1, and `lock.toml`, `state.json`, and proof packs (WP3.2, WP2.5, WP4.1) do not exist yet | `internal/upgrade` migrates `.aayudha/config.toml` and `config.local.toml` (`version`), `lock.toml` (`version`), and `state.json` (`schema_version`) as text, so comments survive; it never touches `ledger`. The byte-exact test runs a simulated v2 binary's migrations over a v1 fixture repo against a hand-written expected diff, then re-plans to zero changes; a v3 fixture is refused by the simulated v2 binary. In the real CLI a write verb (every verb except `doctor`, marked `ReadOnly` in the registry) on a repository newer than the binary exits 2 with the `aayu self-update` hint. `upgrade --apply` is refused for agents. Pack readers per schema version (`proof verify`), the per-story schema column in `status`, and skill-copy migration are n/a until WP4.1, WP2.8, and WP3.2 |

### Deviations recorded while implementing M3 (PR13–PR16)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-3.1a | WP3.1: frontmatter includes `paths`; lint checks "every `aayu` call resolves", hooks, and Mobbin-origin images | Claude Code reads `paths` as globs that limit when the model loads a skill on its own, so an empty list would hide a skill; the plan gives no globs per verb, and a product repo's layer globs are its own. Prose mentions of the binary ("the aayu binary") are not calls. A skill's hooks live in its frontmatter (Claude Code `hooks`). Mobbin terms forbid its pixels, not links to it | `paths` is a registry field (`Command.Paths`), rendered only when set; no verb sets it yet. `internal/skills.Lint` replaces the WP0.4 subset (D-0.4e) and runs over the embedded tree in `go test` (`TestSkillLint`, CI job `build`). Calls are read from inline code, fenced code, and `scripts/`; a call resolves when the command is in the registry, a bare first argument is a value of an enum first positional, and every `--flag` is the command's input or a global flag (`verbs.GlobalFlags`, moved from `internal/cli`). `scripts/` may hold only comments, `set` options, and `aayu …` lines without shell operators. Hooks must be `type: command` with the command exactly `aayu hook <name>` (optionally `--host <host>`). Mobbin: an image file whose name or bytes (embedded metadata) mention Mobbin, or an image embed whose URL does; plain links pass. A ninth rule rejects symlinks (V2). Each rule has a failing fixture in `internal/skills/lint_test.go`, with 200/201-character descriptions and 500/501-line bodies at the boundary |
| D-3.2a | WP3.2: `install` writes copies, `lock.toml`, `AGENTS.md`, the `CLAUDE.md` stub, MCP registrations "using the absolute binary path with version check", host hooks, and an Amp plugin stub; user-home edits are listed and confirmed separately; golden trees per host incl. Windows | Amp reads `.agents/skills/` (and `.claude/skills/`), Cursor `.agents/skills/` (and `.claude/skills/`), Claude Code `.claude/skills/`. Amp's workspace MCP servers (`.amp/settings.json`) need a per-machine `amp mcp approve`; its user settings do not. Claude Code's project servers (`.mcp.json`) need enabling. A Cursor permission hook must print valid JSON or it blocks the action; exit code 2 blocks in Claude Code, Cursor, and (by the stub's choice) Amp. An absolute path is specific to one machine | Hosts: Amp and Cursor get `.agents/skills/<verb>/`, Claude Code `.claude/skills/<verb>/`, all as real copies; a symlinked `.claude/skills` (the pre-V2 setup) or skill directory is replaced by copies. MCP: `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.mcp.json` (enabled through `enabledMcpjsonServers` in `.claude/settings.json`), and `amp.mcpServers` in `~/.config/amp/settings.json`, the one home edit; a `settings.jsonc` or a file that is not plain JSON is left alone with a note to add the entry by hand. Every registration and hook runs the symlink-resolved absolute path of the running binary, and install first runs `<path> version` and refuses (exit 5) unless it reports this binary's version. Hooks are `aayu hook session-start` (reports copies out of sync with the lock) and `aayu hook pre-tool` (blocks tool calls while the `[budget]` breaker's pause file exists; `halt` is n/a until WP5.7), registered as Claude Code `SessionStart` and `PreToolUse` (matcher `*`), Cursor `sessionStart`, `beforeShellExecution`, and `beforeMCPExecution` (JSON replies), and the Amp plugin `.amp/plugins/aayudha.ts` (`session.start`, `tool.call`). JSON files are merged: only aayudha's server entry and hook entries (recognised by their command) are replaced, keys come out sorted. `AGENTS.md` gets the `skills` and `hosts` sections through `hosts.RenderAgentsMD`, the one renderer agreed with WP2.5 (`<!-- BEGIN aayudha:<id> -->` markers; text outside them is never changed). `CLAUDE.md` is a stub importing `@AGENTS.md`, or an existing file gains that line. `lock.toml` (schema v1) records `installed_by`, `skills_version`, `pin`, the hosts (they accumulate across runs; with no `--host` the lock's hosts, else all), and per-skill SHA-256 over the skill's files with LF line endings, so one lock serves every OS. On Windows every text file is written with CRLF; a file that differs only in line endings is not rewritten, so re-apply is a no-op on either. `--host` reads the global flag (`amp`, `cursor`, `claude`, `all`). `--pin` must equal the skills version this binary carries; a lock pinned to another version is refused (exit 4) until `--pin` moves it. `--apply` is refused for agents (exit 4); home edits apply only after a terminal "y" or a human's `--yes`, and otherwise the repository edits land and install exits 4 naming the skipped home edits. `doctor` checks copies against the lock for real, and `doctor --fix` re-syncs copies and the lock (not hooks or registrations, which `install` rewrites); this is also how copies migrate to a new binary's skills, since lock schema v1 has no migrations. The absolute paths in committed host files mean each machine re-runs `install --apply`. Golden trees: `internal/hosts/testdata/golden/<host>-<unix\|windows>/`, from a two-skill fixture so they do not churn with every skill edit, byte-exact (Windows trees are CRLF, kept by `-text` in `.gitattributes`), no symlinks, re-plan empty; the CLI test installs the 30 real skills and lints the copies on disk with the WP3.1 lint |
| D-3.3a | WP3.3: tools generated from the registry; read + authoring verbs by default (`ready --check` among them); write verbs need `[mcp] expose_writes = true`; human-only verbs never exposed; long verbs report progress and honour cancellation and timeouts; prompts for `grill <type>` and `brief`; resources `aayudha://config\|status/<goal>\|ledger/<story>`; accept: "dry-run round-trip through `actions`" | Several read-exposed verbs write on some inputs (`ready` without `--check`, `status --digest` pages through `[notify]`, `proof push\|purge`, `doctor --fix`), and two verbs take a human-only flag (`tell --publish`, `decide --accept`). The registry had no way to say so. No write verb has a handler yet: `triage` (WP2.9) is the first, in the parallel M2 stack | Server on `github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk` v1.8.0 (Go 1.25 floor), stdio through the CLI's `IO` (stdout carries only frames). One tool per verb with the MCP surface, its input schema read from the generated `internal/mcp/schemas/<verb>.json`. Exposure: `read` verbs always (`grill`, `slice`, `plan`, `proof`, `ready`, `research`, `status`, `brief`, `doctor`), `write` verbs only with `expose_writes`, `never` verbs (`decide`, `ship`, `release`, `respond`, `init`, `install`) and plumbing never. Two registry input fields close the gap, rendered into the schemas as `x-aayu-read-only-values` and `x-aayu-human-only`: `ReadOnlyValues` pins an input while writes are hidden (`ready` always runs with `--check`, `status` without `--digest`, `doctor` without `--fix`, `proof` only `get\|verify\|list`), and `HumanOnly` inputs are never exposed. Each call runs the verb in-process as an agent lane (`AAYU_AGENT=1`, empty stdin), with `--json`, flags, then `--` and the positionals, so no value is read as a flag; the server's `--repo` and `--set` carry through. Tools that can write take `dry_run`, which maps to `--dry-run`. Progress: a notification every 5 s while a call runs, when the client sent a progress token; each call has a 15 min deadline; a client cancellation cancels the verb's context. Prompts: `grill` (`type`) returns the skill body plus `skills/grill/references/<type>.md` (n/a until WP5.3, which says so), `brief` (`subject`, `register`, `purpose`) the skill body and the exact `aayu brief` call. Resources: `aayudha://config` (`config show --origin --json`), templates `aayudha://status/{goal}` (`status --json`, n/a until WP2.8) and `aayudha://ledger/{story}` (`proof get --json`, n/a until WP4.2); ids must be issue references. The round-trip test drives the real `aayu mcp` over stdio pipes with the SDK client and, because no write verb has a handler yet, a stand-in `triage` that makes one label write through `actions.Do`: with `dry_run` the result is the `planned` record, the actions log has it, and the fake GitHub saw zero requests; without it, `done` and one request. `doctor`'s MCP check is real: each locked host's registration exists and its absolute path runs and reports this binary's version |
| D-3.4a | WP3.4: tier 1 per PR (artifacts, schemas, produced issue passes `ready`, every `aayu` call succeeds, injection fixtures write nothing); tier 2 nightly and on promotion with a pinned judge from another family, k = 5, pass^k ≥ 0.8 and mean ≥ 3.5, a drop > 0.3 or any pass^k below baseline fails, baseline `evals/results/<main-sha>.json`, `[budget]` cap, skipped on fork PRs; first evals on `ready` and a stub skill | The body-to-item parser and the `ready` verb are WP2.7, in the parallel M2 stack. `ready` is a deterministic verb (mode `none`), so there is no model reasoning for a rubric to judge. Live calls need the `ai-gateway` environment, which only dev, rolling, and main may use, so a `promote/…` candidate branch cannot run tier 2, and CI cannot push results files to `dev`. With n = k the unbiased pass^k estimator is 1 when all k runs pass, else 0 | `internal/evals` plus `evals/` data (`eval.toml`, `schemas/workitem.json`, cases under `cases/<skill>/<name>/`). Tier 1 runs inside `go test` (`TestTier1`, CI job `build`) over recorded runs: artifacts exist; artifacts validate against JSON Schemas (the WP1.6 validator); work items, stored as `workitems.Item` JSON until WP2.7 parses issue bodies, pass `workitems.Validate` at Ready; recorded calls replay in-process (`AAYU_AGENT=1`) against a fake GitHub serving the case's `github/` fixtures, and a call to a verb without a handler is "n/a" (rule 1a), so `ready 12 --check` turns into a real check when WP2.7 lands; an injection case fails if any recorded call is a write or never-exposed verb, if the fake GitHub receives a write, or if the actions log records a performed action. Cases: `ready/story-ready` (tier 1 only, since `ready` has no model reasoning), `stub/csv-export` (tier 1 and 2), `stub/injection` (tier 1). The stub skill lives in `evals/skills/stub/` because `skills/` holds only registry verbs. Tier 2: the skill model is the case lane's direct model (`chore` on Claude Code: `claude-haiku-5-5`); the judge is pinned in `eval.toml` (`gpt-6.1-sol`, openai) and routed as a `review`-lane override; `models.CheckFamilies` refuses a same-family judge before any call. A run whose output is not a valid work item or fails Ready scores 1 without asking the judge (rule 7); otherwise the judge's 1-5 score passes at 4. Per case pass^k is 1 when all 5 runs pass; the suite's is the mean over cases. Inputs and outputs reach models in `<untrusted>` blocks. Spend: the gateway's `[budget]` breaker (story `evals`) and `max_usd = 1.5` per run, charged at the fallback rate when the gateway reports no cost; output is capped at 2000 tokens per call. `evals.yml` runs tier 2 in `environment: ai-gateway` nightly on dev, on pushes to dev that touch skills or the harness, on every push to rolling and main, and on dispatch, never on pull requests (so never on forks), and skips green while the secret is absent. Results go to `evals/results/<sha>.json`, uploaded as the artifact `eval-results-<sha>` (90-day retention) rather than committed; the baseline is a committed `evals/results/<main-sha>.json` if present, else the artifact of main's commit (a release can commit it by PR to keep it past 90 days). Promotion: the new `ci.yml` job `evals-gate` (rolling and stable gates) needs no secret: it fetches the results for the promoted commit (the candidate merge's second parent) and for main and runs `eval -compare`; missing results fail the gate with the dispatch command, and CONTRIBUTING's rolling procedure dispatches `evals.yml` on dev first. `internal/evals/**` joins layer L05 |
| D-3.4b | WP3.4 tier 2 scores every run | The live run on 35aea36 (38183844440) scored four runs 5 and recorded the fifth as a floor miss: the model's reply was not JSON on two attempts, a harness and transport defect rather than a skill result, and pass^5 fell to 0 | Founder decision, 2026-10-12. The judge call asks for structured output (`models.Format`: Responses `text.format` / Chat `response_format`, `json_schema`, strict; a 400 from an endpoint that does not support it is retried once without it, with the schema still in the prompt). `models.Structured` validates every structured reply, retries once, extracts JSON from fences or prose, and returns a `*ReplyError` with the last reply. A run whose judge reply fails to parse or validate twice, or whose skill reply is not JSON twice, or whose call fails, has status `error`: score 0, excluded from pass^k and the mean, with a 240-character excerpt of the reply after credential redaction (`actions.RedactString`). Skill output that is JSON but fails the schema or Ready stays a `fail` scoring 1 (rule 7). pass^k and the mean are over completed runs; `Compare` fails a case with more than one errored run (`MaxErrors = 1`) |
| D-3.4c | WP3.4: rubric runs judge the skill's output; deterministic checks come first (rule 7) | Tier-2 run 38184730879 on 671d95f completed 5 of 5 runs; one scored 2 for inventing requirements the request does not state (an exact `text/csv` content type, a displayed column order). Founder decision, 2026-10-12: treat it as `evolve` would, keep the failing case and change the skill. Whether an arbitrary output invents a requirement is not decidable by rule | The stub skill must not add formats, content types, encodings, file names, orderings, limits, or numbers the request does not state, and lists what is undecided in `open_questions`; the tier-2 reply is `evals/schemas/draft.json` (the work item plus `open_questions`), and the judge sees the questions as such. The csv-export rubric says questions never lower the score and names invented requirements. Cases may carry `scope` rules (`{pattern, why}` over the work item's title and fields), a deterministic guard per known defect: tier 1 checks recorded outputs (`scope`), and tier 2 fails a matching run with score 1 before the judge. csv-export keeps rules for the two invented requirements and for format details and time limits; `TestTier1InventedRequirement` and `TestTier2ScopeGuardAndOpenQuestions` reproduce the live failure. The rubric judge remains the general check |
| D-3.4d | WP3.4: tier 2 reports pass^k and the mean | Run 38185367907 on 026fc44 errored twice with skill replies cut off mid-JSON, and the harness kept no stop reasons or token counts to say why; the charged cost fits two replies stopped at the 2000-token cap and retried. One judged run scored 2 for checking a CSV header row. Founder decision, 2026-10-12 | `models.Response` carries `StopReason` (Messages `stop_reason`, Responses `status` with `incomplete_details.reason`, Chat `finish_reason`) and `Truncated` (stopped at the cap). `models.Structured` returns a truncated reply at once as a `ReplyError` marked `Truncated`, without a retry under the same cap. Every run in the results file lists each model call: role, model, stop reason, truncated, input and output tokens, cap, and any call error. A truncated reply makes the run an `error` whose message starts `truncated:`, with the reply's start and end as the excerpt. Reaching `max_usd` records the remaining runs as errors and still writes the results file. Follow-up (founder decision, 2026-10-12, after run 38185993917 measured complete drafts at 1700-2430 output tokens and showed `input_tokens` 2 on every Anthropic call): the skill cap is 4000 output tokens and `max_usd` is 3.0 (five 4000-token drafts with their judgements cost about 1.85 USD at the fallback rate); the skill model is the registry lane of the verb the skill is or stands in for (`[tier2] verb`; the stub stands in for `grill`, lane `spec`: `claude-opus-5-5` today), never a lane or model named in the case, and when that model's call fails at the endpoint the lane's registry alternative (`ModelDefault.AlternativeID`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`) takes it; the judge stays pinned and must differ in family from both; Anthropic `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` count as input tokens (at the full input rate) for cost and the `[budget]` breaker. Rubric and stub skill: conventions of the format the request names (a CSV header row) are not invented requirements; exact column order, content type, encoding, file name, size limits, and durations are. csv-export gains a size-limit scope rule, and `TestScopeConventionVersusInvented` keeps both sides of the line: a header row passes, each invented specific fails |
| D-3.4e | WP3.4: "pass^k ≥ 0.8 and mean rubric score ≥ 3.5 to pass; a drop of more than 0.3 mean or any pass^k fall below baseline fails the promotion" | The harness applied pass^k ≥ 0.8 to every case as well as the suite. One case's pass^5 is 0 or 1, so a single off run in five failed the promotion gate: the rolling candidates 662cd99 and 349d52c each failed on a different skills case (plan 5 2 5 5 5, then slice at mean 4.20) while the suite stood at pass^5 0.80 and mean 4.80 | pass^k is enforced at the suite (group) level, as the plan states it; per case it is printed as an advisory. Still enforced per case: the mean bar, the error limit, and any fall below the case's baseline pass^k. Each WP's own acceptance (WP5.3, 5.4, 7.3) still quotes its per-case numbers. TestCompareSuitePassK |

### Deviations recorded while implementing M2 (PR7–PR12)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-2.1a | WP2.1: "Ten types", with fields per stage, parents, and links | The plan names the count and, across WPs, Goal, Initiative, Feature, Story, Bug, Decision, Feedback, Incident, Release, and the Experiment that `experiment`/`readout` work on; "Result" is something written onto a Goal, Initiative, Feature, or Experiment, not a type. It fixes no field lists or link kinds | The ten types are Goal, Initiative, Feature, Story, Bug, Decision, Experiment, Release, Feedback, Incident (`internal/workitems/schema.go`, schema v1; `grill`'s `type` input is now this enum). Result is a Complete-stage field. Parents: Initiative → Goal, Feature → Initiative, Story → Feature (required at Ready); Experiment → Feature or Initiative (required at Ready); Bug → Feature or Story, Decision → any delivery type or Incident, Release → Goal (optional); Feedback and Incident are intake with no parent. Links are typed lines `<kind> #N` in a Links field: `supersedes`, `constrains`, `depends-on`, `caused-by`, `becomes`, `includes`, `waives`. A Completion Goal criterion is one line `N. [evidence_kind] state: …; expected: …`; Measure, Threshold, Scenario, and Environment are separate Ready fields of the Completion Goal. A criterion without a valid evidence kind is unmapped. Effort is required at Ready for Story and Bug; `effort:XL` blocks Ready for every type |
| D-2.1b | WP2.1: Priority/Effort/Risk/Health/Horizon "as labels on personal repos, GitHub issue fields where available" | GitHub issue fields are organization-level and in preview, with no support in go-github v90 (the newest release whose `go` directive fits the 1.25 floor, D5) | Every repository uses labels in 0.1 (`priority:P0–P3`, `effort:XS–XL`, `risk:low/medium/high`, `health:on-track/at-risk/off-track`, `horizon:now/next/later`; values proposed here), with one label per prefix enforced by validation. The label is the only home, so these are not form fields. Delivery Status is `status:<value>`. Moving a field to issue fields later is a schema-version migration (WP1.9) |
| D-2.2a | WP2.2: "forms generated from schema"; accept "`init --dry-run` lists labels/types" | `init` itself is WP2.5. GitHub rejects a form whose `type:` names an issue type the owner does not have, and personal accounts have none | `go generate` writes one form per type to `work-items/forms/aayudha-<type>.yml` (the `aayudha-` prefix keeps them apart from a product's own templates); `init` installs them under `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` and adds `type:` only on organizations. Forms carry the Create fields (required) and, for non-intake types, the Ready fields (optional until `ready`); intake forms add the `intake` label. The WP2.2 binary implements only `init --dry-run`: it plans the label and issue-type creations through `actions` (all `planned`, zero writes) and lists them; without `--dry-run` it exits 2 until WP2.5. Forms are checked against GitHub's form rules in `TestFormsPassGitHubFormSchema` and were validated once against the SchemaStore `github-issue-forms` JSON Schema |
| D-2.3a | WP2.3: go-github and githubv4, ETag cache, primary and secondary rate limits with serialized bulk writes, pagination, call budget per verb | go-github v91+ requires Go 1.26 (D5). Creating and deleting a repository needs rights the founder's day-to-day fine-grained token lacks. The plan does not say how "every external write goes through `internal/actions`" is enforced for API calls | `github.com/google/go-github/v90` (BSD-3-Clause) and `github.com/shurcooL/githubv4` (MIT, GraphQL for Projects v2 only), with `go-querystring` and `shurcooL/graphql` (`golang.org/x/oauth2`, used only by githubv4's own tests, is held at v0.36.0 because v0.37 requires Go 1.26). One transport stack in `internal/github`: a mutating request without the context mark that only `Client.Write` (a wrapper over `actions.Do`) sets fails with `ErrUnlogged` before it leaves the process, so a write outside `actions` is impossible rather than merely discouraged; the action's capability is checked per request; writes are serialized with a 1 s gap; 403/429 responses are retried up to 3 times after `Retry-After`, the primary reset, or one minute for an unlabelled secondary limit, and fail at once when the wait exceeds 2 minutes; a per-run in-memory ETag cache revalidates repeated GETs (304s are counted as `not_modified`); a call budget (default 2000 per run, including 304s) stops a run with `ErrBudget`; `Stats` is what verbs report in `--json`. GitHub and network errors map to exit 5. Live tests create `aayu-scratch-*` repositories with `AAYU_LIVE_ADMIN_TOKEN` when set (the founder's admin token) and delete them; `TestLive` with `AAYU_RECORD=1` rewrites the recording that `TestRecordedContract` replays request by request |
| D-2.4a | WP2.4: temp worktree, fetch-rebase-retry with bounded backoff, per-repo file lock, content-addressed paths | GitHub reports a lost race between two pushes to the same ref as `[remote rejected] (failed)`, not as a non-fast-forward. A ledger commit is a set of whole-file writes and appends, so "rebase" can re-apply them to the new tip instead of replaying a patch. `flock` is not portable to Windows | `internal/ledger.Writer` fetches the tip into a private ref, builds the commit in a temporary detached worktree, and pushes it without force; on a rejected or lost-race push it waits (100 ms·2ⁿ with jitter, capped at 5 s, 10 attempts), resets the worktree to the new tip, re-applies the same files, and pushes again. A commit that changes nothing is not pushed. The lock is an `O_EXCL` file in the clone's git common dir, taken over after 10 minutes (a dead holder). `ContentPath` gives content-addressed paths. A token travels as an `http.extraHeader` in `GIT_CONFIG_*` environment variables, never on a command line; a human-fallback write carries an `Aayudha-Identity: identity:human-fallback` trailer. Each write is a `ledger.push` action, except checkpoint segment pushes (`ledger.Sink`, now the actions log's segment sink in a checkout, falling back to the local outbox when the remote has no ledger), which carry the log itself. Accept: 5 writers in separate clones plus 5 sharing one clone all land against a local bare remote (11 commits, every writer's commit an ancestor of the tip, shared appends intact), and 5 concurrent writers land on a live scratch repository (`TestLiveConcurrentWriters`) |
| D-2.5a | WP2.5: init writes labels, issue types, forms, `.aayudha/config.toml`, `AGENTS.md`, the `ledger` branch, and `.aayudha/state.json` | The repository files cannot be pushed to the default branch: on a governed repository it takes pull requests only, and forms and config are protected paths. The plan names no ledger ruleset for init, though WP0.1 expects `ledger` to be protected and "adds its rulesets alongside existing ones" implies one. A run needs a stable scope for idempotency keys that a later uninit/init cycle does not reuse | GitHub objects go through the API (`internal/initrepo`, every write an action): labels, issue types (organizations only), the orphan `ledger` (a tree, a parentless commit, and the ref; on a private repository with `.gitattributes` LFS lines for media extensions and a `.lfsconfig`; on a public one with neither), and ruleset `aayudha-ledger` (deletion, non-fast-forward, and update restricted; bypass: repository admin role, D1) unless an active ruleset already protects `refs/heads/ledger` with deletion and non-fast-forward rules. Repository files are written into the local checkout, and init prints that they must be committed through a pull request: `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/aayudha-<type>.yml`, `.aayudha/config.toml` (only when absent; public repositories get `[proof] media = "" # required: private bucket`; a repository without `dev` gets `[branches]` mappings, a new config section), `.aayudha/.gitignore` for runtime state, the `work-items` section of `AGENTS.md` through the shared `internal/hosts` renderer, and `state.json`. Objects that exist already are adopted (recorded with `adopted: true`) and never changed; a file that exists with other content is kept and reported. State records each object's GitHub ID (labels, types, rulesets) or SHA (ledger) after every write, the completed steps, and a run ID that scopes idempotency keys. An incomplete state refuses a plain rerun (exit 2) and `--resume` continues; a complete state reruns as a no-op. `init` and `uninit` need a yes on a terminal or a human's `--yes`. `uninit` deletes, by stored ID, what init created and nothing it adopted; it keeps a ledger that has been written to since init and files edited since init, removes init's `AGENTS.md` section, and deletes `state.json` when nothing is left. `proof push --media` (WP4.2) exists only as the V3 refusal: exit 4 while `[proof] media` is empty on a public repository. `doctor` gains `ledger-media`: a public repository whose ledger has LFS configuration is a floor-blocking failure |
| D-2.6a | WP2.6: `--projects` (Portfolio/Delivery/Ops views), `--gates` (broker and Ready guard), `--identity` (GitHub App), `--integrations` (bucket, PostHog, Sentry, GrowthBook), each with a cost/scope summary and `y/N` | The Projects v2 API cannot create views, so three views of one project are not possible from code. Creating a GitHub App needs the human's browser (the manifest flow). A gate workflow needs the aayudha binary, and there is no published release yet | `--projects` creates three Projects v2 (`<repo> Portfolio`, `<repo> Delivery`, `<repo> Ops`) owned by the repository owner and linked to the repository (GraphQL `createProjectV2`; needs the `project` scope), adopting any with the same title; they hold views only. `--gates` writes exactly `aayudha-token.yml` (the WP1.4 broker) and `aayudha-ready-guard.yml` (golden-tested); the guard installs the binary with `go install …/cmd/aayudha@<release tag>` (a development build installs `@dev`), runs `aayudha ready <n> --guard --ci` with the workflow's own `issues: write` token, and never writes the ledger. `--identity` serves a local page that posts the App manifest (contents, issues, pull requests write; actions, checks read; no webhook) to GitHub, takes the code GitHub redirects back with, converts it, seals the private key straight into the repository secret `AAYUDHA_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` (libsodium sealed box, `golang.org/x/crypto/nacl/box`), sets the variable `AAYUDHA_APP_CLIENT_ID`, adds `[identity] app_id app_slug broker` to config, makes the App the ledger ruleset's only bypass actor in place of the admin role (D1), and prints the App's installation URL; the key never touches disk or the actions log. `--integrations` adds `[integrations.<name>] secret scope = "read"` for PostHog, Sentry, and GrowthBook (values are set with `aayu auth set-secret`) and, on a terminal, `[proof] media` for an `s3://` or `r2://` bucket. Each flag first looks for what it would create and asks only when something is missing, so reruns write nothing. The App, projects, and integrations are recorded as external objects: `uninit` keeps them and `uninit --external` lists them; `uninit` deletes the secret and variable it set |
| D-2.7a | WP2.7: body → typed struct by heading; `ready` posts a replace-in-place snapshot (body hash + Completion Goal hash), writes `specs/<id>/ready.md` under the bot or the tagged human identity; a guard workflow strips `status:ready`; accept: "`audit` fixture lists" the human-fallback snapshot | `audit` is WP7.6. The plan does not fix the hash inputs, the snapshot's form, or the guard's identity; D1 rules out the workflow token for ledger writes | `workitems.Parse` maps level-2 and level-3 heading sections (the issue-form layout; headings inside code fences are ignored) to fields by label or field ID; the type is the GitHub issue type or the `type:<type>` label (they must agree, R1); a section that is not a field of the type is a problem. Feedback and Incident are intake and never Ready. Body hash: SHA-256 of the body with CRLF→LF, trailing spaces trimmed per line, and the whole trimmed. Completion Goal hash: SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the parsed criteria with Measure, Threshold, Scenario, and Environment. Technical Owner: the body's value, else the Code Architect seat's paired human, else the literal "the Code Architect twin". `ready` resolves the parent (sub-issue API) and the types of linked issues, validates, and on a pass writes the ledger file (snapshot plus the body at Ready), then the `<!-- aayudha:ready-snapshot -->` comment, then the labels (`status:ready` replaces any other `status:*`, plus `identity:human-fallback` on the day-one path); a failing item writes nothing and exits 4, as does `--check`. A rerun on an unchanged item writes nothing. The guard (`ready --guard --ci`, run by `aayudha-ready-guard.yml` with that workflow's `issues: write` token, stamped `ci` in the actions log) removes `status:ready` and explains in a `<!-- aayudha:ready-guard -->` comment when the item no longer validates, has no snapshot, or its body hash differs from the snapshot's; it never writes the ledger. Until `audit` exists, the actions log is what it will reconcile: every human-fallback write is a record with `"identity":"human-fallback"` (the test greps it with `aayu actions grep`) |
| D-2.8a | WP2.8: rollups with proof coverage, streak, blocked/over-budget, shipped-but-unmeasured, decisions near SLA, provisional decisions, cost; outputs table, `--json`, Goal comment, `--digest`, MCP resource; `status --all`; parent-close rule | "Near SLA", "over budget", and the cost source are not defined; `status` is a read verb exposed over MCP, yet the Goal comment and the parent-close rule write. Measure is WP7.2 | Rollups count every work item under a Goal, Initiative, or Feature at any depth (sub-issue API, every page): by Delivery Status, health, and risk; blocked (`status:blocked`); over budget (the item's `cost_usd` in the local actions log above `[budget] per_story_usd`); shipped but unmeasured (`status:released`); open Decisions at or past 75 % of `[decisions] sla_hours` in business hours of `[time]`, with the deadline and whether it is overdue; `decision:provisional` Decisions, with their ratify-or-revert hours "n/a until WP5.6"; cost summed from the actions log. Proof coverage reads "n/a until M4" and streak "n/a until M5". Without arguments `status` rolls up every open Goal (`type:goal`). It writes nothing unless `--apply` (new, pinned to false for MCP), which posts the replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:status -->` Goal comment and closes a Feature whose children are all released, measured, done, or cancelled and that has a Result; Initiatives and Goals close on `measure` (n/a until WP7.2). `--digest` pages the founder digest through the WP1.6 `[notify]` sender, deduplicated per repository per day; the MCP resource `aayudha://status/<n>` (WP3.3) reads `status --json`. `status --all` prints one row per `[fleet]` repository with its first open Goal's rollup |
| D-2.9a | WP2.9: Incident → linked Bug; label-only enforced in `internal/actions`; the triage workflow is the only one that mints an `issues: write`-only token | Creating the linked Bug is an issue write, which the labels-only capability forbids by design. The plan does not say how intake issues are recognised or how the binary decides when no host model did | Intake issues carry the `intake` label (the Feedback and Incident forms set it); triage refuses any other issue (exit 4, no writes). The class comes from `--as feedback\|bug\|incident` when the host's model chose it, else from the chore lane's model through the WP1.6 gateway, with the title and body in one `<untrusted source="issue">` block (a closing tag inside is escaped) and the answer validated against a closed JSON Schema (`class`, `accepted`, `reason`; anything else is rejected and asked again). Triage writes labels only, each write carrying the `labels-only` capability that the GitHub client checks per request: it adds `type:<class>`, `needs:grill` for accepted Feedback (grill creating the Feature is n/a until WP5.3), and the new label `needs:bug` for an Incident (`respond` files the linked Bug, WP5.6), and removes `intake` and any other `type:` label. `init --gates` now installs a third workflow, `aayudha-triage.yml` (golden): on `issues: opened/labeled` with `intake`, it mints a bot App token with `permission-issues: write` only when the App exists, otherwise uses the workflow token limited to `issues: write` by `permissions`, and runs `aayudha triage <n> --ci` |

### Deviations recorded while implementing M4 (PR17–PR22)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-4.1a | WP4.1: `pack.json` v1 with story, `pr_head_sha`, checkpoint, seat, host, model, identity, `completion_goal_hash`, criteria, artifacts `{path, kind, sha256, size, lfs\|bucket}`, checks `{floor, result, value, threshold, tool, version, waiver?}`, cost, links; schema versioned | The plan names no authority field, no place for media that were not pushed, and no tombstone, and `proof verify` has to know what "head" and "criteria" to compare against | `internal/proof` (schema `internal/proof/schema/pack.v1.json`, validated with the WP1.6 JSON Schema subset; `Decode` dispatches on `schema_version` so every shipped version keeps a reader, WP1.9). Added fields: `repo`, `pr`, `base_sha`, `footprint`, `authority {kind advisory\|authoritative, workflow, run_id, writer}`, `created_at`; artifact `store` is `git` (data on the ledger), `lfs`, `bucket` (with `object`), or `none` (media not pushed: hash only), plus an optional `tombstone {purged_at, reason}`; check `result` is `pass\|fail\|skipped\|waived\|blocked` with `detail` and `network`. `proof verify <story>` checks the best pack (newest authoritative, else newest advisory) against the PR's current head (API), and the Completion Goal hash and criterion numbers of the Ready snapshot on the ledger (`specs/<n>/ready.md`, parsed by `ready.ParseSnapshot`): every stored artifact's SHA-256 and size, every Ready criterion mapped to at least one artifact, every evidence reference resolvable, `pr_head_sha` = head. Floor results are reported (`floors_hold`) but are not integrity problems. Exit 4 on any problem |
| D-4.2a | WP4.2: LFS by extension on private repos; `[proof] media` bucket with lifecycle rules; retention TTL; `proof push\|get\|verify\|list\|purge`; one replace-in-place comment on PR and issue; agent packs advisory, CI packs authoritative; accept: live scratch test, public fixture refuses LFS media, purge removes bucket objects and tombstones `pack.json` | No S3 SDK is on the §2 list. GitHub keeps LFS objects until the repository is deleted, so an LFS "purge" can only remove the file from the ledger tip. GitHub publishes no LFS quota API for personal accounts. There is no bucket for this repository yet | Ledger layout `proof/<story>/<head sha>/<advisory\|authoritative>/{pack.json, artifacts/…}`; `verify` leaves the local pack in `.aayudha/proof/<story>/` (git-ignored). Media are artifacts of kind screenshot, recording, or sr-transcript, or with a media extension (`proof.MediaExts`, also init's LFS list). `proof push` commits data artifacts; media only with `--media`: to the bucket when `[proof] media` is set, else to Git LFS on a private repository (`ledger.Writer.LFS` runs the LFS filters and `git lfs push` before the commit), else refused with exit 4 before anything is written (V3). The bucket is any S3-compatible store: `s3://bucket[/prefix][?region=…&endpoint=…]` or `r2://<account>/bucket[/prefix]`, path-style SigV4 (`internal/proof/sigv4`, checked against AWS's published "GET Bucket Lifecycle" example), keys `<prefix>/proof/<owner>/<repo>/<story>/<sha256>`, credentials in the secret named by the new `[proof] media_secret` (default `proof-media`, value `ACCESS_KEY_ID:SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`). Retention is a per-repository bucket lifecycle rule `aayudha-proof-retention/<owner>/<repo>` (expiry = `retention_days`), merged into the bucket's existing rules. `proof purge <story>` purges that story's media, `proof purge` media older than `retention_days`: bucket objects are deleted, LFS files removed from the ledger tip, and each `pack.json` rewritten with tombstones (hashes kept). Packs are authoritative only when pushed with `--ci` from `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` (`GITHUB_WORKFLOW_REF`), otherwise advisory; CI writes the ledger only with `AAYU_BOT_TOKEN` (D1). `proof push` posts `<!-- aayudha:proof -->` on the issue and the PR. Tests: a fake S3 (`internal/proof/fakes3`, recomputes every signature) for push, lifecycle, verify, tamper, and purge; a local bare remote with git-lfs for the private path; the CLI end to end. Live: `TestLiveLFSScratch` (private `aayu-scratch-*`: the screenshot lands in GitHub LFS, verifies by download, purge removes it) and `TestLiveBucket` (behind `AAYU_LIVE=1` plus `AAYU_LIVE_BUCKET`/`AAYU_LIVE_BUCKET_KEYS`; run against a local SeaweedFS S3 store, since no bucket exists yet: a founder action). `doctor lfs-quota` now says where media go and, without a bucket, where to read LFS usage, since there is no API (D-1.7a) |
| D-4.3a | WP4.3: floor interface, scrubbed child env, no-network default, results into the pack; diff coverage from lcov, Cobertura, and Go coverprofile; threshold from config (90 %); accept: 89.9 % fails, 90.0 % passes, a child cannot read `AAYU_*` or `GITHUB_TOKEN` | The plan does not say how a floor's tools are isolated, which test command produces coverage, which lines are "the diff", or how a pack's criteria get evidence that CI actually observed. Unprivileged network namespaces are Linux-only and Ubuntu's AppArmor can forbid them | `internal/verify`: `Floor{Name, Applies, Run}`, `Execute` (a floor whose tool is missing or errors is `blocked`, never a pass; one that does not apply is `skipped` with its reason), `Pack`. Children get only an allow-listed environment (PATH, locale, temp, Go and Node cache locations, never `AAYU_*`, `GH_*`, `GITHUB_*`, or anything named token/secret/password) and a throwaway HOME; the tool is resolved on the child's PATH. No network by default: on Linux the binary re-executes itself in a new user and network namespace with only loopback up (CAP_NET_ADMIN is dropped before the tool runs); where namespaces are unavailable the proxies point at a closed port and `GOPROXY=off` (`network: proxy-blocked` in the check). Floors that need the network say so (semgrep's rule registry, the OSV database). Coverage: `[verify.floors.coverage] command` (argv) and `report` (new keys), else in a Go module `go test -json -covermode=set -coverpkg=./... -coverprofile ./...` (since WP4.8: code exercised by another package's tests counts, as the CLI's tests exercise most packages); the changed lines are the new-side lines of `git diff -U0 <merge-base>..<head>`; lines the report does not list are not executable; the comparison is exact in integers, so 899/1000 fails 90.0 and 900/1000 passes; a failing test command fails the floor. Criteria evidence: `.aayudha/evidence/<story>.toml` in the PR maps each criterion to tests (`[[criterion]] id, tests`), and verify turns the `go test -json` events of tests that passed in this run into `evidence/criterion-<n>.jsonl` (kind from the criterion); a test that did not run or failed leaves the criterion unmapped. `aayu verify [pr] [--floor f]`: story from `Refs #n` in the PR body, an `Aayudha-Story` trailer, or a `story/<n>-…` branch; the Ready snapshot from the ledger; refuses (exit 4) a checkout that is not the PR head; writes `.aayudha/proof/<story>/`; exit 3 on a floor miss |

The CI `security` job already installs the pinned tools | `sast`: registry packs per language tracked in git (`p/golang`, `p/javascript`, `p/typescript`, `p/python`, …, plus `p/github-actions`), CRITICAL → critical, ERROR/HIGH → high, WARNING/MEDIUM → medium. `secrets`: `gitleaks git --redact` over the PR's commits (`base..head`); any finding above `max_findings` (0) fails. `deps`: `osv-scanner scan source -r`, one finding per advisory group by CVSS (≥ 9 critical, ≥ 7 high), else the database severity, and an unscored group counts as high (rule 7 errs closed). Reports are artifacts (`reports/*.json`, kind report). Tests: stand-in tools with recorded reports in `go test`; the real pinned tools in the CI `security` job (`TestFloorsWithRealTools`: a tainted SQL string, a GitHub token, and `golang.org/x/text v0.3.0` each fail their floor; a clean module passes), with every planted fixture generated at run time so this repository's own scans never see it |

A group whose every advisory the call analysis marks not called, or unimportant, is reported ("N more not called") but not counted. `TestTrackedLockfiles` covers a worktree inside an ignored directory; `TestParseOSVCallAnalysis` both sides of the call analysis; the real-tools planted fixture still fails (`golang.org/x/text v0.3.0`) |
| D-4.5a | WP4.5: Playwright drives every Completion Goal state; axe zero serious/critical at WCAG 2.2 AA; keyboard-only; reduced motion; 200 % text; 44×44 targets; Lighthouse CI budgets from config; `[verify] seed`; refuse when `AAYU_ENV` resolves to production; PII redaction (DOM masking + OCR/regex) that blocks the pack | A criterion's state is free text, so the floor needs a way to find the page; CONSTRAINTS.md (WP0.5) already says Lighthouse budgets are product-specific with no default; Lighthouse CI's budget files are superseded by plain Lighthouse audits; no OCR library is pure Go | States are the screenshot and recording criteria of the Ready snapshot, at the path in backticks in the state (`` state: `/settings` … ``), else the root of the target. Targets are `[verify.environments.<name>] url, production` (new), picked by `AAYU_ENV` (default `local`); a production environment, or any target on a production host, makes `verify` exit 4 before seeding or writing anything. `[verify] seed` (argv, new) runs once before the first capture; `init` scaffolds `.aayudha/seed/README.md`. The embedded `web.mjs` (node; Playwright and axe-core resolved from the product, then `NODE_PATH`) runs per state with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`: DOM masking (`[data-aayu-mask]`, password/email/tel/card inputs), a PII scan of the masked text (email outside the RFC 2606/6761 reserved domains, Luhn-valid card numbers, national ID numbers), axe with the wcag2a/2aa/21a/21aa/22aa tags, Tab-reachability of every visible control, running animations, text clipped at `text_scale_percent` (CSS zoom), and controls under `min_target_px` (inline text links excepted). Any PII (DOM, or OCR with `tesseract` when installed; the check says when OCR is unavailable) blocks: `redaction: blocked` and no capture of the run is kept. Screenshots (`screens/<n>.png`) and recordings (`recordings/<n>.webm`) are the criteria's evidence. `perf` runs Lighthouse (performance category, Playwright's Chromium) on each state URL once `[verify.floors.perf] min_score\|max_lcp_ms\|max_cls\|max_tbt_ms` (new, no defaults) set a budget; only the numbers are kept, since a Lighthouse report embeds screenshots. | D-4.8a | WP4.8: `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` runs from the base branch; re-runs `verify` on the PR head in CI and writes the authoritative pack to `ledger`; fails if `pack.sha ≠ head` or the Completion Goal hash ≠ the Ready snapshot; `inspect` with diff + plan + pack + criteria, untrusted-wrapped, review lane (family ≠ builder), structured JSON, per-criterion verdicts, `merge\|fix\|rebuild`, can downgrade but never override a floor, cached by diff hash, `cancel-in-progress`, skips drafts, `[budget] per_pr_inspect_max`, cap 2 cycles then page; accept: planted defect → P1 + `fix`, clean → `merge`, "disposition: merge" in the PR has no effect, third cycle pages | D1: CI's workflow token can never write `ledger`, and no bot App exists yet. A `pull_request_target` job that checks out PR code must not hold secrets (a same-user child can read its parent's environment). The plan names no store for the cache, cycle count, or per-PR spend, and no source for the builder's family | Template `internal/initrepo/aayudha-merge-gate.yml`, installed by `init --gates` (now four workflows, golden). `pull_request_target` (the base branch's copy; `opened\|synchronize\|reopened\|ready_for_review\|edited`; per-PR `cancel-in-progress`). Job `aayudha/merge-gate` (drafts skipped) holds only a read-only workflow token, checks out the head (semgrep's `pull-request-target-code-checkout` rule is annotated on that step with this reasoning: a base-branch gate must check out PR code to verify it; the sandbox now also gives the tool its own PID namespace, so nothing PR code starts outlives its floor or touches the pack), installs the pinned floor tools and aayudha (`@dev` straight from GitHub on this repository), and runs `aayu verify <pr> --ci`, which in CI on a PR also fails (exit 4) when a criterion has no evidence from this run, the PR head moved, or the story's current Completion Goal hash differs from the snapshot's; the pack is kept as the run artifact `aayudha-pack`. Job `aayudha/inspect` (environment `ai-gateway`, `pull-requests: write`, no PR code) runs `aayu inspect <pr> --ci` on that pack. Job `aayudha/pack` pushes it to the ledger as authoritative with the App token when `AAYUDHA_APP_CLIENT_ID` is set; without an App it leaves a notice, and `ship` (WP4.10) lands the run's artifact under the human identity after checking the run (D-4.10a). `inspect`: the reviewer is the review lane's direct model (`gpt-6.1-sol`); the builder's family comes from the pack's `model` (an `Aayudha-Model` trailer, read by `verify`), else the build lane of the pack's host; `CheckFamilies` refuses a same-family pair. The prompt holds the floors as facts and the Ready criteria, story, evidence excerpts (text artifacts only; media never reach the model), PR text, and diff (capped at 120 kB) as `<untrusted>` blocks; the reply is validated against a closed schema (`disposition`, `summary`, `findings[{severity P0-P3, file, line, title, detail, criterion}]`, `criteria[{id, verdict met\|unmet\|unclear, evidence}]`). Rule 7 in code: a floor that does not hold, a P0/P1 finding, or an unmet criterion turns `merge` into `fix` (recorded as `overridden`). The replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:inspect -->` PR comment carries the state (input hash over diff + pack head + checks + criteria, cycles, spend): the same inputs are answered from it without a model call; spend at `per_pr_inspect_max` exits 6 and pages; after two `fix`/`rebuild` cycles the next run pages and exits 4 without calling the model. Exit 0 on `merge` (and drafts), 4 on `fix`/`rebuild`. Re-entry: `fix` names the fix-in-PR path (`build --resume` n/a until WP5.5), `rebuild` sends the story back to Ready review. Live acceptance: `TestInspectLive` in the dispatch-only `inspect-live.yml` on dev; scripted-model tests (`TestInspectDispositions`, `TestInspectMergeBudgetDraft`) cover the plumbing, rule 7, the cache, budget, drafts, and the paging third cycle |

Accepting a waiver by its `[decisions] authority` is n/a until WP5.6. `ship` refusing a non-holding pack (exit 4) and proceeding on a waived one is tested with `ship` in PR22 |
| D-4.10a | WP4.10: `grill story` as a markdown-only skill writing the issue via `actions`; `build` minimal (refuse unless Ready and body hash = snapshot; worktree from `origin/dev`; `Aayudha-Checkpoint` commits; open PR); `ship` minimal (green gate, one approving human review, squash-merge, Complete section, cleanup); accept: tier-1 eval for `grill story` yields an issue passing `ready`, `build` on a non-Ready issue exits 4, `ship` with only a bot review exits 4 | A skill cannot write GitHub itself, so the binary needs inputs for the draft. GitHub refuses self-approval (D2), there is one human and no bot App, so no PR here can get an approving review from someone other than its author. No bot App also means CI cannot write the authoritative pack (D1). WP2.8's proof coverage was n/a until M4 | `grill` gains `--title`, `--body`, `--labels` (label-held fields only), and `--create` (pinned false over MCP): it validates the draft at Create and Ready (the parent from `--parent`) and, only with `--create` and a pass, creates the issue (`type:story`, `status:planned`, the labels, `identity:human-fallback` on day one) and links it under the parent; other types are n/a until WP5.3. `skills/grill/SKILL.md` and `references/story.md` hold the interview; tier-1 case `evals/cases/grill/story-empty-claims`. `build <n>`: refuses (exit 4) unless the story passes Ready, carries `status:ready`, and its body hash equals the snapshot comment's; the first run cuts `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>` on `story/<n>-<slug>` from `origin/dev`; each later run commits the agent's changes (`feat: <title> (#n, checkpoint k/N)`, DCO sign-off, trailers `Aayudha-Story`, `Aayudha-Checkpoint` with N = the larger of k and the change's layer count, `Aayudha-Model` from `AAYU_MODEL` or `[models.build.<host>]`), pushes, and opens the PR (`Refs #n`); no lease, budget breaker, per-checkpoint verify, or `--resume` (WP5.5). `ship <pr>`: the head's `aayudha/merge-gate` and `aayudha/inspect` check runs are green; the authoritative pack for the head is on the ledger, or ship lands it from the newest successful base-branch `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` run's `aayudha-pack` artifact under the human identity, recording the run (`authority.run_id`, `writer`); the pack verifies against the Ready snapshot and the head, and its floors hold (a skipped mobile floor blocks unless waived, WP4.9); then the human pass: the latest decisive review per reviewer must include an approval of the head by a human who is not the author (bot accounts and `[identity] bot_logins` never count). `--solo-bypass` (human-only, refused for agents) is the closest enforceable pass while D2 holds: only with no bot identity and no second paired human, and only after the merging human's own PR comment starting `Solo-mode bypass:`; it is written to the ship record, the Complete section, and the actions log for `audit` (WP7.6) to reconcile. Then squash-merge at the head SHA, `status:merged` (labels first, so the Ready guard ignores the body edit), the Outcome section (Complete), `proof/<story>/<head>/authoritative/ship.json` on the ledger, the branch and worktree removed, and the actions segment checkpointed to the ledger. The merge gate passes a PR that serves no story (no `Refs #n`, trailer, or `story/<n>-` branch) without running a floor, since it has nothing to prove. `status` in a checkout now reports proof coverage: done stories and bugs (merged, released, measured, done) whose authoritative pack maps every Ready criterion, matches its hashes, and holds its floors, over all of them |

A `ship` that refused after landing the gate's pack never checkpointed. | D-M4.5a | M4.5: one real story end to end, `grill story` → `ready` → `build` → `verify` (coverage + SAST floors) → `inspect` → approving human review → `ship`; accept: the story reaches `merged` with an authoritative pack, Complete section filled, actions segment on `ledger`; `status` shows proof coverage 1/1 | One human and no bot App: every agent write runs under the founder's account (`identity:human-fallback`), so the founder is the author of the story's PR and GitHub refuses self-approval (D2): no approving review by anyone other than the author is possible. CI cannot write the ledger without an App (D1). Two defects surfaced on the way: the merge gate failed closed reading `aayu halt` (WP5.7 landed while the gate was in review; fixed in #93), and the `deps` floor passed on nothing in a build worktree and counted Go advisories osv-scanner's call analysis marks not called (D-4.4b, #95) | Story #91 ("doctor names the floor each missing tool serves", under Feature #37): drafted by the agent as `grill story`'s host model from the founder's task (not a live interview), validated and created with `aayu grill story --create`, made Ready with `aayu ready 91` (snapshot `specs/91/ready.md`). `aayu build 91` cut the worktree, then committed checkpoint 1/1 (with `.aayudha/evidence/91.toml` mapping both criteria to `TestOptionalToolsNamesFloors`/`TestChecksGolden`) and opened PR #92. `aayu verify 92` locally wrote an advisory pack (`proof push`). The base-branch merge gate (run 38197859955, head 44d18af) passed: coverage 9/9 changed executable lines in an isolated network namespace, sast, secrets, deps pass, both criteria mapped; `aayudha/inspect`: `merge`, criteria 1 and 2 met, `gpt-6.1-sol`, 0.11 USD; `aayudha/pack` left the pack as the run artifact (no App). `aayu ship 92` landed that artifact as the authoritative pack under the human identity (`authority.run_id` 38197859955, `writer` "rahulnakmol (aayu ship)") and then refused, exit 4: no human pass. The founder's PR comment "Solo-mode bypass: …" and `aayu ship 92 --solo-bypass` merged it (91058d6) with `human_pass.kind = solo-bypass` in `proof/91/44d18af…/authoritative/ship.json`, the Outcome section, and the actions log, for `audit` (WP7.6) to reconcile; no review was faked. The ship's segment push failed on this orb's git credential helper (D-4.10b), so the surviving lines of the build, pack-landing, and ship runs were appended by hand, unchanged, as `actions/2026-10-12/amp-<run>.jsonl` (ledger 0de0996); the lines of the `grill`, `ready`, `verify`, and `proof push` runs lived in worktree-local logs that ship and cleanup removed, and GitHub's event history and the ledger commits are their record (rule 6). `aayu status` in a checkout: Goal #29, Initiative #30, and Feature #37 report proof coverage 1/1 |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP5.2 and WP6.4 (read-only adapters, ahead of M5 and M6)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-5.2a | WP5.2: PostHog, Sentry, and GitHub read adapters return `{metric, population, window, value, source_query, fetched_at}`; accept: recorded fixtures give the golden tuples, a missing adapter exits 5 naming the `[integrations]` key, a write-scoped key is refused by WP1.3 before any call. WP1.3: `doctor` verifies read-only scope where the provider API allows | Built ahead of M5 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12) with no PostHog or Sentry account and no read-only GitHub key, so no exchange could be captured live. `[integrations.<name>]` had only `secret` and `scope`, and the providers also need a host, an organization, and a project. The plan names no metrics and no GitHub token for baselines. No verb consumes a baseline before `grill` (WP5.3) | `internal/integrations` (the tuple, `Resolve`, a read-only transport) with the adapters `posthog`, `sentry`, and `githubread`, and the catalog `providers` (`Baseline` for verbs, `Check` for `doctor`). `[integrations.<name>]` gains `url` (when not the default host), `org` (Sentry), and `project` (PostHog project ID, Sentry project ID, or the GitHub `owner/name`, default the current repository). Metrics: PostHog `events` and `users` (one HogQL `count()` or `count(DISTINCT person_id)`, the filter a HogQL predicate); Sentry `crash_free_sessions` and `crash_free_users` (sessions API, a fraction in [0, 1]; no sessions is an error, not 0), `errors` and `users_affected` (events API, errors dataset); GitHub `issues_opened`, `issues_closed`, `prs_opened`, `prs_merged` (search API `total_count`; incomplete results are refused). The window is half-open `[from, to)` in UTC; `population` names the provider, project, and filter; `source_query` is the exact request (method, URL, and PostHog's JSON body), so re-running it over the window reproduces `value`. GitHub baselines use their own key, `[integrations.github]`, not the human token, so the read-only rule covers them too.
