Before any data request, in order: a missing section, field, or secret is `ErrNotConfigured` (exit 5; the message names the section, the fields to add, and the `aayu auth set-secret` command); `scope = "write"` is `secrets.ErrWriteScoped` (exit 4) with zero requests sent; then the provider's own report of the key (PostHog `GET /api/personal_api_keys/@current/`, Sentry `GET /api/0/`, GitHub `X-OAuth-Scopes` on `GET /user`), where any scope that is not read-only (`*`, `…:write`, `project:releases`, `repo`) is refused (exit 4). Fine-grained and App GitHub tokens report no scopes, so the configured scope is trusted and `doctor` says so. Every adapter request passes a transport that allows GET and HEAD plus the one PostHog query POST and refuses anything else as `actions.ErrCapability`, so the adapters cannot write and have nothing to put in the actions log. The fixtures are synthetic, written from each provider's API reference and labelled so in their `source` field; `TestBaselinesGolden` replays them request by request (method, URL, body, auth header) against tuples written by hand. `TestLive` (behind `AAYU_LIVE=1`) skips each provider whose key is absent; its GitHub case passed against this repository on 2026-10-12. `doctor`'s `integrations` check lists every adapter (`not configured; set [integrations.<name>] for …` is not a failure), refuses a write-scoped key without sending it, and reports reachability and the verified scope; an unreachable provider or a write-capable key fails the check without blocking. The CLI maps `ErrNotConfigured` and provider errors to exit 5 and an invalid query to exit 2 |
| D-6.4a | WP6.4: Figma REST + PAT primary (`get images`, nodes, variables), remote MCP opt-in; Mobbin MCP client returning URLs and IDs only, never pixels; lint blocks Mobbin-origin images anywhere; accept: `doctor` reachability, a `research` fixture returns a reference set with URLs only | Built ahead of M6 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12) with no Figma or Mobbin account. Mobbin's MCP endpoint (`https://api.mobbin.com/mcp`, streamable HTTP, Pro plan and up) and Figma's (`https://mcp.figma.com/mcp`) authorise with OAuth only, and the Go SDK's client-side OAuth is experimental (V5). Mobbin's results carry a preview image and an expiring `image_url` beside the page link; its published tools are `search_screens`, `search_flows`, `search_sections`, all read-only, and their exact result shape is not published. `research` itself is WP6.5. The WP3.1 Mobbin rule covered skills only | `internal/integrations/figma`: REST with `X-Figma-Token` (`Nodes` from `/v1/files/:key/nodes`, `Images` as rendered URLs from `/v1/images/:key`, `Variables` from `/v1/files/:key/variables/local` in each collection's default mode with aliases resolved and colours as `#rrggbb[aa]`); `[integrations.figma] transport = "mcp"` (a new key, Figma only) switches `Nodes` and `Variables` to the remote MCP tools `get_metadata` and `get_variable_defs` (variables used by the given nodes, without collection or type) and refuses `Images` (REST only). `internal/integrations/mobbin`: `Search(kind, query, platform, limit)` returns an `integrations.ReferenceSet` of `{source, kind, id, url}`: the ID and the `https://mobbin.com/…` page URL of each result, read from structured content or JSON text, plus Mobbin page links in plain text; image, audio, and embedded blocks are never read, and image URLs, CDN hosts, app names, and descriptions are dropped, so the set holds URLs and IDs only (a result whose only link is an image is dropped). Only arguments the tool's input schema declares are sent. For both MCP servers the secret is an OAuth access token minted outside aayudha (for example by a host's MCP login) and sent as a bearer token; there is no refresh, so an expired token shows as unreachable in `doctor`. The token's scope is the server's tool list: a tool that is neither one the adapter knows as read-only nor annotated `readOnlyHint` makes the token write-capable, and the adapter then refuses to call anything (exit 4); if Figma's server offers write tools to a token, the opt-in is refused and REST stays the path. MCP traffic passes the read-only transport (POST and DELETE to the endpoint path only). `providers` adds `figma` and `mobbin` (`Check` for `doctor`, `providers.Figma` and `providers.Mobbin` for WP6.5's verbs). `doctor` reports Figma reachable through `GET /v1/me` (Figma does not report a PAT's scopes, so the token needs `current_user:read` and the configured scope is trusted) and Mobbin through `initialize` and `tools/list`. Lint: `skills.MobbinImages` applies the WP3.1 rule to any tree, and `TestNoMobbinOriginImages` (CI `build`) runs it over the whole repository except `.git` and `node_modules`; product repositories and `ledger/design/` get it with the WP6.5 artifacts. Fixtures are synthetic and labelled so: a replayed REST session for Figma, and recorded MCP surfaces served by an in-process go-sdk server for Mobbin and Figma. `TestLive` in each package runs behind `AAYU_LIVE=1` and skips without `AAYU_SECRET_FIGMA` (plus `AAYU_LIVE_FIGMA_FILE`, `AAYU_LIVE_FIGMA_NODE`) or `AAYU_SECRET_MOBBIN` |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP6.7 and WP7.2 (ahead of M6 and M7)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-6.7a | WP6.7: `experiment` and `readout` on GrowthBook + PostHog, with a minimum sample size, writing Results; accept: fixture lift computed, under-sample refused | Built ahead of M6 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12) with no PostHog or GrowthBook account. Integration keys are read-only (WP1.3), so the binary cannot create the feature flag the registry lists as an `experiment` output. GrowthBook's REST API has no endpoint that reports a key's role. The plan names no statistical test, metric type, or Result format, and WP2.8's "a readout/Result exists" does not say where the readout lives | `internal/experiment` holds the statistics, all deterministic (rule 7): the per-arm minimum sample of a two-sided two-proportion z-test, `n = (z(1-α/2)·√(2p̄(1-p̄)) + z(1-β)·√(p1(1-p1)+p2(1-p2)))² / (p2-p1)²` rounded up, and a readout of two arms (pooled z, two-sided p, unpooled 1-α confidence interval for the difference); provider statistics are never used. `experiment <feature> --hypothesis --metric --flag --mde [--exposure --alpha --power --days]` (new registry inputs; parent a Feature or Initiative, D-2.1a) takes the baseline rate from two reproducible PostHog baselines over the last `--days` whole UTC days (persons in the `--exposure` population, and those among them who sent `--metric`), refuses a zero population (baseline gate, exit 4), computes the size from `--mde` (absolute `0.02` or relative `10%`), alpha 0.05 and power 0.8 by default, and files an Experiment (`status:planned`) as a sub-issue whose Baseline lists both tuples and whose Minimum sample is `N per arm; two-sided two-proportion z-test, alpha …, power …, baseline rate …, detectable rate …`; a second open Experiment on the same flag under the same parent is refused. The flag is created in PostHog or GrowthBook by a human. `readout <experiment> [--source posthog\|growthbook] [--metric] [--from]` re-derives the size from the Minimum sample parameters and refuses (exit 4, nothing written) when the stated size differs (the body is untrusted, §0.1) or when either arm has fewer users than the size (the min-sample gate). PostHog counts come from one HogQL query over the `$feature/<flag>` event property, grouped by variant, from `--from` (default the Experiment's creation) to now, with `control` as the control arm; the flag key and event name are refused unless they match a strict pattern, since they reach HogQL from the issue body. GrowthBook: `[integrations.growthbook]` (new adapter `internal/integrations/growthbook`, doctor reachability through `GET /api/v1/experiments?limit=1`; the configured read scope is trusted and doctor says so) finds the experiment by tracking key (= the flag key) and reads the metric's frequentist numerator and denominator per variation from the latest results, the first variation as control; a metric that is not a conversion (numerator above denominator or not whole) is refused. Readout compares exactly two arms. It sets `status:measured` first (so the Ready guard has no `status:ready` to strip on the body edit), then writes the Result: relative and absolute lift, the verdict at alpha, z, p, the interval, a per-arm table, the window, and the exact source query. `status`'s parent-close rule now also accepts an Experiment child with a Result as the Feature's readout. Fixtures are synthetic and labelled so; the hand-worked sizes (3841 for 0.10 → 0.12 at 0.05 and 0.8; 10921 for 0.05 → 0.06 at 0.05 and 0.9) and lifts (PostHog +20 %, z 2.8748, p 0.00404; GrowthBook −10 %, z −1.8879, p 0.0590) are in the tests. `TestLiveReadout` runs behind `AAYU_LIVE=1` and skips without `AAYU_SECRET_GROWTHBOOK` or the PostHog settings |
| D-7.2a | WP7.2: `measure` extends the WP5.2 adapters with GrowthBook, billing CSV, and App Insights/AWS/GCP exports; SQLite (`modernc.org/sqlite`) snapshots, DuckDB optional shell-out; AARRR/North Star queries; Results on Goal/Initiative; triggers the parent-close rule; accept: fixture exports → expected cohorts | Built ahead of M7 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12) with no billing, Azure, AWS, GCP, or GrowthBook account. The plan names no export formats, no AARRR definitions, no metric syntax for a Goal, and no home for snapshots. `modernc.org/sqlite` v1.60 requires Go 1.26 (D5). DuckDB is cgo (R7). WP2.8 says Initiatives and Goals close "on measure against baseline" without saying when a measure closes them | Exports are files, read by `internal/integrations/exports` as untrusted input (§0.1): `--billing` a CSV with a header (`user_id`/`Customer ID`, `created`/`Created date (UTC)`, `amount`, `currency`, optional `status`, only `paid`/`succeeded` counted, and `amount refunded`, netted), `--appinsights` Application Insights events as JSON lines (Log Analytics export `TimeGenerated`/`Name`/`UserAuthenticatedId` else `UserId`, or classic `timestamp`/`name`/`user_Id`), `--aws` CloudWatch RUM events (`event_timestamp` ms, `event_type`, `user_details.userId`), `--gcp` the GA4/Firebase BigQuery export (`event_timestamp` µs, `event_name`, `user_id` else `user_pseudo_id`). Rows become fixed, bounded fields (ids ≤ 256 bytes, names ≤ 200, no control characters, amounts with at most two decimals into minor units, a 1 GiB file cap); an unparsable row is counted as skipped, an unpaid one as excluded, and a file with no usable row is refused (exit 2). Sources are joined on the exported user id. GrowthBook joins the provider catalog as a baseline source (`growthbook:conversion_rate <tracking key> <metric ID>`, the control variation's rate in the latest results, windowed by GrowthBook's own analysis dates). `modernc.org/sqlite` v1.59.0 (the newest on Go 1.25) builds the snapshot at `.aayudha/state/measure/<issue or all>-<UTC time>.sqlite` (git-ignored; the Result records its SHA-256 and every export's, so it can be rebuilt; pushing snapshots to `ledger` is left for later) with tables `events`, `payments`, `sources`, and `meta`, keeping rows in the half-open window (`--from`, `--to`, default the 28 days before today). New config `[measure] acquisition activation activation_days retention referral north_star`. AARRR per weekly acquisition cohort, all bounds half-open: acquired at the first acquisition event in the window (any event when unset); activated by the activation event within `activation_days` (default 7); retained by activity (the retention event, or any) in days 7–13; referred by a referral event after acquisition; paying by a payment after acquisition, with revenue. A cohort is complete when its last user is observable for the whole horizon (`max(activation_days, 14)` days) inside the window. North Star: distinct users who sent the configured event, or `revenue` (the paid total). One plain-SQL query per question, run by the built-in engine or, with `--engine duckdb`, by the `duckdb` binary found on `PATH` (attaching the snapshot through its sqlite extension, with only `PATH` and `HOME` in its environment); both give the same rows, and doctor lists `duckdb` among optional tools. `measure <goal or initiative>` reads the metric from `--metric` or the Metric field: `acquisition` (users acquired), `activation`, `retention`, `referral`, `revenue` (rates over complete cohorts only; none complete is exit 4), `north_star`, or `<source>:<metric> [filter]` for a provider baseline over the window. The baseline is the first number in Baseline (none is the baseline gate, exit 4); the target is Target's optional comparator and first number (without one, the direction it lies from the baseline); a trailing `%` divides by 100. It sets `status:done` when the target is met, else `status:measured`, then writes the Result (verdict first, `Target met:` or `Target not met:`, then value, baseline, target, change, window, snapshot, sources, and the query), then applies the parent-close rule to the item and everything under it. `status` computes that rule for Goals and Initiatives too: every child released, measured, done, or cancelled, and a Result that starts `Target met:`, so `status --apply` also closes them. The fixtures are synthetic (`internal/measure/testdata/README.md`); the expected cohorts are worked out by hand in `TestMeasureCohortsAndResults`, and `TestMeasureDuckDB` compares DuckDB's answer when the binary is installed (it passed locally against DuckDB v1.5.6; CI skips it). Binary size grows from 21.6 to 25.7 MB (linux/amd64), under the 60 MB budget |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP5.1 and WP5.7 (seats and autonomy, ahead of M4.5)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-5.1a | WP5.1: RACI generated from the registry with one Accountable seat per verb and one owner per layer, executes vs owns distinguished, rendered into `AGENTS.md`; `seat add <seat> --human <login>` writes `[seats.<seat>]`, adds the login to CODEOWNERS for the seat's layers, posts the charter and a "how we work" digest as an onboarding issue, and flips the WP0.1 solo-mode policy off when it adds the second human (D2: one approval with native CODEOWNER review; the gate and the admin bypass removed); `remove` reverses it, hands open stories' Technical Owner to the twin, and records the change on `ledger/seats/`. Accept: registry lint; `seat add` fixture gives the expected CODEOWNERS diff and onboarding issue; `seat remove` leaves no dangling human owner | Built ahead of M4.5 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). GitHub evaluates CODEOWNERS from the base branch and never counts an author's own approval, so switching the ruleset in the same step as the CODEOWNERS change deadlocks (nobody can approve the PR that makes the second human a code owner), and after the bypass is gone a path whose only code owner is one human can never be changed by that human. The gate reads only root-anchored literal patterns and fails closed on any other. `init` does not install the gate (D-2.5a), so only aayudha has the solo-mode ruleset. A personal repository has no organization to count humans in. The registry had no Responsible, Consulted, or Informed data | RACI: `Command.Seat` is A; R is always A's agent twin in the verb's lane (with its paired human); `Command.Owns` (else A) owns thresholds and policy, so `verify` is executed by the AI Native Engineer against the Quality Architect's floors and `inspect` policy is the Engineering Manager's, executed by the review lane; C and I live in `internal/verbs/raci.go`. `verbs.LintRACI` (A known; every verb has a RACI entry; C and I known, unique, and distinct from A and the threshold owner; every layer one known owner) runs in the generator and `TestLintRACI`, so a breach fails `go generate` and CI. The generator writes `docs/RACI.md`, the region between `BEGIN/END GENERATED: raci` in each hand-authored `seats/<seat>/CHARTER.md` (twelve; generation fails if one is missing or a stray seat directory exists), and the `aayudha:raci` section of `AGENTS.md` through `hosts.RenderAgentsMD`; `seat add\|remove` render the same section into a product's `AGENTS.md`. Humans are counted as the distinct user logins in CODEOWNERS plus paired seat humans, excluding teams, emails, and `[identity] bot_logins`. `seat` keeps one block at the end of CODEOWNERS (`# BEGIN aayudha:seats` … `# END aayudha:seats`) rebuilt from the paired seats, never editing hand-written lines: each glob of a paired seat's layers becomes a root-anchored literal line (`dir/**` → `/dir/`) owned by the seat's human and the hand-written owners; with two or more humans every hand-written line is re-declared with the paired humans of the layers that cover it, and any line that would name fewer than two humans gets every human, so each human can review the other's changes. The same seats render the same block, so `remove` restores the file byte for byte. The policy switch reconciles ruleset `protected-paths-codeowner-review` (found by name; absent means "n/a" on product repositories): second-human review (one approval, CODEOWNER review, the `protected-paths` check dropped, no bypass actors) only when both the checkout's rendered CODEOWNERS and the default branch's (contents API) name two humans; so `seat add` is run twice, once to write the files (merged by a solo-mode bypass) and again after the merge to switch. Solo mode (no approvals, CODEOWNER review on, the check required with the GitHub Actions app, the admin role as bypass in pull-request mode) returns as soon as either names fewer than two, and `remove` switches back before writing files so the founder can merge them. `seat add\|remove` are refused for agents, need a terminal yes or a human's `--yes`, verify the login is a GitHub `User` and not a bot login, and refuse a seat already paired with someone else. The onboarding issue (label `seat:onboarding`, found again by a marker so reruns post nothing) holds the digest, the seat's generated RACI, and the charter. `remove` rewrites the Technical Owner field of open Story and Bug bodies and the Technical Owner row of their Ready snapshot comments that name the human (when the human sits in no other seat) to "the `<Seat>` twin"; the Ready guard then strips `status:ready` from a story whose body changed, so it is re-readied. Both actions append to `seats/changes.jsonl` on `ledger` when they change something. Tested against the fake GitHub with a fixture checkout holding aayudha's own CODEOWNERS and config and the live ruleset's JSON (`TestSeatAddRemove`); this repository's rulesets were not changed and no second human was added (founder action) |
| D-5.7a | WP5.7: `aayudha-streak.yml` (protected) computes the streak per layer footprint from merged-PR history; corrections detected automatically (changes-requested review, non-bot commit on the PR, post-pass `fix`/`rebuild`, revert, Incident/Bug linked within N days) plus the manual `aayu:correction`; `autonomy/corrections.jsonl` on `ledger` written by CI; uses the WP1.6 `[notify]` sender; `aayu halt` sets a repository variable every workflow and verb checks, `aayu resume` clears it. Accept: streak math at 19/20/21 and on each correction kind; `halt` stops a running loop within one poll | Built ahead of M4.5 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). D1: the workflow token can never push `ledger`, and the bot App does not exist yet. The plan fixes neither N, nor which PRs are agent work, nor what "post-pass" and "linked" mean. V7 fixes the plumbing list, so there is no command slot for the streak. `inspect` (WP4.8) and `build` (WP5.5), whose dispositions and checkpoint commits the detection reads, are not built. A required check whose job is skipped counts as passing, so a job-level `if:` cannot halt CI. A running job never re-reads `vars`. The App manifest (D-2.6a) and the workflow token have no Variables permission | `internal/autonomy` computes, `aayu status --streak` (a new `status` input; MCP read, `--apply` still pinned off) reports, and the template `aayudha-streak.yml` (embedded, golden-tested, installed by `init --gates` as the fourth gate workflow, and committed on aayudha as the template rendered for `dev`) runs it on every push to `dev`, daily at 05:17 UTC, and on dispatch, with only read permissions. Agent work: a PR opened by a bot (`type: Bot`, a `[bot]` login, or `[identity] bot_logins`) or labelled `agent-authored` or `identity:human-fallback`. Corrections, per agent PR: any `CHANGES_REQUESTED` review; a commit whose author is not a bot, is not a merge, and carries no `Aayudha-Checkpoint:` trailer (so on the day-one path every hand-made commit counts); a comment holding an `<!-- aayudha:` marker and `disposition: fix\|rebuild` posted after the first human approval (n/a until `inspect` and `review` post them); a later merged PR titled `Revert "<title>"`, saying `Reverts …#N`, or carrying a commit that reverts the merge commit; a `type:bug` or `type:incident` issue created within `[autonomy] correction_days` (new key, proposed 14) after the merge that names `#N` or `…/pull/N`; the `aayu:correction` label. Footprints come from the layer globs in `LAYERS.md` at the checkout root (`verify.ReadLayers`, WP4.3), else the registry. A layer's streak is the number of agent PRs on it merged after its newest corrected one; it is autonomous at `[autonomy] streak` (20) unless it is in `[autonomy] never`, and a footprint is autonomous only when every layer in it is. The newest 100 merged PRs are scanned. With `--apply`, refused outside `--ci`, the workflow appends the corrections the ledger file lacks (keyed by PR, kind, and ref; read through the contents API at `ledger`) under the App token and pages them through `[notify]`; without `AAYUDHA_APP_CLIENT_ID` it runs without `--apply` and logs a `::notice::` that the ledger write was skipped, and the streak still lands in the job summary. Halt: `aayu halt [--reason]` sets the Actions variable `AAYUDHA_HALT` to the reason, login, and time (kept on a second halt) and pages through `[notify]` (urgent); `aayu resume` (refused for agents) deletes it, clears the `[budget]` breaker's pause file, and pages. Every job in every aayudha workflow (13 in `ci.yml`, 6 in `release.yml`, the others, the protected-paths trio, and the broker, Ready guard, triage, and streak templates) starts with a step that fails while `vars.AAYUDHA_HALT` is set, so a halted repository can merge nothing; template steps that run the binary pass `AAYUDHA_HALT` in their environment. Every verb except the read-only `doctor` checks before it runs (the environment when a workflow set it, else the API with the human token; agent lanes and CI fail closed when GitHub cannot answer, humans get a warning) and, when it could read the API, re-reads it every 30 s while it runs and is cancelled with exit 4 when it is set. The host `pre-tool` hook blocks agent tool calls while halted, asking GitHub at most once per poll. `halt` does not cancel workflow runs already past their first step (SECURITY.md says to cancel them). Tested: `TestStreakAtThreshold` and `TestCorrectionKinds` (each kind with a near miss that must not count), `TestStatusStreak` (fake GitHub, local ledger), `TestHaltStopsRunningLoop` (200 ms poll), `TestHaltFromWorkflowAndHook`, and `TestHaltLive` (AAYU_LIVE=1: a scratch repository, a 3 s poll; stopped 1.3 s after the halt on 2026-10-12). A workflow carrying the halt step on a scratch repository failed at that step while halted and passed after `aayu resume`. The ledger write from CI is untested live until the founder creates the App |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP6.5 and WP6.6 (design verbs and UI review, ahead of M4.5)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-6.5a | WP6.5: `research`, `wireframe`, `mockup`, `prototype` "as settled", artifacts under `ledger/design/<feature>/`; prototype behind an OpenFeature flag with Code Connect mapping; screenshot diff vs Figma within `[review] figma_diff_max` (2 % of pixels after an anti-aliasing mask) with colour and typography token mismatches counted apart and zero; accept: the eval chain on a fixture feature produces all four with pinned references, a 1.5 % prototype passes and a 2.5 % one fails, a non-token colour fails regardless of pixel diff | Built ahead of M4.5 and M6 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12) with no Figma or Mobbin account. The four verbs run in the host (mode `host`), so the plan leaves open what the binary checks, what "pinned" means, what a token mismatch is, how a flag counts as "behind", and where states come from (a Feature has no criteria). The WP3.4 harness replays calls against a file-serving fake GitHub with no ledger remote, Figma, or Mobbin. Figma's local-variables endpoint is Enterprise-only. Design media on a public ledger meet V3 | `internal/design` plus one handler per verb (`internal/cli/design.go`). The host writes each stage into a `--from` directory with a `stage.json` (new registry inputs: `research --from --query --platform`, `wireframe\|mockup\|prototype --from`, `prototype --captures`); the binary checks it, refuses Mobbin-origin images in it (the WP6.4 rule), and lands `design/<feature>/<stage>/manifest.json` plus its files on the ledger under the bot, or the founder tagged `identity:human-fallback` (D1). Research: references are https page URLs with an ID (never an image; Mobbin ones must be Mobbin page URLs), from the host and, with `--query`, Mobbin screens through the WP6.4 adapter; notes are Markdown. Wireframes: one file per state (SVG, HTML, Markdown, or an image). Mockups: SVG or HTML per wireframe state; any colour literal that is not a token value fails (exit 3). Prototype: the flag must be a boolean flag in `[design] flags` (flagd JSON, new key, default `flags.flagd.json`) whose default variant is false; every Figma component in `components` needs a Code Connect mapping (figma.com URLs with a node-id in the listed sources); each state (URL path on the `[verify.environments]` target, never production; Figma frame ID) is rendered by the embedded Playwright script at the frame's size and compared with Figma's PNG render (REST `images`, downloaded without the Figma token): a pixel differs above a YIQ distance of 0.1, and pixels that look like anti-aliasing in either image are masked (an independent implementation of the Vysniauskas 2009 detector pixelmatch uses). The gate passes when the percentage is at most `[review] figma_diff_max` (new, 2.0, rendered in CONSTRAINTS.md) and there are zero colour mismatches (computed text, background, visible border, outline, SVG fill and stroke colours that are not token values) and zero typography mismatches (font sizes, weights, and families outside the token scale), plus zero drift between Figma variables and tokens of the same name when the variables endpoint answers. A miss exits 3 and writes nothing. "Pinned": each manifest records the previous stage's manifest by SHA-256, carries research's references forward, and pins Figma frames by the SHA-256 of their render. Media follow the proof rules: text on the ledger; images to the `[proof] media` bucket, else Git LFS on a private repository, else only their hashes (this repository, public, without a bucket). `[design] tokens` (new, default the design system's `tokens.json`). The eval chain is `TestDesignChain` (CI `build`): the host's recorded outputs for a fixture Feature (`internal/cli/testdata/design/orders`) through all four verbs against the fake GitHub, a local ledger remote, the recorded Mobbin MCP surface, and a recorded Figma REST session with generated renders; each stage pins the one before. `TestFigmaDiffTolerance` (generated images: 1.5 % passes, 2.0 % passes, 2.5 % fails), `TestNonTokenColourFailsRegardlessOfPixels`, and `TestPrototypeCaptureWithBrowser` (CI `floors-web`: the fixture prototype in Chromium renders only with its flag on; 1.50 % passes, 2.50 % fails, a non-token colour fails at 0.03 %) |
| D-6.6a | WP6.6: lane A (Gemini 3.1 Pro / Fable 5.1 medium, `--deep` high) with Nielsen 10×0–4 and personas incl. screen-reader; lane B (impeccable `detect --json`, Apache-2.0, shelled out; axe); isolated; dispositions `recapture\|rebuild\|fix\|ship`; `polish` consumes the critique; `baseline` per SHA; lane A k = 3, a finding counts when ≥ 2 of 3 runs report it at severity ≥ 2; accept: three planted defects (missing focus ring, 3.8:1 body text, 32 px tap target) each caught by lane A in ≥ 2 of 3 runs and by lane B in 1 of 1, a clean fixture ships from both lanes, `polish` removes all three on re-run | Built ahead of M4.5 and M6 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). The gateway serves only `gemini-3.1-pro-low` (D-1.6a), and Fable 5.1 is anthropic, so it is valid only when the builder is not. impeccable 4.5.2 on the planted page reports only the contrast defect: it has no rule for a missing focus indicator, and its touch-target rule did not fire on a 32 px button at 1280×800 or 390×844. axe's `target-size` is WCAG 2.5.8 (24 px), which 32 px passes, and axe has no focus-visible rule. The plan defines no matching of findings across runs, no disposition rules, and no source of the states. The model gateway sent text only. `polish` runs in the host (mode `host`), so its acceptance needs a host model | `internal/uireview` plus `review`, `polish`, and `baseline` handlers (`internal/cli/review.go`). States are the screenshot and recording criteria of the story's Ready snapshot on the `[verify.environments]` target (never production), and the checkout must be the PR head. The embedded Playwright script captures each state once at 1280×800 (a screenshot at rest and one after a Tab press, the markup without comments, and the accessibility tree), and both lanes judge that capture without seeing each other. Lane A is the ui-review lane resolved on the direct host (`gemini-3.1-pro-low` through the gateway), checked by `models.CheckFamilies` against the builder's family (the local pack's model, else the build lane on the host). It runs 3 times per state at effort `medium` (`high` with `--deep`), with both screenshots (`models.Message.Images`, new: each wire format's image block) and the markup and tree in `<untrusted>` blocks. It answers structured JSON: findings (a kind from a closed list such as `focus-visible`, `contrast`, and `target-size`, a Nielsen heuristic and 0–4 severity, the element, a summary, and a fix), the 10 heuristics' worst severities, the four personas (keyboard-only, screen-reader, low-vision, first-time), and `rebuild`. Consensus is per state and kind: the kind counts when ≥ 2 runs report it at severity ≥ 2 (severity is the lower median of those runs), and `rebuild` also needs 2 runs. A state with fewer than 2 answering runs fails the lane (exit 5). Lane B is impeccable 4.5.2 (`detect --json --no-config --no-advisory`, Playwright's Chromium through `CHROME_PATH`, telemetry and update checks off, pinned in `packages/floors` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`), axe (WCAG 2.2 AA tags), and two probes from the WP4.5 floor: a focus-indicator probe that tabs through the page and flags any control whose outline, shadow, border, background, colour, and underline are unchanged when focused, and controls under `[verify.floors.a11y] min_target_px` (44). Every lane B finding counts. Dispositions, worst first: `recapture` (a capture failed), `rebuild`, `fix` (any counted finding), `ship`. The combined disposition is the worse lane's, so lane A cannot override lane B (rule 7). `review` writes `.aayudha/review/<pr>/critique.json` with every run's reply, keeps the screenshots, and posts a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:review -->` PR comment; it exits 4 unless `ship`. `polish` needs that critique, re-runs both lanes, and reports each finding as resolved, remaining, or new (matched by lane, state, and kind); it exits 4 while any remain. The host makes the fixes (skill). `baseline [--sha]` refuses unless the checkout is at the SHA, captures each path in `[review] baseline` (new, proposed `["/"]`), and writes `baselines/<sha>/manifest.json`. Screenshots follow the proof media rules (D-6.5a). Tests: `TestLaneAConsensus` (2 of 3 counts, 1 of 3 and severity 1 do not, rebuild at 2 of 3), `TestLaneBAndDispositions`, `TestCompare`, and `TestWireFormats` (images per format) in CI `build`. `TestLaneBWithBrowser`, `TestReviewAndPolish` (CLI end to end with a scripted lane A), and `TestBaseline` run in CI `floors-web`. Live acceptance: `TestReviewLive` in the dispatch-only `ux-review-live.yml` (environment `ai-gateway`). It runs the real lane A and lane B on the planted and clean fixtures, then polish, where the build lane's model (`claude-opus-5-5` through the gateway) applies the critique to the page source with the polish skill as its instructions, standing in for the host as tier-2 evals do. Both lanes then re-run. Results: the run's artifact `ux-review-live-<run id>` |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP7.3 and WP7.4 (brief, ahead of M7)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-7.3a | WP7.3: registers `eli5\|ste\|engineer\|regulatory`; purposes `explain\|ideate\|innovate\|close`; HTML artifact with grounding footer; STE100 linter; a11y check on the HTML; `close` refuses without an authoritative pack and Result; ground rule 5: the `brief` adversarial pass calls a model directly. Accept: planted 30-word sentence fails STE lint; zero serious/critical axe findings and every image has alt text; `brief close` on a story without an authoritative pack exits 4 | Built ahead of M7 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). The registry declares `brief` hybrid (the host's model writes), but the plan names no draft format, grounding format, or citation syntax. The ASD-STE100 dictionary is ASD's copyright and cannot be redistributed, and no deterministic part-of-speech tagger is available. A Story or Bug has no Result field (its Complete section has Outcome). The plan does not say which packs a `close` on a Goal or Feature needs, how a pack read from the ledger proves it is authoritative, or the exit codes of a lint miss and an unsupported claim | `internal/brief` and `aayu brief <issue> [--register] [--purpose] [--draft <file>]` (new registry input `draft`; defaults `engineer`, `explain`). Without `--draft` it prints the grounding to write from (`--json` for the struct): the subject with its parsed fields and body SHA-256 (untrusted, wrapped in a provenance block), every work item under it, the best pack of each story and bug from the ledger (`P1`…: authority, merge-gate run, head, floors, artifacts, SHA-256 of `pack.json`), the Result (`R`; a Story's or Bug's Outcome), and the measure snapshots the Result names (`D1`…, found under `.aayudha/state/measure/`). The host writes a Markdown draft: `# Title`, the takeaway paragraph, sections, and citations `[#n]`, `[P1]`, `[R]`, `[D1]`. With `--draft`, deterministic checks first (rule 7), exit 3 with no model call on any miss: structure (title, takeaway first, no raw HTML or Markdown images), every citation names a source and at least one exists, purpose (`ideate` an `## Ideas` section with two or more items, `innovate` a `## Hypothesis` section, `close` cites `R` and an authoritative pack), and the register: `eli5` a Flesch-Kincaid grade of at most 6 (0.39 × words/sentences + 11.8 × syllables/words − 15.59, vowel-group syllables, code spans and numbers one syllable); `ste` the STE lint: sentences of at most 20 words (colons and semicolons do not end one), every word on `skills/brief/references/ste-words.md` (aayudha's own list, 562 words by part of speech, regular `-s/-es/-ies/-ed` forms pass, plus 26 words to avoid with their replacements) unless it is a technical name (a code span, a number, an acronym, a capitalised word inside a sentence, or a word of the titles of the subject and its items or a pack's floor names), and one instruction per sentence (clauses split at commas, semicolons, `and`, `or`, `then`; a sentence that opens with an approved base-form verb, or a lead-in such as "If …," and then one, gives as many instructions as its clauses that open with one); `regulatory` a citation on every paragraph and list item. Then the adversarial pass: one structured call on the review lane (the direct host's default when the host's own is host-managed) with the grounding and the draft as untrusted blocks, refused unless its family differs from the spec lane's writer (`models.CheckFamilies`); any claim the sources do not support is exit 4 and nothing is rendered; `--dry-run` skips the call and the page says so. The page is one self-contained HTML file (goldmark with raw HTML off; Content Security Policy `default-src 'none'`, inline styles and script, `data:` images): a header with the register, purpose, and pack counts; citations linked to a grounding footer that lists every source with its hashes, the checks that passed, the adversarial verdict, and the accessibility result; written to `.aayudha/state/briefs/<n>/<register>-<purpose>.html` with a `.json` manifest. Accessibility: every `<img>` has alt text and every SVG image a title (checked in Go), then `verify.CheckPage` runs the WP4.5 web floor script on the file (axe-core with the WCAG 2.2 AA tags, Tab reachability, reduced motion); a serious or critical finding or a missing alt text is exit 3 with the page kept for review, and missing Playwright or axe-core is exit 5. `close` is refused (exit 4) before anything else unless the subject has a Result and every story and bug it covers (the subject itself, or every one under it, at least one) has an authoritative pack whose floors hold; a pack counts as authoritative only when it sits on an `…/authoritative/` ledger path and records `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` as its workflow. Tests: `TestSTELint` (the planted 30-word sentence, 20 and 21 words, an unapproved word with its replacement, two instructions), `TestReadability` (the formula on a hand-worked sentence), `TestCheckRules`, `TestBriefCLI` (close without an authoritative pack exits 4; a planted 30-word sentence exits 3 before any model call; an unsupported claim exits 4; a serious axe finding exits 3; close renders once the merge gate's pack and an Outcome exist), and `TestBriefPageWithBrowser` in the `floors` workflow (`floors-web`: 0 serious and 0 critical with Playwright 1.64.0 and axe-core 4.14.0) |
| D-7.3b | WP7.3 accept: register evals, tier 2 at the WP3.4 bar per register, the `eli5` rubric additionally requiring a reading grade of at most 6 and `ste` the STE lint | WP3.4's tier 2 drafts JSON work items, scores the suite as a whole, and has no step for the host's loop of rendering, reading `aayu brief`'s findings, and revising. The STE word list is a reference the host reads, not part of the skill body. Whether a grade or a lint passed is decidable, so it should not be left to the judge (rule 7) | Four cases on one grounding (`evals/cases/brief/grounding.json`: Feature #7 with Story #12, its authoritative pack P1, and a Result): `eli5-explain`, `ste-explain`, `engineer-ideate`, `regulatory-close`. A new case key `brief = { register, purpose, draft }`: tier 1 runs the same checks on the recorded draft and renders it (alt text). Tier 2: the skill model is `brief`'s registry lane (`spec`: `claude-opus-5-5`, fallback `claude-sonnet-5-5`), the judge stays `gpt-6.1-sol`, k = 5; the prompt is the skill body plus the case's `[tier2] references` (`ste-words.md` for `ste`) with the grounding as an untrusted block, and the reply is the Markdown draft. A draft that fails the checks gets the findings once and revises, as the skill's step 3 does in a host; one that still fails scores 1 without the judge (`revised` marks runs that needed the revision). So grade ≤ 6 and the STE lint are deterministic preconditions of a score, and the judge is told which checks passed. `Compare` applies the bar (pass^k ≥ 0.8, mean ≥ 3.5) to every case as well as the suite, which is the per-register bar (for the single stub case nothing changes). `max_usd` 3.0 → 9.0 for twenty more runs. `evals.yml`'s dev-push filter adds `internal/brief/`. `TestTier2BriefRevisesOnceThenJudges` covers the revision, the floor score, and the per-case bar. The first live run, evals.yml run 38200697472 on 41a8a56 (charged 6.21 USD), failed. The stub held (pass^5 1, mean 5.00), but every brief case had pass^5 0: eli5 mean 2.80, engineer-ideate 4.40, regulatory-close 3.80, and ste-explain 2.67 with 2 errors. No draft failed the deterministic checks. The judges scored 3 for a changed fact: a rate per reviewer given as "each reviewer" or with "per reviewer" dropped, a button or a save action no source names, "merged" for "released", "not tested" for "out of scope". In `ste`, the lint rejected "reviewer" (not in the titles), so drafts wrote "users" and changed the metric. Two `ste` drafts spent the 4000-token cap on thinking. Fix (one round, as the founder asked): the skill's Draft rules now keep every number's unit and population, record states only as the sources do, and name no screen detail, reason, or effect that no source names; the words of the subject's summary and of the Result are technical names too; the skill cap is 8000 and `max_usd` 12.0. The verification run, evals.yml run 38202244333 on fec95a5 (charged 7.35 USD), still failed. Nothing errored and the stub held at 5.00. The brief cases still had pass^5 0: eli5 mean 3.80, engineer-ideate 3.40, regulatory-close 4.20, ste-explain 3.60. The remaining defect is in the grounding, not the drafts. `brief.Pack` lists a pack's artifacts but not its `criteria` map (criterion → evidence), so a draft that says "the recording proves criterion 3" makes a claim the judge cannot trace, and every case was marked down for it. Open (founder decision, no further fix round taken): add each pack's criterion → evidence map, and once WP4.8 posts it, the inspect disposition per criterion, to the grounding; or have the skill state criteria only as the expected results the evidence is mapped to |
| D-7.3c | WP7.3: the grounding footer and the adversarial pass hold a brief to its sources | After the D-7.3b fix round the remaining tier-2 marks were for evidence claims the grounding could not trace: the packs in the grounding listed artifacts but not which criterion each proves, nor inspect's verdicts. Founder decision, 2026-10-12: add the grounding, do not weaken the claims, and control cost | Each grounding pack carries its criterion → evidence map: every criterion of the story's Ready snapshot on the ledger (id, evidence kind, "state: …; expected: …"; the pack's own criterion ids when there is no snapshot) with the artifacts the pack maps to it (path, kind, SHA-256; empty when unmapped), plus the inspect disposition (model, head, who posted it) and per-criterion verdict and judgement from inspect's replace-in-place comment on the pack's PR, only when that result is for the pack's head. A mapped criterion is citable as `[P1.c3]`; an unmapped one has no citation. The evidence rule (`evidence` check, deterministic, before any model call): a sentence that says a criterion is proved, met, shown, verified, or evidenced, or that a recording, trace, screenshot, or transcript shows something, needs the criterion's map entry cited in the same paragraph or list item; a criterion with no mapped evidence must be said to be not proved; negated sentences claim nothing. The footer lists each pack's map under it (evidence with hashes, "No evidence in the pack; not proved", inspect verdicts), and citations of an entry link to it. New tier-1 case `brief/unmapped-criterion` on `grounding-unmapped.json` (criterion 3 unmapped, inspect `fix`): its draft passes, and `TestTier1UnmappedCriterionClaim` plants a proof claim and a citation of the missing entry, each failing. Cost: `eval -skip <prefixes>` leaves tier-2 cases out (recorded as `skipped` in the results file); `evals.yml` passes `-skip brief/` on pushes to `dev`, so the brief cases (about 6–7 USD a run) run nightly, on dispatch, and on pushes to `rolling` and `main`, whose results the promotion gate reads, while the stub keeps its trigger; a push touching only `internal/brief/` no longer starts tier 2. `max_usd` stays 12.0 |
| D-7.3d | WP7.3: register evals at the WP3.4 bar; one `max_usd` per tier-2 run | Run 38207049608 on bf0a837 (dispatch, charged 11.57 USD) after D-7.3c: no errors and no untraceable evidence claims, but pass^5 0 for eli5 (mean 4.40), engineer-ideate (4.60), and regulatory-close (4.20); ste passed (1, 4.80). Each failure was one lapse in one run: an interface control no source names ("press one button"), an inference stated as fact, a close record without what the measure cannot show, a rate per reviewer restated. The same run held every case of every skill under one 12 USD cap and spent 11.57 of it. Founder decision, 2026-10-12: these are skill defects; split the cap per group | Skill: an aggregate keeps the grounding's population wording; no UI element, control, or step the grounding does not name; an inference beyond the floors, the criterion map, or the Result is labelled as one or left out; a close brief (any register) and every `regulatory` brief has a `## Limits of this evidence` section stating what the evidence and the measures do not show. Deterministic checks (before any model call): `limits` (the section exists with prose, for close or `regulatory`); `population` (in a unit citing `[R]`, a number from the Result, dates and criterion numbers aside, needs the Result's `per <population>` wording, time units excepted, and `each`/`every <population>` is refused); `ui` (button, menu, tab, dialog, icon, click, tap, press, and the like are refused unless the grounding's text uses them). The recorded drafts of three brief cases failed these checks as written and were corrected; `TestTier1BriefRules` plants each defect on a copied case and fails tier 1. Rubrics say a labelled inference is not a claim and an unnamed control is invented; the regulatory rubric names what the measure cannot show. The STE list gains `suggest` and `inference`. Cost: `[[groups]]` in `eval.toml`, each with its own `max_usd`: `brief` (prefix `brief/`, 12 USD) and `skills` (every other case, 6 USD); `eval -group <name>` runs one group under its cap (`-skip` is gone), `eval -groups` lists them; `evals.yml` runs one matrix job per group (`evals-tier2 (<group>)`), skips the `brief` group on pushes to `dev`, takes a dispatch input `group`, and writes `results/<sha>-<group>.json` as the artifact `eval-results-<sha>-<group>` with main's same-group artifact as its baseline; `evals-gate` fetches every group's results for the promoted commit, compares each against main's, and fails when any group is missing or fails |
| D-7.3e | WP7.3: register evals at the WP3.4 bar | Run 38210971285 (`group=brief` on 2ddd4cb, charged 7.72 of 12 USD) after D-7.3d: regulatory-close (pass^5 1, mean 5.00) and ste (1, 4.60) passed; eli5 (0, 4.20, 3 of 5) and engineer-ideate (0, 4.60, 4 of 5) failed on one defect class: absence of evidence or an out-of-scope item written as a fact ("Other lists can't be saved this way yet", "Its behaviour at larger sizes is untested") and a count read as the behaviour behind it ("This count shows that people use the file"). Founder decision, 2026-10-12: one final round with a sharper rule, a deterministic check only where it can be precise | Skill and the four rubrics: absence of evidence is written "the evidence does not show …", never "not done", "not possible", "untested", or "can't"; out-of-scope items "outside this story's scope"; a measure shows only what it counts (name the count, not the behaviour behind it). Deterministic `absence` check: those words (and "not tested", "cannot") in prose are refused unless the phrase with its next word is the grounding's own ("cannot run" a screen reader, from a floor detail). The count-as-behaviour lapse cannot be told apart from a grounded statement by a general rule, so it stays with the rubric, plus a per-case `scope` guard on the four brief cases (a sentence saying a count shows that people, reviewers, or users use, read, open, or audit something), which brief cases now apply to their prose in tier 1 and, as a finding the draft revises once, in tier 2. `TestTier1BriefRules` plants the three quoted sentences, each failing tier 1 |
| D-7.3f | WP7.3: register evals at the WP3.4 bar | Run 38216409539 (`group=brief` on ba9e8f6, charged 7.89 USD) after D-7.3e: engineer-ideate and regulatory-close passed (pass^5 1, mean 5.00); eli5 (0, 3.60) renamed the measure's unit ("This number counts files saved"; the Result counts `export_csv` events), and ste (0, 3.60) wrote an out-of-scope item as "does not include", and once failed the 20-word STE limit after its revision because the population rule made it repeat "per reviewer per week" for a rate and its target in one 22-word sentence, a conflict our own checks created. Founder decision, 2026-10-12: make all three deterministic | `population` and the new `unit` check now work sentence by sentence in each unit citing `[R]`: a sentence stating one of the Result's numbers (dates and criterion numbers aside) carries the Result's `per <population>` once, however many rates it holds; each such number is followed within four words by the Result's unit noun, the word before the Result's first `per <population>` ("exports"), singular or plural; and everyday substitutes ("files saved", "files made", "downloads") are refused in those units unless they are the Result's own unit. The `absence` check adds "does not include", "doesn't include", "is not included", and "are not included" in a sentence that names an item the subject's Scope field lists after "Out of scope:" (an item is named when the sentence has all its content words). The skill tells `ste` to write a rate and its target as two sentences, and states the unit and exclusion rules; the rubrics name both. Five recorded drafts broke the unit rule as written ("4.2 times per reviewer", "3 saves", a target of "at least 3" with no unit) and were corrected. `TestTier1BriefRules` plants "This number counts files saved", the "does not include PDF files…" sentence, and the 22-word sentence, each failing tier 1, and checks that the split rate and target pass |
| D-7.3g | WP7.3 accept: register evals at the WP3.4 bar per register (pass^5 ≥ 0.8 and mean ≥ 3.5), with eli5 at Flesch-Kincaid ≤ 6 and ste passing the STE lint | **Not met as written.** After five fix rounds (D-7.3b to D-7.3f), run 38222172014 (`group=brief` on 88dddb9) gave pass^5 1 for regulatory-close (mean 5.00) and engineer-ideate (4.80), and pass^5 0 for ste (4.20, 4 of 5 runs passing) and eli5 (3.80, 3 of 5); suite pass^5 0.50, mean 4.45. The deterministic preconditions (grade, STE lint, citations, evidence map, limits, population, unit, UI, absence) hold on every judged run. Open causes are judgement calls no rule decides: passive voice in ste ("are met", "were lost") and everyday restatements in eli5 ("check them away from the screen" for audit offline). Total charged across the six brief tier-2 runs (38200697472, 38202244333, 38207049608, 38210971285, 38216409539, 38222172014): 49.43 USD at the gateway's charged rate (6.21, 7.35, 11.57, 7.72, 7.89, 8.69; the 11.57 run also held about 4.5 USD of other skills' cases). Founder decision, 2026-10-12: gate brief on the mean, keep #64 and #65 open on this criterion | A group in `eval.toml` may set `pass_k = "advisory"` (default `"required"`, the full WP3.4 bar; any other value is refused). `eval -compare` and `evals-gate` pass such a group on the mean bar, per case and for the suite (mean ≥ 3.5), the error limit, and the drop rule (mean at most 0.3 below main's baseline); its pass^k findings, absolute and against the baseline, are computed and printed as `ADVISORY` lines but do not fail it. Only the `brief` group sets it; every other group keeps the full bar (`TestGroupPassKPolicy` covers both sides, a register mean of 3.40, and a drop beyond 0.3). Cause 1 of D-7.3f is closed by rule: the unit check's substitutes now include "saves" and "files … saved/made/created/downloaded" in either order with up to three words between ("how many files were saved", "made more files"), with tier-1 fixtures for both forms. #64 and #65 carry a comment (through `internal/actions`) that their tier-2 criterion is not met at pass^5 and is gated on the mean only; their status is unchanged |
| D-7.3h | WP7.3: register evals; D-7.3g gated the brief group on the mean | The rolling candidate's tier 2 (evals.yml run 38230815198 on 662cd99) failed the brief group even on the mean: eli5 3.20 and ste 3.00 (engineer-ideate 4.00, regulatory-close 5.00). The judge marked eli5 down mostly for everyday restatements ("away from the screen", "they like to check offline"), which the eli5 register asks for, so its rubric is in tension with itself. It marked ste down for "did not run" and "cannot run", which the `absence` check deliberately allows when a floor detail says it. One ste run was failed before the judge by the `unit` check on "saves" in a Limits-style sentence, "the evidence does not show that the export saves time": a false positive, since "saves" there is a verb. Founder decision, 2026-10-12 | `[[groups]]` take `gate = "enforced"` (default) or `"advisory"`; any other value is refused. An advisory-gate group's tier 2 is computed and printed as before, but `eval -compare` and `-tier 2` exit 0 on a failing result (printing that the group does not gate), and `evals-gate` compares only gating groups (`eval -groups -gating`). New group `brief-plain` (prefixes `brief/eli5-`, `brief/ste-`, 6 USD, pass_k and gate advisory) holds the plain registers, before `brief`, which keeps engineer-ideate and regulatory-close on the D-7.3g mean bar at 6 USD. `evals.yml` skips both brief groups on pushes to `dev`. Tests: `TestGroupGatePolicy` (brief-plain does not gate; brief, skills, and an unknown group do; an unknown gate value is refused), `TestCompareAdvisoryGate` (the run's means exit 0 in brief-plain and fail in brief), and `TestGroups` for `-groups -gating`. The `unit` check counts "saves" only as a counted noun (after a number, "count(s)", "number of", or "many"), with fixtures on both sides, including the run's sentence. Feedback [#152](https://github.com/rahulnakmol/agentic-ayudha/issues/152), filed through `internal/actions`, describes the eli5 restatement versus fact-fidelity tension and the ste absence-phrasing conflict for `evolve`. #64 and #65 stay open on the tier-2 criterion |
| D-7.4a | WP7.4: `*.diagram` → SVG (static + interactive), charts from snapshots with their query, illustrations via Painter/Gemini labelled as such, annotated screens from packs; alt text states the takeaway. Accept: every chart carries a query and re-running the query reproduces the chart data (golden) | The plan names no diagram format. Image generation is a host tool (Painter) or a model, and CI must not call an image model. `measure`'s query engine returns integers only. Whether alt text "states the takeaway" is not decidable by rule | Visuals are fenced blocks (`diagram`, `chart`, `illustration`, `screen`): `key: value` header lines, then a body. `alt` is required on each: a sentence of at least four words ending in a full stop that does not open by naming the medium ("Diagram of …"); the judge and the adversarial pass read the rest. `*.diagram` (inline, or `src:` a file beside the draft): lines `id: Label \| note`, `a -> b: label`, `title:`, `direction: right\|down`; at most 40 nodes and 80 edges; layered layout (longest-path ranks, barycentre order), and a left-to-right flow wider than 760 px turns top-to-bottom unless a direction is set. It is written as a static SVG image file beside the page (`role="img"`, the alt as title, the edges in words as description) and drawn inline as an interactive group: nodes are focusable buttons, and focus, hover, click, Enter, or Space highlights a node with its neighbours and shows its note in a live region (Escape clears), with a "Diagram as text" list. Charts: `snapshot: D1` (a snapshot the Result names, its SHA-256 checked), a body of one read-only `SELECT` or `WITH` statement (no `ATTACH`, `PRAGMA`, or writes; opened `mode=ro` with `query_only`; at most 200 rows and 8 columns; integers, reals, and text, through `database/sql` on `modernc.org/sqlite`), `x`, `y`, and `kind: bar\|line`; drawn as an SVG chart with the data table, the snapshot and its hash, and the query in the page, and the rows in the manifest. Illustrations: `src` is an image the host made with Painter or Gemini and `generator` names it (required); PNG, JPEG, WebP, or GIF up to 5 MiB, Mobbin-origin images refused (WP6.4); captioned "Generated illustration made with … ; it explains, it is not evidence". The binary never calls an image model, and the tests use a labelled fixture image. Screens: `pack: P1` and `artifact:` a screenshot of that pack, read through the proof store with its SHA-256 checked; body lines `x,y: note` (percent) become numbered marks with a notes list. Tests: `TestChartGolden` (the acquisition cohorts 3, 2, 1 in weeks 0, 1, 3, worked out by hand from the WP7.2 fixture exports; re-running the stored query on the snapshot gives the same rows; write statements are refused) and `TestRenderSample` |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP5.6 and WP7.1 (decide, respond, loops; ahead of M5 and M7)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-5.6a | WP5.6: `decide` with `[decisions] authority.<type\|layer>`; an SLA in business hours: at 24 h a page to the authority and an escalation comment mentioning founder B (`escalate_to`), at 48 h a reversible recommendation applied provisionally (`decision:provisional`, ratify/revert deadline in the body; ratification clears it), an irreversible one only re-paged and shown overdue; `constrains`-linked stories shown blocked; supersede chain; `decide --waiver <floor>`; `ops/actions.toml` read only from the protected default branch at a pinned SHA, edits needing two human CODEOWNER reviews and never `auto` on first merge; `respond` proposing a catalog action that, for `approval = human`, executes only through a GitHub Environment with required reviewers and a short-lived credential never in the agent env; timeline; close with a linked Bug and retro entry. Accept: non-catalog command refused, same-PR catalog edit unusable, human approval required and verified, the 48-hour reversible/irreversible pair | Built ahead of M5 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). Nothing runs a sweep on a clock, so something must invoke it; `decide`'s registry inputs had a required Decision and no way to name the waived item; `respond` had no parameter or close input. The plan fixes no SLA start, ratify deadline, authority key set, or Decision-type taxonomy. A repository-wide rule cannot require two reviews for one path, and solo mode has one human (D2). A waiver applied provisionally would let an SLA lapse override a floor (rule 7). D-1.2a holds: the SLAs are business hours of `[time]` | `decide`'s positional is optional: `aayu decide` sweeps every open Decision (`type:decision`), `aayu decide <n>` one; WP7.1's loops run the sweep on a schedule. The clock starts at the issue's creation (as WP2.8's "near SLA" does) and counts `sla_hours` (24) and `provisional_after_hours` (48) business hours (`config.Clock.Add`; new `Clock.Between` gives status its hours left). At the SLA: one page per Decision per day through `[notify]` (key `decision-sla:` then `decision-overdue:`; on the GitHub channel the mention goes to the authority's login) and a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:decision-escalation -->` comment that mentions `@<escalate_to>`, or says nobody is configured; `escalate_to` stays unset by default (there is no founder B yet) and is validated as a login. At 48 h a Decision that is open, `reversible: true`, Decision-ready, not already provisional, and not a waiver gets `status:validation` (so the Ready guard does not strip it for the edit), a Decision section saying the recommendation applies from the 48-hour instant with "Ratify or revert by <RFC 3339>" one more `sla_hours` later, then `decision:provisional` last, so an interrupted run redoes it and a rerun writes nothing. Authority: `[decisions.authority]` keyed by the Decision's parent's work-item type, then `L04` for a floor waiver (the floors' layer), else the Founder seat; a seat value resolves to its paired human, else `[autonomy] approver`; no login configured means any human (day one). `decide <n> --accept <option>` is refused for agents and bot identities (`identity.IsBot`, `bot_logins`) and for anyone but the authority's login, needs the option named in Options (whole word) and a Decision-ready body, writes the Decision section, `status:done`, clears `decision:provisional`, closes it, and closes every `supersedes` target as not planned with a `<!-- aayudha:decision-superseded -->` comment. `status` counts open items an open Decision `constrains` as blocked and shows each provisional Decision's business hours left (`#n 9 h`, `overdue`, `no deadline`). `decide <story\|bug> --waiver <floor>` files a sub-issue Decision labelled `decision:waiver` (new label), `reversible: false`, recommendation `fix`, `waives #n`; reruns find the open one; the redaction floor is not waivable (V3); a waiver is never applied provisionally, whatever its body says. `verify` (D-4.9a) now applies only accepted waivers: closed as completed, by a non-bot, with `waive, accepted by @<login>` in the Decision section, so a filed waiver waives nothing (`TestVerifyMobileSkipAndWaiver` files one, sees the floor still fail, accepts it, then sees it waived); `audit` counting `decision:waiver` is n/a until WP7.6. Catalog (`internal/ops`, schema in `ops/README.md`): `version = 1`, `[[action]]` with `argv_template` (no shell; `{param}` fills a whole value; `sh -c` refused; the program is never a parameter), typed `params` (string with an anchored pattern and no leading `-`, int with bounds, enum, bool; `environment` reserved), `approval`, `environments`, `max_per_hour`, `rollback` (`none` or another action), `blast_radius` (`single\|service\|global`; global never auto); unknown keys refused. `respond` reads it through the contents API at the default branch's head SHA; the executor re-reads it at that pinned SHA only after the compare API shows the commit on the default branch. "Never auto on first merge" is enforced at read time: an `auto` action is effective only if its definition (everything but `approval`) first appeared on the default branch as `human`, walking up to 30 commits of the file's history, else it runs as `human`. Two CODEOWNER reviews: CODEOWNERS and the protected-paths gate cover `ops/`, and solo mode merges by the recorded admin bypass (D2); a second required review is n/a until a second human (WP5.1). `respond` gains `--params k=v,…` and `--close`. `respond <incident>` prints the incident wrapped as untrusted, the catalog, and the timeline, appending each finished `aayudha-ops.yml` run with who approved it; `--action` validates the action and parameters, refuses (exit 4) a human action whose environment has no required reviewers or lets any branch deploy, enforces `max_per_hour` over the last hour's runs, and dispatches `aayudha-ops.yml` on the default branch (`return_run_details`), appending to a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:timeline -->` comment. `aayudha-ops.yml` (template, installed by `init --gates` as the fifth gate workflow; not committed on aayudha, whose own catalog is empty) runs the job in the action's environment, so a human action waits for a required reviewer before any step runs; `respond --ci` then refuses unless the run is that workflow on the default branch with the matching run name, the catalog commit is on the default branch, a non-bot reviewer approved that environment (run approvals API), and the hourly cap holds, and runs the argv with only PATH/HOME/locale, the environment secret `AAYUDHA_OPS_CREDENTIAL`, and the job's OIDC request variables (no GitHub token, no `AAYU_*`); the executor writes nothing to GitHub. `--close` files the linked Bug (`caused-by #n`), appends `{incident, bug, timeline, …}` to `retro/incidents.jsonl` on the ledger, removes `needs:bug`, and closes the incident `status:done`. Tests: `TestDecideSLA` (47:59 and 48:00 business hours; the reversible Decision gains `decision:provisional` with a deadline of Thursday 10:00 London, the irreversible one and the waiver do not; every escalation mentions the fixture login `founder-b`; rerun writes nothing; acceptance refusals and supersede), `TestDecideWaiver`, `TestRespondProposes`, `TestRespondExecutes`, `TestRespondClose`, `internal/ops` (validation, rendering, auto provenance, PR commit refused). Live on 2026-10-12 against the public scratch repository `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-ops-1791773191` (environment `production` with the founder as required reviewer and a `main`-only branch policy; deleted afterwards): a non-catalog command exited 4; the proposed run waited with no step executed until the reviewer's approval (given through the API with the founder's token, as the test's reviewer), then printed `credential=present github_token=absent oidc=available` and `approved by @rahulnakmol`; a run naming a pull request's catalog commit failed after approval with "the catalog commit is not on the protected default branch". No environment was created on aayudha |
| D-7.1a | WP7.1: `loops/*.toml` → Amp schedules/plugin, Cursor Automations, and an Actions fallback; nightly `status --digest`, weekly `retro`, monthly seat self-review, quarterly `evolve`; all honour `halt` and `[budget]`; schedules in `[time] tz`. Accept: golden outputs per host (Amp schedule JSON, Cursor automation file, Actions cron with the tz-converted expression); the Actions fallback runs `status --digest` on the dogfood repo on schedule and the fake notify sink receives one digest; `halt` makes the next scheduled run exit 4 with no writes | Built ahead of M7 (founder scheduling, 2026-10-12). V7 fixes the plumbing list, so there is no `loops` command. Amp has no schedule file: per its docs (Automations, 2026-10-12) a schedule is set on one thread by an agent through `set_schedule` (`title`, `prompt`, `schedule` as an RFC 5545 rule, `schedule_label`, `run_mode`), one schedule per thread. Cursor has no committable automation file either: automations are created in the editor, at cursor.com/automations, with `/automate`, through its API, or with its Terraform provider (`cursor/cursor` ~> 0.1, resource `cursor_platform_workflow`, `trigger.cron.schedule`); its docs do not state the cron's time zone, and the only statement found is a Cursor staff reply on the forum (2026-06-25) that it is UTC, with an unverified `CRON_TZ=` prefix. GitHub's cron is UTC. `[notify]` holds non-urgent pages until business hours, and a CI run keeps no held pages, so a digest sent overnight would be lost. `retro` and `evolve` are WP7.6 and WP7.7. No billing API reports one personal repository's Actions minutes. aayudha has no `[notify]` channel and no bot App (D1) | A loop is `loops/<name>.toml`: `description`, `command` (an aayu command; refused when it uses `--yes`, a human-only input, or a command gated on a terminal, a human pass, or the merge gate), `cron` (five fields in `[time] tz`: one minute; hours as a list or `*`; days of month or of week, not both; an hourly loop has no day fields), optional `hosts` (`amp`, `cursor`, `actions`; default all), `prompt` (extra work for a host's agent), and `bot` (the command writes as the bot). `internal/loops` renders, `go generate ./...` writes aayudha's own (CI drift-checks them), and `init --gates` writes a product's when it has `loops/`: `.github/workflows/aayudha-loops.yml`, `loops/amp/<name>.json` (the `set_schedule` input: the rule as `DTSTART;TZID=<tz>:20260105T<hhmm>00` plus `RRULE:FREQ=…;BYHOUR;BYMINUTE`, local time, so DST needs nothing; `run_mode` `fresh`; a human asks an Amp thread in the project to set it), and `loops/cursor/aayudha-loops.tf` (one `cursor_platform_workflow` per loop with `git_repo`, `git_branch`, the prompt, and cron triggers, applied with `terraform apply`). For the UTC hosts each local cron becomes one UTC cron per offset the zone uses (sampled on 15 January and 15 July), moving the days of the week when the time crosses midnight UTC; a day-of-month loop that would cross is refused. Every run starts with `aayu hook loop:<name>` (the `hook` plumbing command, no new command): exit 4 while `AAYUDHA_HALT` is set, exit 6 while the `[budget]` breaker's pause file exists or, in Actions, when this workflow's finished runs this month, each rounded up to a whole minute, reach `[budget] ci_minutes_month` (other workflows are not metered); otherwise it reports whether this run is the scheduled one: in Actions only the cron for the zone's current offset (`github.event.schedule`), elsewhere a time within 59 minutes after the local time, so of the two UTC crons exactly one runs each day; in Actions it writes `due=true\|false` to `GITHUB_OUTPUT`. Agents run the command with `AAYU_AGENT=1`, so verbs refuse while paused (exit 6) and while halted (exit 4). Each Actions job also starts with the halt step, here exiting 4, and keeps the `file` channel's outbox as an artifact. Loops: `digest` (`status --digest`, Mon–Fri 09:05: at the opening of business hours rather than nightly), `decisions` (the WP5.6 `decide` sweep, Mon–Fri 09:15, 13:15, 17:15; `bot`, so the Actions job mints the App token and skips with a notice without one), `seat-review` (monthly, Amp and Cursor only, since it needs a model), `retro` and `evolve` (not rendered: n/a until WP7.6 and WP7.7; the workflow lists them). `[notify] channel = "file"` (new) appends each page as a JSON line to `target` (default `.aayudha/state/notify-outbox.jsonl`); aayudha's own config uses it, so its digest lands in the run's `aayudha-notify-digest` artifact: that file is the "fake notify sink" of the acceptance. Tests: `TestUTCCrons` (London, Kolkata across midnight, New York, hourly, the Sydney refusal), `TestDue`, `TestParseRefuses`, `TestGolden` (`internal/loops/testdata/golden`: all three hosts), `TestLoopDigestOnSchedule` (the digest job against the fake GitHub at Monday 09:05 London: the BST cron is due and the GMT one is not, one digest in the outbox, then halt exits 4 at the guard and in the verb with no writes, then the minutes budget and the pause file exit 6), and `TestInitExtras` (`--gates` renders a checkout's loops). Live on 2026-10-12 against the scratch repository `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-loops-1791775332` (the generated workflow installing this branch; deleted afterwards): with `AAYUDHA_HALT` set, a dispatched digest run failed at its halt step with exit code 4 and ran no other step; with it cleared, the guard printed `dispatched by hand` and `status --digest` ran, its page held because 04:30 London is outside business hours, which is why the digest loop runs at 09:05 |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP7.5 and WP7.8 (`tell` and `release`)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-7.5a | WP7.5: `tell docs\|guide\|changelog\|release\|launch\|brand`; tone from `brand.yaml`; claims link only to Features with authoritative packs; UTM tagging; `tell publish` human-identity only, logged; accept: a guide references only proven Features, publish refuses a bot identity | `tell` runs in the host (mode `host`), so the host's model writes the copy and the binary can only brief and check it. Packs belong to stories, not Features, and the plan does not say what makes a Feature proven, what counts as a claim, which links get which UTM values, or where publishing puts the copy: the docs site is WP8.2 and no blog or social integration exists. `grill brand` (WP5.3, #101) was built in parallel | `internal/tell` plus `internal/cli/tell.go`. New registry inputs: `--from` (the host's Markdown draft), `--tag`, and `--campaign`. Without `--from`, `tell <kind>` prints the brief: the voice, tone, and avoided words, the Features that may be claimed, and the UTM tags. A Feature is proven when at least one story or bug under it (at any depth) is done (merged, released, measured, done) and every done one is covered by an authoritative pack: one that maps every Ready criterion, matches its hashes, and whose floors hold. This is the `status` proof-coverage rule (WP2.8), so coverage must be k/k with k > 0. Outside a checkout the ledger cannot be read, so nothing is proven. Tone: `design/brand.yaml`, read with grill brand's own strict parser (`design.ParseBrand`); tell uses `name` and `voice: {tone, avoid}` and leaves the palette and pairs to grill. A missing file, tone, or avoid list falls back to the documented default: a plain, specific, calm voice and an avoid list of hype words (`seamless`, `revolutionary`, `best-in-class`, …). Single or hyphenated words on the avoid list are refused literally; longer entries ("exclamation marks") only guide the host. With `--from`, every link to an issue or pull request of the repository is a claim: `#N`, `owner/name#N`, or any `issues/N` or `pull/N` path (absolute, root-relative, or relative, as GitHub resolves them) that does not name another repository on github.com. Each must be a proven Feature, so stories, bugs, PRs, and unproven Features are refused. Avoided words are refused too, case-insensitively, outside URLs. Any refusal exits 4 and lists the problems by line. Fenced code blocks, delimited as CommonMark renders them (closed only by a run of the opening character at least as long), are neither checked nor tagged. Every other absolute http(s) link gets `utm_source=<repository name>`, `utm_medium=<kind>`, and `utm_campaign=<--campaign, else --tag, else <kind>-<date>>`; a link that already has a `utm_` parameter keeps the author's. The checked copy is written to `.aayudha/tell/<kind>/<campaign>.md`. `--publish` (human-only; never exposed over MCP) is refused, with exit 4 and no writes (not even the local copy), for an agent lane (`AAYU_AGENT=1`), in CI (`--ci`), and when `GET /user` for the human token reports a bot (`type: Bot`, a `[bot]` login, or `[identity] bot_logins`). A human's publish is one `tell.publish` action in the log. It lands `tell/<kind>/<campaign>.md` and a `.json` publication record (SHA-256, cited Features, UTM, who, and when) on the ledger. For `release` with `--tag`, it also replaces tell's `<!-- aayudha:tell -->` block in that tag's GitHub Release notes; a tag without a GitHub Release, such as `rolling-*`, is left alone. `tell.ReleaseNotes` writes deterministic notes for `release` (WP7.8): shipped stories grouped under their proven Feature, linking only the Feature, with the rest counted and not described. Tests: `TestTellGuideCitesOnlyProvenFeatures` and `TestTellPublishHumanOnly` (the thin slice shipped end to end against the fake GitHub and a local ledger), `TestLoadBrand`, `TestTag`, `TestCheckFences`, `TestCheckRefs`, and `TestReleaseNotes` |
| D-7.8a | WP7.8: `release`: the promotion ladder for a Release issue; artifact identity; stamps included stories `released`; `tell release`; shipping evidence; triggers the `measure` schedule; accept: a dogfood rolling snapshot with stamped stories | D-0.4a removes the human approval on this repository but keeps the gates and conventions, and CONTRIBUTING.md already fixes the procedure. The registry gives `release` the human identity and a terminal confirmation. WP stories here merge through `gh pr merge --admin` (solo-mode bypass), not `aayu ship`, so they keep `status:ready`. Only a story's PR links it to the commit that shipped it. The plan does not say whether "included" means what the Release lists or what the promoted range ships, what identifies a rolling snapshot (it has no GitHub Release, since `.goreleaser.yaml` ignores `rolling-*` tags, #77), or what the `measure` schedule is: loops (WP7.1) are in review, and `measure` needs exports that a human supplies. Rulesets on a personal account's private repository need GitHub Pro | `aayu release <release> --to rolling\|main` (`internal/cli/release.go`) drives CONTRIBUTING.md's procedure and nothing else. It is refused for agent lanes, in CI, and for a bot account (WP7.5's checks). It needs a checkout and a yes on a terminal or `--yes` from the human (on this repository, the D-0.4a path for the maintainer's credentials). The Release must pass Ready, be open, and, for main, have a `vX.Y.Z` Version that is not yet tagged. The ladder rule: main needs this Release's rolling rung (`releases/<n>/rolling.json` on the ledger) and that commit must still be on rolling. Branch names come from `[branches]`. The range is `git rev-list --no-merges origin/<target>..origin/<source>`. Its stories are those named, through `Refs #n`, an `Aayudha-Story` trailer, or a `story/<n>-` branch, by the merged PRs into dev that GitHub links to each commit. The Release's `includes` links expand a Feature to its stories and bugs. Each listed item must be in the range or already released, measured, done, or cancelled; anything else refuses (exit 4) before anything is written. Every story the range ships is stamped, listed or not, because the artifact holds it: those not yet released become `status:released` (from `ready` or `merged` alike). Ones the Release did not list are reported. `--dry-run` reports all of this and writes nothing. Then: the candidate `promote/rolling-<ts>` (or `promote/main-<v>`) is cut from origin/target in a temporary worktree with `git merge --no-ff --signoff -m "Rolling snapshot: <ts>"` (or `"Release: <v>"`) and pushed. For rolling, `evals.yml` runs on the source ref when the repository has it, and a red run stops the promotion. CONTRIBUTING's `gh run watch` there has no `--exit-status`, because `evals-gate` reads its results; `release` stops earlier, with the same outcome. `ci.yml` is dispatched on the candidate with `gate=rolling\|stable`, through `return_run_details`. The run must be on the candidate's SHA, and a failed run has its failed jobs re-run once (the transient `release-snapshot` signing timeouts). A gate that still fails exits 4 and promotes nothing. That exact commit is pushed to the target as a fast-forward and tagged: lightweight `rolling-<date>`, or `rolling-<date>.<n>` for the n-th snapshot of the day, or annotated `vX.Y.Z` (`Release vX.Y.Z: <title>`). For main, the binary waits for the tag's `release.yml` run and re-runs it once. A run still red after the bookkeeping exits 5 with the `gh run rerun` hint. Any failure after the target moved (the tag, the tag's run, stamping, notes, records, or evidence) exits 5. The message names the commit now on the target and the steps left; they have no resume yet and are finished by hand. The candidate branch is deleted on every exit. Every push, dispatch, and re-run is an action in the log. Artifact identity: the commit, tree, and source SHA; the gate's run artifacts (name and digest) for rolling, and the GitHub Release assets (name, `sha256:` digest, size) for main.
