A tag with no GitHub Release is noted; any other lookup failure is a failure after landing. `tell release` uses WP7.5's `ReleaseNotes`, checks them with `tell.Check`, and publishes them under the human account to `tell/release/<tag>.md` on the ledger and, for `v*`, to the GitHub Release. Records: `releases/<n>/<target>.json` on the ledger. The Release gets `status:released` and a Shipping evidence entry per rung (the runs, artifacts, stories, notes, and measure due dates); the main rung closes it. Measure schedule: each Goal or Initiative above a stamped story gets `measure/due/<n>/<tag>.json` on the ledger (one per release, so a later release never erases or postpones an earlier obligation), due 28 days after the release (measure's default window), for the loops (WP7.1) and the human to run `aayu measure` on. The release notes are built and checked before anything lands, so a story title with an avoided word refuses the promotion rather than its bookkeeping. Tests: `TestReleaseLadder` covers the fake GitHub and a local remote: the refusals, the dry run, a gate failing twice, the re-run, stamping (an abandoned PR holding the same commit serves nothing), notes, records, a second same-day snapshot that ships an unlisted story without `evals.yml`, main refused while the snapshot is gone from rolling, main with `release.yml` re-run and release assets, and a version that already shipped. `TestReleaseFailsClosed` injects GitHub failures: before landing nothing is promoted, and after landing the exit is 5 naming what landed. `TestReleaseRefusesBeforeLanding` covers an avoided word in the notes, a conflicting candidate, an unreadable brand, red evals, and a gate run on another commit; a tag the remote refuses after rolling moved is in `TestReleaseFailsClosed`. `TestReleaseRedTagWorkflow` covers a `release.yml` still red after its re-run (exit 5 with the `gh run rerun` command) and a product without one. `TestReleaseLive` (AAYU_LIVE=1) runs on an `aayu-scratch-*` repository created with `AAYU_LIVE_ADMIN_TOKEN`, made public so it can carry aayudha's `rolling-main-promotion` and `release-tags` rulesets, and deleted. It has stand-in `ci.yml`, `evals.yml`, and `release.yml` workflows, a Goal > Feature > Story tree, and the story's PR squash-merged into dev. The ladder ran under the human's fine-grained token through the admin-only rulesets: rolling in about 50 s, then main in about 45 s, with the `checksums.txt` asset digest recorded (passed on 2026-10-12, last on the final head of #110). The repository's own promotions are left to the orchestrator |
| D-7.8b | WP7.8: `release` stamps the stories it ships `released` | Found running the first `aayu release` on aayudha (Release #147). `release` read only the first `Refs #n` of a merged PR, so #97 (`Refs #64`, `Refs #65`) shipped #64 but not #65; and it stamped any story a PR in the range referenced, including stories that never merged (the WP8.1 umbrella #70, referenced by PR #142 alongside story #130), which would claim undone work | `release` reads every `Refs`/`closes` line of a merged PR (#146). A referenced story that is not `status:merged` and not listed by the Release is reported as "referenced but not merged" and not stamped; it adds no measure obligation. Listed stories still must be in the range or already released |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP5.3–WP5.5 (grill, slice and plan, build and ship)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| D-5.3a | WP5.3: question sets per type in `references/<type>.md`; baseline required before a target on Goal/Initiative; refuses Ready without a validator pass; writes via `actions` under the human's identity when a human runs it, the bot otherwise; `grill brand` → `design/brand.yaml` with an AA palette check; bubbletea TUI for the CLI, prompt for MCP/skill; untrusted wrapping of pasted material; accept: ten tier-1 fixtures end in an issue passing `ready` 10/10, tier 2 on `grill goal` and `grill story` meets the WP3.4 bar with the results committed as the baseline, a Goal with no reachable baseline is refused Ready naming the metric | Feedback and Incident are intake: `ready` refuses them by design (WP2.7, WP2.9), so no Feedback or Incident issue can pass `ready`. The plan names no brand file format, no AA thresholds per use, no baseline window, and no rule for metrics no adapter serves (WP7.2's snapshot metrics). bubbletea v2.0.10 and later need Go 1.26 (D5). CI cannot push a results file to `dev` (D-3.4a). Two more tier-2 cases do not fit the 12 USD cap | `grill <type>` takes all ten types plus `brand` (new enum value). A human run writes with the human token, untagged; an agent lane (`AAYU_AGENT=1`, MCP) writes under the bot, or the tagged human fallback (WP1.4). New issues carry `type:<type>` and `status:planned`, or `status:backlog` for intake, as the forms file them. Goal and Initiative: a Metric `<source>:<metric> [filter]` (the `measure` syntax) is fetched through its WP5.2 adapter over the 28 whole UTC days before today and written into the Baseline section (value first, then metric, population, half-open window, fetch time, and the source query in a fence), replacing what the draft held; when the adapter is missing, unreachable, or write-scoped the draft fails Ready (exit 4, nothing written) with `baseline: no reachable baseline for metric "<metric>" (a Target needs a reproducible baseline first): <adapter error naming [integrations.<name>]>`. A snapshot metric (`activation`, …) must already hold its measured value in Baseline, else the same refusal. Without `--title`/`--body`, a human on a terminal gets the bubbletea TUI (`charm.land/bubbletea/v2` v2.0.9): one question per field in schema order with the reference's questions as hints, parent and labels last, a review screen running the same validation that jumps to the first problem and creates only a passing draft; pasted text only becomes answer text. MCP: the `grill` prompt serves every reference and takes an optional `material` argument, wrapped as `<untrusted source="pasted">`. `grill brand --body <yaml>` parses `design/brand.yaml` strictly (`version`, `name`, `voice {tone, avoid}`, `palette {name: "#rrggbb"}`, `pairs [{fg, bg, use}]`) and checks every pair's WCAG contrast, floored to two decimals, against 4.5:1 for `text` and 3:1 for `large-text` and `ui` (SC 1.4.3, 1.4.11); any failing pair or missing text pair refuses (exit 4, nothing written); `--create` writes the file in the checkout for a PR. Tier 1: `evals/cases/grill/<type>-…` for all ten types record `grill <type> … --create`, then `ready 21 --check` for the eight non-intake types; for Feedback and Incident the run ends at the created issue, and the case's Ready check is the schema validation of the recorded item (`TestTier1GrillEveryType`). Tier 2: `grill/story-empty-claims` and `grill/goal-first-claim` carry rubrics and send their question set through `[tier2] references` (WP7.3's key); `max_usd` is 15.0. The results of the first passing `evals.yml` run on `dev` are committed as `evals/results/<sha>.json` in the next PR |
| D-5.3b | WP5.3 tier 2: `grill goal` and `grill story` meet the WP3.4 bar | Run 38204111801 on 5752ac6 (evals.yml, push to dev): `grill/goal-first-claim` scored 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 (pass^5 1, mean 5.0); `grill/story-empty-claims` scored 1 five times, every run the deterministic floor "fails Ready: Effort is required at Ready": the skill's "Drafting without a human" rule sent every decision the request leaves open to `open_questions`, effort included. The run took about 26 of the job's 30 minutes with seven cases, so the slice and plan cases of WP5.4 do not fit, and the brief cases (D-7.3b) fail the suite bar, so the run's verdict cannot stand for one WP's cases | The one fix: the grill skill and `references/story.md` say effort is the skill's estimate of size, required at Ready, never an open question (priority only when given). `evals.yml` gains a dispatch input `cases` (a regular expression over case IDs, passed to `eval -tier 2 -cases`) and a 60-minute timeout; the WP5.3 and WP5.4 bar is read from a dispatch on dev with `cases` = `^(grill\|slice\|plan)/`, whose results file is committed as the baseline. Outcome: run 38206893151 on bf0a837 (after the fix, push to dev) scored `grill/story-empty-claims` 5, 2, 5, 2, 5 and `grill/goal-first-claim` 5×5; the dispatched run 38207978439 on 967054b (`cases` `^(grill\|slice\|plan)/`, 3.84 USD) scored the story 5, 5, 5, 5, 2 (mean 4.4, pass^5 0) and the goal 5×5 (mean 5.0, pass^5 1). Every story run now passes Ready; the low scores are the judge finding a requirement the request does not state (a screen-reader criterion, unchanged rendering for claimants with claims). `grill story` therefore misses the per-case pass^5 bar (WP5.3's pair: pass^5 0.5, mean 4.7). Per the founder's instruction (one fix, no loop) it is left for the founder: a scope guard like the stub's (D-3.4c) or an `evolve` case is the next step. `evals/results/967054b….json` is committed as the record of that run; it is not a passing baseline for `grill story` |
| D-5.3c | WP5.3 tier 2 for `grill story` (D-5.3b left it below the bar) | Founder decision, 2026-10-12: the extra screen-reader criterion is the stub's defect class (D-3.4c), so it gets the same treatment, not another dispatch | The grill skill and `references/story.md` say a Completion Goal states only what the request or its parent asks for: accessibility, performance, and security are floors `verify` enforces on every story and are not criteria unless requested, nor are checks for users the request does not mention. `grill/story-empty-claims` gains five `scope` rules (screen reader, keyboard or focus, other accessibility, performance budgets, claimants who already have claims), checked in tier 1 and before the judge in tier 2; `TestGrillStoryScope` keeps both sides (the recorded criteria and a reworded requested one pass, each unrequested kind fails) |
| D-5.4a | WP5.4: `slice` makes XS-L stories with criteria, deps, and a footprint computed from the Feature's expected paths via `LAYERS.md` globs and confirmed by the plan, and closes an XL as "split into"; `plan` has checkpoints per layer, contracts, tests, instrumentation, rollback, with an EM plan-time review for `autonomy.never` layers recorded in the pack; accept: evals on a 3-layer and a 6-layer (L07 migration) Feature give stories ≤ L passing `ready` (tier 1, 100 %), each plan one checkpoint per footprint layer and a rollback for any L07 checkpoint, tier 2 at the WP3.4 bar, an XL input returned as ≥ 2 stories with the XL closed "split into" | Both verbs run in the host (mode `host`), so the binary needs the drafts as input. The Story schema has no footprint field, and expected paths are globs, not changed paths. A Feature is a container: closing it would orphan its stories. The pack is written by the merge gate before `ship`, and nothing yet carries a plan into it. The eval scratch repository has no `LAYERS.md`, and the WP3.4 tier-1 replay cannot reach files the recorded run wrote | `slice <feature> --draft <file> [--create]` and `plan <story> --draft <file> [--create] [--approve]` (new registry inputs; `--create` pinned off over MCP, `--approve` human-only); without `--draft` each prints what to work within (the Feature's expected paths and footprint; the story's footprint, criteria, and never layers). `internal/planning` holds the deterministic checks. A story's footprint is held in `layer:Lxx` labels: `slice` computes it from the story's `paths` (globs inside the Feature's Expected paths) by glob overlap with the `LAYERS.md` globs (the registry's layer table when the checkout has none, as `status --streak` does). `slice` refuses (exit 4, nothing written) a Feature without Expected paths, a story that fails Ready under the Feature (so XL, unmapped criteria, missing effort), paths outside the Feature's, a Feature layer no story covers, `depends_on` that is out of range or cyclic, and a hand-written Links field; `--create` writes the stories in dependency order as sub-issues with `status:planned`, their labels, and `depends-on #N` links. An XL input is a Story labelled `effort:XL` (a Feature is always sliced, never closed): it needs at least two stories, which go under its Feature, and it is closed as not planned with `status:cancelled` and a comment "Split into #a, #b". `plan` needs a story or bug that passes Ready and refuses a second checkpoint for a layer, a footprint layer without one, one outside the footprint (re-slice), a checkpoint without tests, an L07 checkpoint without a rollback, and a criterion no checkpoint covers; a story without layer labels takes the plan's layers as its footprint (written as labels). `--create` writes `specs/<story>/plan.json` to the ledger (the plan, the body hash it was made for, and `em_review {required, layers}` when the footprint meets `[autonomy] never`); `plan --approve` by the EM seat's paired human, else the `[autonomy] approver`, else the one human in solo mode (recorded as `solo`) adds `approved_by`, `approved_at`, `as`; `ship` (WP5.5) reads it and records it in the ship record next to the authoritative pack. Evals: `slice/grill-followups-3-layer` (L02, L03, L05), `slice/proof-retention-6-layer` (L02, L03, L04, L06, L07, L09, with the config migration), `slice/xl-story`, `plan/config-migration` (L07 with rollback), `plan/purge-kind-flag` (L02, L03); recorded calls may name the case's files as `{case}/…`, and the tier-1 fake answers label writes with a list. Tier 2: `[tier2] draft = "slice"\|"plan"` and `issue` run the drafts through the same checks against the case's fixture before the judge (`slice/proof-retention-6-layer`, `plan/config-migration`); `max_usd` is 18.0 |
| D-5.4b | WP5.4 tier 2 meets the WP3.4 bar | Run 38204807563 on a73790a (evals.yml, push to dev, all nine cases): `plan/config-migration` scored 5, 5, 5, 5, 5; `slice/proof-retention-6-layer` scored 2, 2, 4, 5, 2: the judge found every Feature requirement covered but three drafts required behaviour the Feature does not name (a `--dry-run` mode, repeatable flags, API retries, a manual workflow trigger, per-kind counts) | The one fix: the slice skill gets a scope step: every criterion restates something the Feature asks for; extra flags, retries, limits, triggers, reports, and outputs go to `open_questions`; fixture values in a criterion's state stay allowed. The WP5.3 and WP5.4 bar is read from the evals.yml run on dev after this fix. Outcome: the dispatched run 38207978439 on 967054b scored `slice/proof-retention-6-layer` 5, 5, 5, 5, 4 (mean 4.8, pass^5 1) and `plan/config-migration` 5×5 (mean 5.0, pass^5 1): WP5.4's tier 2 meets the WP3.4 bar; the results file `evals/results/967054b….json` is committed as its baseline |
| D-5.4c | WP5.4: `plan` evals meet the WP3.4 bar | The promotion-candidate tier-2 run 38230815198 scored plan/config-migration 5 2 5 5 5 (pass^5 0): one plan also rejected legacy configs and added a backup-free downgrade, behaviour the story does not ask for. The scope guard (D-3.4c) applied only to work items and brief prose | `plan`'s skill forbids unrequested behaviour (downgrades, refusing older inputs, new flags) and routes such choices to `open_questions`; a rollback restores, never drops data. Case scope rules now also run on `slice`/`plan` drafts, open questions excepted (TestPlanScope) |
| D-5.5a | WP5.5: `build` refuses unless Ready and the body hash equals the snapshot; per-story lease (assignee + lock comment with TTL); worktree `.aayudha/worktrees/<id>` from `origin/dev`; `Aayudha-Checkpoint: n/N` commits; replace-in-place progress comment and checkpoint record on `ledger`; `verify` per checkpoint; `[autonomy] budget {wall, cost, attempts}` → `status:blocked` + page on breach or a second failure on a never layer; `--resume` from the last trailer; retries 429/5xx with jitter. `ship`: green gate; human pass = approving review from the seat's human (solo → `[autonomy] approver`), `type != Bot`; never layers need CODEOWNER approval regardless of streak; approval ID and streak state written to the pack; squash-merge; Complete section; `merged`; cleanup. `gc` prunes stale worktrees and leases. Accept: dogfood story Ready → merged; a bot review is rejected as a pass; a stale lease is reclaimed by `gc` | The plan names no TTL, no way to find leased stories, and no home for the checkpoint record. The binary never sees the host model's spend. Floors whose tools are absent fail by rule 7, and most laptops lack the pinned scanners. A POST or PATCH that answered 5xx may have landed. The pack is the merge gate's verified artifact, written before `ship` runs. aayudha has no `[autonomy] approver` and one human, so GitHub refuses the only possible approval (D2) | Lease: the lock comment `<!-- aayudha:lease holder=<login>@<host> expires=<UTC> -->`, the holder's login as assignee (an App's `[bot]` login cannot be assigned), and the new label `aayudha:leased` so `gc` and `doctor` find leases; `LeaseTTL` is 4 h, renewed by every build run; a live lease of another holder refuses the build (exit 4); an expired one is taken over. A story branch already on origin without a local worktree needs `--resume`, which cuts the worktree from that branch and reports its last `Aayudha-Checkpoint` trailer. N is the plan's checkpoint count when `specs/<n>/plan.json` is on the ledger (WP5.4), else the larger of k and the change's layer count; a retry of a checkpoint that missed keeps its k and adds `Aayudha-Attempt: <a>`. Each checkpoint runs `aayu verify` in the worktree; a floor that fails only because its tool is not installed there is reported but does not count (the merge gate runs the pinned tools and stays authoritative). `build/<n>/state.json` on the ledger holds the start time, checkpoints with their verify result, failed attempts, failures per layer, and any block; the `<!-- aayudha:progress -->` comment shows the same with the budget. Budget: wall time since the first run, spend from the story's actions-log costs plus the host's `AAYU_COST_USD`, and failed checkpoint attempts; a breach, or a second miss on an `[autonomy] never` layer, sets `status:blocked`, pages through `[notify]`, and exits 4; a human's `aayu ready` restarts the budget. The GitHub transport retries a 5xx with exponential backoff from 1 s only where repeating cannot write twice (GET, HEAD, PUT, DELETE, GraphQL queries), and adds up to 25 % random jitter to every wait, the rate-limit waits included. `ship`: the human pass must come from the Engineering Manager seat's paired human, else the `[autonomy] approver`, else any human who is not the author (bots and `[identity] bot_logins` never count); when the pack's footprint meets `[autonomy] never`, the approver must also be a CODEOWNER (base-branch CODEOWNERS) of every changed path in those layers, whatever the streak, and the EM's plan-time review must be approved on the ledger. The streak is computed at ship time from merged-PR history (WP5.7); a footprint the streak makes autonomous with no never layer ships without a review (`human_pass.kind = autonomous`). The approval ID, the pass rule, the never layers, the plan review, and the streak state go into `ship.json` beside the authoritative pack, not into `pack.json`, which stays as the gate wrote and verified it. `ship` releases the lease. `--solo-bypass` stays the human-only path (D-M4.5a). `gc` (human identity) reclaims expired leases, leases of closed stories, and stray labels, and removes the worktree and local branch of a closed story when nothing in it is uncommitted or unpushed (else keeps it, saying why); `doctor`'s `leases` check lists stale leases |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP4.8b and WP5.5b (review quality, delegation)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. Research and sources:
[docs/research/agent-delegation-and-review.md](research/agent-delegation-and-review.md).

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-4.8b | WP4.8: `inspect` sends the diff, plan, pack, and criteria to one cross-family reviewer and posts one comment | Founder request (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): review every agent-authored PR better, borrowing from Alibaba's open review skill and Greptile, with automatic evaluation plus a peer review, measured by precision and recall on seeded defects. Every published reviewer adds a verify or filter stage after generation; self-rated confidence alone filtered poorly (research §1) | WP4.8b, added after WP4.8 |
| S-5.5b | The plan has no way to hand a story to another agent; `build` assumes the host's own agent writes the change | Founder request (2026-10-15): from Claude Code or Amp, send a Ready story to the Codex, Cursor, Claude Code, or Amp cloud agent with slash commands, review its PR, and close the loop; cloud by default, local fallback in an aayu worktree; one brief; generated environments; worktrees for every code-changing verb | WP5.5b, added after WP5.5 |
| D-5.5b.1 | The request names `aayu delegate <story> --to <agent>` and prefers extending `build` if that keeps the registry clean | Delegation needs exactly `build`'s gates (Ready and body hash, lease, budget, halt), its worktree, branch, commit, and PR rules, its checkpoint record, and its RACI row (AI Native Engineer). A `delegate` verb would duplicate all of them and add a 33rd verb | `build` gains `--delegate <codex\|cursor\|claude\|amp\|auto>` and `--local`; the slash commands give the short form. No new verb |
| D-4.8b.1 | WP4.8b: retrieve the changed symbols, their callers, related tests, and the `LAYERS.md` footprint | The merge gate's `aayudha/inspect` job holds the model key and must never run or check out PR code (D-4.8a); a whole-repository symbol graph needs every file of the head | `inspect.BuildContext` reads the head through the contents API at its SHA (`headSource`): each changed directory is an area; Go files are parsed (never built) for the changed declarations, their call sites in the same package, and the `Test*`/`Fuzz*`/`Benchmark*`/`Example*` functions that name them; other languages use the diff's hunk headers and sibling test files. At most 40 files and 200 kB each are read, 40 kB of context reaches the prompt, and a new file's source is not repeated (the diff shows it). Each area's plan lists files, layers, changed symbols, callers, related tests, whether a test changed, generated files, and risks: one per touched layer with a known risk (L02, L04, L06, L07, L09) and one per pattern over the added lines (concurrency, external input, secrets, destructive operations, ignored errors, boundaries), plus "no test changed in this area". Identifiers in the plan are cut to identifier characters; the retrieved code is an `<untrusted source="code-context">` block. Callers outside the package are not found (later: a repository index) |
| D-4.8b.2 | WP4.8b: findings with severity, evidence (file:line), suggested fix; noise filtering (confidence threshold, de-duplication, style nits the linters own); learning from dismissed findings | Model-chosen line numbers drift; self-rated confidence filtered poorly on its own in every published reviewer (research §1); aayudha has no vector store; in solo mode every agent (Cursor and Amp included) writes under the founder's account, so a 👎 reaction or a reply cannot be told apart from the builder's own | Findings v2 carry `category`, `quote`, `fix`, and `confidence`; aayu places each by its quote (an added line first), keeps the model's line only when it is in a hunk, else posts it in the summary only (line 0). `Filter.Clean`: a file outside the diff is dropped; `style`, and P2/P3 titles naming typos, formatting, imports, or naming, are linter-owned; files matching `[inspect] generated` or with a `Code generated … DO NOT EDIT` header are not reviewed; P2/P3 below `[inspect] min_confidence` (0.5) are dropped, P0/P1 never; P3 nits are counted, not posted; duplicates (same file, within 3 lines, title-and-detail word overlap ≥ 0.35, or the same fingerprint) merge keeping the highest severity. Dismissal is human-only: `inspect <pr> --dismiss <id> --reason …` (refused for agents, hidden from MCP) appends to `inspect/dismissed.jsonl` on the ledger; a finding is suppressed when it matches one dismissal's fingerprint, or resembles (word overlap ≥ 0.6, same file or directory) `[inspect] suppress_after` (2) distinct dismissals, never a P0 in security, data, or concurrency. A fix whose every P0–P2 finding was refuted or dismissed, with no unmet criterion, is lifted to merge with a note; the floors still downgrade it (rule 7). The comment lists what was not posted and why, and a collapsed review plan |
| D-4.8b.3 | WP4.8b: peer mode, two different-family reviewers cross-check before posting; general mode, one reviewer | The registry's review lane is one model; a delegated Codex PR is built by the review lane's own family (OpenAI) | `[inspect] mode` (general) and `delegated_mode` (general since D-4.8b.7, for PRs with a `delegate/<n>/state.json`), or `inspect --mode`. Reviewer A is the review lane's model; when its family equals the builder's and it is a registry default, the first of `verbs.PeerReviewCandidates` (GPT-6.1 Sol, Grok 4.7, Gemini 3.1 Pro, Claude Opus 5.5) of another family reviews instead, with a note (a configured one is refused). Reviewer B is `[inspect] peer_model` or the first candidate whose family differs from both. Each reviews the same input; each one's single-source findings go to the other with `VerifySystem`, an asymmetric "prove it wrong" check (after OpenCodeReview's filter, as an idea: refute only what the diff contradicts, never for low importance); refuted findings are dropped, except a P0 in a protected category, which is posted as disputed. Dispositions combine to the worse; a criterion is unmet when either says so. When B fails, the review falls back to A in general mode with a note. The builder's family for a delegated PR comes from the delegation state on the ledger |
| D-4.8b.4 | WP4.8b: posts structured, de-duplicated comments; requests fixes | A bot or the founder's account cannot request changes on the founder's own PRs (D2), and receivers act on review comments | One PR review per run with event COMMENT (never APPROVE or REQUEST_CHANGES) and one inline comment per new P0–P2 finding placed on a line, each with its ID marker, fix, and any dispute; the IDs posted go into inspect's state so a later cycle posts only new ones. The summary comment and its state stay replace-in-place. The summary speaks as the Engineering Manager twin (Kiran, `seats/engineering-manager/persona.toml`) with the evidence rule; findings stay in the schema |
| D-4.8b.5 | WP4.8b: evaluate with seeded-defect PRs, precision and recall, general vs peer, before and after, through the eval harness | The harness scores skills with a rubric judge; a reviewer is scored against known defects, and the review calls cost about 70 per run of the set | `[review]` cases in `evals/cases/inspect/`: twelve synthetic PRs (nine with planted defects of eight kinds, ten defects in all; three clean controls), generated as real `git diff` output, with the head's files stored as `<path>.txt` so this repository's build, linters, and scanners never compile or flag them. Tier 1 checks each case's construction (every defect on added lines). Tier 2 runs each case k = 2 times in three variants: `legacy` (the 0.1 prompt and schema, kept in `internal/evals/review.go`), `general`, and `peer`, four cases at a time, and scores what each would post: a finding within 3 lines of an unmatched defect in its file is a true positive, a second one a duplicate (false positive), any other a false positive; a run scores 1 + 4·F1 (a clean control 5 with no false positive, else 1). The results file gains per-variant precision, recall, F1, and clean-control false positives. The `review` group is advisory (never gates promotion) and runs only on `workflow_dispatch` (new group keys `k` and `events`; `cmd/eval` reads the workflow's `EVENT`), so `evals.yml` needs no change |
| D-5.5b.2 | WP5.5b: hand the receiving agent the Ready snapshot, plan, and evidence map; prefer the most portable trigger with a per-host adapter; the PR must reference the story | The receivers differ in transport: Cursor has a REST API that opens the PR; Amp's CLI starts an orb thread; claude-code-action answers an `@claude` comment but does not open PRs; Codex cloud has only the experimental `codex cloud exec` and a "Create PR" click (research §5). PRs from Cursor and Amp are opened as the human, so authorship identifies nothing | `internal/delegate`: one `Brief` for every receiver (the Ready snapshot's criteria from the ledger, the story body quoted as data, the plan's checkpoints, an evidence-map skeleton filled with the plan's tests, branch and PR rules with `Refs #n`, Conventional Commits and DCO, `[delegate] checks`, CODEOWNERS paths, the cycle cap, AGENTS.md abridged), passed through the actions log's redaction and a credential-shape filter; its SHA-256 is recorded. Adapters: `CursorAPI` (`POST /v1/agents` with `autoCreatePR`, `GET …/runs/{id}`, `POST …/runs`), `AmpCLI` (`amp -ox --project`, `amp threads continue`), `ClaudeMention` (an `@claude` comment on the story, then on the PR; aayu opens the PR from the pushed `claude/issue-<n>-…` branch), `CodexCloud` (`codex cloud exec`, `status`, and `diff`; aayu applies the returned diff in `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-codex` and opens the PR through build's checkpoint, verify, and push). The PR is found by its PR URL, a `Refs #n` body or story branch, or the receiver's branch. Fixtures in `internal/delegate/testdata/` are synthetic, in the documented shapes; `AAYU_LIVE=1` tests skip without credentials |
| D-5.5b.3 | WP5.5b: the review loop ends at merge-ready or the cap; the receiving agent addresses the findings | inspect runs in the merge gate, not in `build`; there are no webhooks from Cursor v1, Amp, or Codex; inspect stops calling the model after two fix cycles | Each later `build <n> --delegate <r>` syncs: the receiver's status, the PR, and inspect's state from its PR comment. On `fix` for the current head it sends one follow-up per head (`delegate.FollowUpMessage`: the finding IDs, places, and suggested fixes) through the adapter, or, locally, runs the CLI on it in the worktree and pushes a checkpoint; on `merge` it reports merge-ready and names `aayu ship <pr>`; on `rebuild`, or when the head moved after inspect's cap, it pages a human and exits 4. The hand-off, follow-ups, and local runs are actions (`delegate.handoff`, `delegate.follow-up`, `delegate.local-run`); `delegate/<n>/state.json` and a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:delegate -->` comment on the story record every step. The sync runs when the founder's host (or `/codex` … `/amp`) asks; a scheduled loop is later |
| D-5.5b.4 | WP5.5b: cloud by default, local fallback in an aayu worktree with a scrubbed environment and no merge rights | A local CLI runs as the user, whose HOME holds git and gh credentials | `--local`, or a receiver whose cloud agent is not connected (no Cursor key, no `codex` with `[delegate] codex_env`, no claude-code-action workflow, no `amp` with `AMP_API_KEY`), runs `codex exec --sandbox workspace-write`, `cursor-agent -p --force`, `claude -p --permission-mode acceptEdits`, or `amp -x` in `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-<agent>`, under `[delegate] local_timeout_minutes`. Its environment keeps the toolchain variables and the receiver's own key only (no `GITHUB_*`, `GH_*`, `AAYU_*`, or other secret-named variable), with an empty `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, no git credential helper, and `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=false`, so it cannot push; aayu commits (`Aayudha-Delegate: <r>`), verifies, pushes, and opens the PR. Receivers in the cloud push through their own GitHub Apps; none can merge: `dev`'s rules and `ship`'s human pass hold |
| D-5.5b.5 | WP5.5b: environments generated from one source and kept in sync; a doctor check per receiver | Codex cloud environments are defined only in its UI; Cursor reads `.cursor/environment.json` from the default branch; Amp reads `.agents/setup`; claude-code-action runs in the repository's workflow (research §6) | `.agents/setup` is the source. `aayu doctor --fix` writes `.cursor/environment.json` (`install: bash .agents/setup`; other keys kept), committed here for `dev`. The `delegate-env` check reports, per receiver: source executable and its Go pin not older than `go.mod`; Amp native; Cursor drift; Codex manual (the exact Install script, internet preset, and `[delegate] codex_env`); Claude, whether a claude-code-action workflow runs `.agents/setup` first. aayu writes no `.github/` file for it. A repository without `.agents/setup` passes with "not set up" |
| D-5.5b.6 | WP5.5b: worktrees for everything that changes code, a view of parallel work, `gc` cleanup | `build` and `evolve` already cut worktrees; `polish` asked the human to check the PR head out in the current checkout; nothing listed work in flight | Audit: `build` (`.aayudha/worktrees/<n>`), `evolve` (`evolve-<skill>-<n>`), and the delegate local mode (`<n>-<agent>`) cut worktrees; `polish` off the PR head now prepares `.aayudha/worktrees/pr-<n>` on the PR's branch and says to apply the critique there (the critique directory is shared by the clone's worktrees); `review` writes no code. `aayu status --work` lists every aayu worktree (story, agent, branch, PR, age, uncommitted or unpushed work) and every open cloud delegation from the ledger. `gc` removes `<n>-<agent>` and `pr-<n>` worktrees of closed stories and PRs, as it does `<n>` |
| D-5.5b.7 | WP5.5b: pick the cheapest adequate receiver for the story's size and say why; slash commands through the host plugins | Prices change often, and a story's size is its Effort label | `--delegate auto`: XS and S go to the first connected of Codex (plan allowance, no VM charge), Claude Code, Amp, Cursor; M and L to Cursor (follow-up runs), Amp (threads), Claude Code, Codex; with none connected, the first local CLI; the reason (with the cost note) is in the result and the story comment. `/codex`, `/cursor`, `/claude`, `/amp` are `hosts/commands/*.md`, which the Claude Code, Codex, and Amp plugins (WP3.2b) load; each checks Ready (or takes a goal through `grill story` and `ready`), delegates, syncs on request, and stops at merge-ready or the cap, never running `ship` |
| D-4.8b.6 | WP4.8b accept: precision and recall for general vs peer mode, before and after, on a dispatched tier-2 run | Run 38690306973 (`evals.yml`, dispatch, group `review`, dev at 8dbe384, k = 2, 18.30 USD charged): ten planted defects and three clean controls, 24 runs per variant. Before (the 0.1 prompt): precision 0.55, recall 0.90, F1 0.68 (TP 18, FP 15, of which 5 on clean controls). General mode: precision 0.73, recall 0.95, F1 0.83 (TP 19, FP 7, of which 4 on clean controls). Peer mode: precision 0.46, recall 1.00, F1 0.63 (TP 18, FP 21, of which 2 on clean controls; 3 runs errored on gateway 524s from grok-4.7). Peer finds every planted defect and the fewest false positives on clean code, but the second reviewer (grok-4.7) raises extra single-source findings that the asymmetric check rarely refutes, so precision falls, at about five times general mode's cost. Every variant flags `clean-retry-backoff` twice | Reported as measured; the group stays advisory. General mode is the better default for every PR. Delegated PRs (`[inspect] delegated_mode`) also default to general since D-4.8b.7 (founder decision); peer stays available through `inspect --mode peer`. Follow-ups: a peer threshold (post a single-source finding of B only at P0–P1 or confidence ≥ 0.8), and a look at `clean-retry-backoff`'s two findings in case the control is ambiguous |
| D-4.8b.7 | WP4.8b/WP5.5b: delegated PRs reviewed in peer mode by default (`[inspect] delegated_mode = "peer"`) | The reviewer eval (run 38690306973, D-4.8b.6) measured general mode's precision at 0.73 against peer's 0.46, with recall 0.95 against 1.00, at about a fifth of peer's cost | Founder decision, 2026-10-15: `[inspect] delegated_mode = "general"` in `internal/config/defaults.toml`, so delegated PRs get one reviewer of a family different from the builder's, like every other PR. Peer stays available per run with `inspect --mode peer` or per repository with `delegated_mode = "peer"` |
| S-5.5c | WP5.5b: `/codex`, `/cursor`, `/claude`, `/amp` ship through the host plugins | Founder request (2026-10-16): when aayudha is a plugin in any host, the user calls another agent from inside it with as little friction as possible, and every pairing works | WP5.5c, in small PRs to `dev`: the host command matrix (this row's D-5.5c.1 to D-5.5c.3), then the brief and the receiver adapters, then cloud-by-default routing on the agent-environments work's detection |
| D-5.5c.1 | WP5.5b: the four hand-off commands load in the Claude Code and Codex plugin | Codex 0.163.0 drops a plugin command whose body uses `$ARGUMENTS`, `$<n>`, `{{…}}`, `` !` ``, or an `@mention` (codex-rs `core-plugins/src/command_migration.rs`), so before this change Codex offered only `home` and `next`; Claude Code and Cursor append the user's text when a command does not use `$ARGUMENTS` | The command sources use no `$ARGUMENTS`: the host appends what the user typed (Amp's plugin asks for it when the command has an `argument-hint` and appends it as `ARGUMENTS:`). `TestPluginManifests` fails on any of Codex's refused tokens; `TestHostPluginsLoad` checks that Codex lists `aayudha:source-command-{claude,cursor,amp}` |
| D-5.5c.2 | WP5.5c: no host offers a hand-off to itself | Claude Code and Codex shared one manifest, which loaded the whole `hosts/commands/` directory, so Claude Code offered `/aayudha:claude` and Codex `codex`. Codex reads `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` before `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, and Cursor reads `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json` first. Claude Code 2.1.301 lists no command given as a single file in `commands`, though its docs allow one, but it does load several directories | The sources move to `hosts/commands/common/` (`home`, `next`) and `hosts/commands/<agent>/<agent>.md`. Three manifests (`.claude-plugin`, `.codex-plugin`, `.cursor-plugin`) each list `common/` and every agent's directory except their own, with hooks for their host (`hosts/codex/hooks.json`, `hosts/cursor/hooks.json` in Cursor's format), and `.cursor-plugin/marketplace.json` lists the plugin. `install --host amp` gives the Amp plugin every command except `amp`. The pre-tool hook already allowed `aayu ready`, `grill --assess`, `build --delegate`, and `status --work`; a test now holds that on all four hosts |
| D-5.5c.3 | WP5.5c: prove every host loads the commands | Claude Code (`claude plugin details`), Codex (`codex debug prompt-input`), and Amp (`amp plugins list`) list a plugin's commands without signing in. The Cursor CLI (2026.10.14) has no headless way to list a plugin's commands, and every run that would show them needs a sign-in | `TestHostPluginsLoad` runs live on Claude Code 2.1.301, Codex 0.163.0, and Amp in the orb. Cursor's manifest is checked only against Cursor's documented schema (`TestPluginManifests`), so Cursor as a host is proven by golden files only until a signed-in Cursor CLI runs the test |
| D-5.5c.4 | WP5.5b: Claude Code's cloud transport is the `@claude` mention through claude-code-action | That action needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` as a repository secret, against the founder's rule of no AI keys in a product repository's CI. Claude Code on the web starts from the user's signed-in CLI: `claude --cloud "<task>"`, with follow-ups through `claude -p … --cloud <session>`; sessions push a branch and open no PR | `delegate.ClaudeCloud` replaces `ClaudeMention`: it runs `claude --cloud <brief>` in the repository, records the session ID and its `claude.ai/code` link, treats the branch the brief names as the signal (aayu opens the PR from it), and queues follow-ups on the session. Claude Code counts as cloud-connected when its CLI is on PATH; whether that CLI's claude.ai sign-in can start cloud sessions is checked by the agent-environments detection, which the routing PR adopts. `[delegate] claude_trigger` stays in the schema, unused, so 0.1 configurations still load. The fixtures are synthetic: no claude.ai sign-in was available |
| D-5.5c.5 | WP5.5b: the local receiver keeps its own key | `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` are generic provider variables that often hold a model gateway's credential; `codex exec` reads `CODEX_API_KEY` (or its saved login), and Claude Code's print mode prefers `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` over the user's subscription when it is set | The scrubbed environment passes only variables that belong to the receiver's own CLI: `CODEX_API_KEY`, `CURSOR_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `AMP_API_KEY`. Provider keys and base URLs never pass, so a receiver runs on its user's own sign-in and never on a gateway credential |
| D-5.5c.6 | WP5.5c: every hand-off gets a fresh worktree (local) or branch (cloud); the receiver breaks the work down, uses its own subagents, verifies the Completion Goal with evidence, and returns what it proved; the calling host reports it | A re-hand-off after a failure reused `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-<agent>` and the story branch. Receivers report in different places (a PR body, a CLI's final message) | `delegate/<n>/state.json` counts hand-offs. Hand-off *k* > 1 uses `story/<n>-<slug>-<k>` and `.aayudha/worktrees/<n>-<agent>-<k>`, and a local hand-off skips a number an old worktree or remote branch still holds; syncs keep their hand-off's branch and worktree. The brief adds "How to work and report": break the work down by checkpoint, use your own subagents, verify every criterion with evidence before you report, and write a `## What I proved` section (PR body in the cloud, final message locally), with the line that the merge gate's proof decides. `build --delegate` reads that section from the PR body or the CLI's output, keeps it on the ledger, reports it as the receiver's preview (`delegation.proved`), and quotes it in the PR body aayu opens. It is untrusted text, never a gate |
| D-5.5c.7 | WP5.5c: a receiver never gets merge rights | A local receiver can run `aayu` itself, and so could hand the story on to a third agent | `build --delegate` refuses (exit 4) when `AAYU_DELEGATED` is set, which the scrubbed environment sets for every local receiver; the brief tells cloud receivers not to hand the story on |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP7.6 and WP7.7 (`audit`, `retro`, `evolve`)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-7.6a | WP7.6 `audit`: floors, proof coverage, waivers, decision SLA, autonomy exceptions, reconciliation of GitHub audit events against `actions` segments (flags unlogged writes), licence scan; report to `ledger/audit/<date>/`; inputs (`ledger` SHA, GitHub event cursor, actions segment list, config hash) in the header; accept: dogfood outputs match fixtures, a direct `gh` write outside `actions` is flagged, a solo-mode self-approval (WP0.1) and a human-fallback write (WP1.4) each appear, a re-run with the recorded inputs is byte-identical. WP0.1 and D2: the hand-run reconciliations on the ledger (`audit/2026-10-11/…`, `audit/2026-10-12/…`) are what `audit` automates | GitHub has no single event stream with a cursor. Rule suites (`rulesets/rule-suites`) need a repository admin token, are listed for at most a month (`time_period`), and list no actor for a deploy-key push (D1); the repository events API stops at 300 events or 90 days; issue timelines carry no request identity, so a write can be tied to an actions record only by request, target, and time. Nothing distinguishes a raw `gh` write from an `aayu` run whose segment never reached the ledger (D-M4.5a, D-4.10b). D2 made the self-approval a ruleset bypass after a "Solo-mode bypass:" comment, and D-4.10a added `ship --solo-bypass`. The plan names no rule for which PR delivered a story, and stories #38–#50 merged before the merge gate and `ship` existed (founder decision, 2026-10-12: done but unproven, counted as uncovered) | `internal/audit`: `Fetch` reads every source up to `--until` (the cursor, exclusive) and the ledger at `--ledger` (a new `ledger.Writer.ReadAt`, which must be the tip or an ancestor, and `Tree.Log` with the `Aayudha-Identity` trailer); `Build` is pure and `Markdown` holds no clock reading but the inputs, so the same inputs give the same bytes. Because GitHub's history is mutable (a comment can be edited) and rule suites age out after a month, everything read from GitHub is also frozen into a GitHub inputs snapshot (`audit/<date>/audit-<cursor>.inputs.json`, comment text cut to its first 160 bytes, PR bodies reduced to the stories they reference) written in the same ledger commit as the report; `aayu audit --replay <snapshot>` regenerates the report byte for byte from the snapshot and the recorded ledger commit without asking GitHub, and refuses when the configuration hash differs. The header records the window, the cursor and the newest rule suite, the snapshot path and SHA-256, the ledger commit, the code commit (the dev commit at the cursor, for the licence scan), the SHA-256 of the effective config, and every segment with its SHA-256 and record count, plus both regeneration commands (`--replay`, and a live re-read with `--since --until --ledger`, which gives the same bytes while GitHub is unchanged). Bypasses (rule suites with result `bypass` pushed in the window, each read in full for its failed rules) are classified: a PR merge (the suite's `after_sha` is a merged PR's merge commit) is a **solo-mode bypass** when the only failed rule is a required status check whose details name only `protected-paths` and a comment starting "Solo-mode bypass:" came at or before the push, otherwise unexplained (no comment, or another check bypassed); a push to the ledger branch is a **founder-identity ledger write (D1)** when its commit is in the ledger history at the recorded commit, a deploy-key push named from that commit; a push to `rolling`/`main` is a **promotion** when its commit subject is `Rolling snapshot: …`/`Release: v…` and a successful `ci.yml` `workflow_dispatch` run (the gate) on that commit started before the push; a `v*` tag bypass is accounted for when the tag is on `main`, when `release.yml` refused it (rule 8), or when a never-released tag is deleted; anything else is unexplained unless an earlier report on the ledger lists its rule-suite ID (every table row of a hand-run reconciliation; only rows an `aayu audit` report marks accounted for). `ship --solo-bypass` passes come from the ship records. Human-fallback writes: segment records with identity `human-fallback`, items labelled `identity:human-fallback` in the window, and ledger commits with that trailer. Unlogged writes: every write in the timelines of items changed in the window (creations, comments, labels, closes, merges, reviews, sub-issues, …; mirrors and consequences left out), every `workflow_dispatch` run, and every ledger commit, matched, oldest first, to a done segment record with the same request and target within 10 minutes, each record accounting for one write (one per label of a labels request), a comment record whose result is a comment ID matching only that comment, and a dispatch record matching only a run of the workflow file it dispatched, so a logged write cannot cover a direct one beside it; `…[bot]` actors that are not `[identity] bot_logins` are workflow and App writes, recorded by their runs; the rest are listed, grouped by target. A story is delivered when its status at the cursor is merged, released, measured, or done, or a PR merged into dev delivers it: a `story/<n>-` branch or `Refs #n` (Closes, Fixes, Resolves) in the body, or, in a PR with no such reference (written before the convention), `Story #n`; the first such PR is the merge PR. It is covered by an authoritative pack that verifies against the Ready snapshot and whose floors hold; otherwise it is **merged without proof (pre-gate)** when its merge PR merged before GitHub registered `aayudha-merge-gate.yml`, else merged without proof, both uncovered. Labels at the cursor are replayed from the timeline for items changed in the window and for every story, bug, and Decision. Floors and waivers come from packs created in the window (misses counted in authoritative packs); Decision SLA is business hours of `[time]` from creation to close or the cursor; autonomy exceptions are WP5.7 corrections and agent merges on `[autonomy] never` layers; the licence scan checks every go.mod requirement of the code commit against a THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md row with the same version and an allowed licence (the CI allow list) and reports the CI `security` job's conclusion on that commit (go-licenses and the npm scan). Without an admin token `audit` exits 4 rather than report an incomplete reconciliation; unexplained bypasses, unlogged writes, and uncovered stories are reported, not an exit code. The report goes to `audit/<date>/audit-<cursor>.md` under the bot, or the founder tagged `identity:human-fallback` (D1), and the run's segment is checkpointed; an identical report already at the tip is not written again. `--since` defaults to the cursor of the newest report, else seven days back. `audit --mark-merged <list>` (no report) moves listed stories the audit calls merged without proof (pre-gate) to `status:merged` with a replace-in-place `<!-- aayudha:audit-pre-gate -->` comment naming the merge PR and saying that no authoritative pack exists, through `internal/actions`, and refuses (exit 4, nothing written) any story that is covered, not delivered, or merged after the gate. Its first run here found two go.mod modules missing from THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md (`github.com/mattn/go-isatty`, `github.com/ncruces/go-strftime`, both MIT, via `modernc.org/libc`), added in the same PR. Tests: `TestAuditDogfoodFixture` (a synthetic copy of the hand-run reconciliations' cases against the fake GitHub and a local ledger with fixed commit dates and no machine git configuration, compared with the reviewed golden `internal/cli/testdata/audit/report.golden`), `TestAuditFlagsUnloggedWrite`, `TestLoggedCommentIsNotReused`, `TestLoggedDispatchNamesItsWorkflow`, `TestClassifyBypasses`, `TestAuditSoloBypassAndHumanFallback`, `TestAuditRerunByteIdentical` (a live re-read after the ledger moved on, then a replay after the solo-mode comment is edited and the rule suites age out), `TestAuditMarkMerged`, `TestAuditGitHubErrors` (any GitHub error exits 5 and writes nothing) |
| D-7.6b | WP7.6 `retro`: estimate vs observed checkpoints, correction rate, streaks, floor misses, cost per story; files findings as Feedback issues on `agentic-ayudha`, never PRs; inputs in the header | The plan fixes no checkpoint budget per Effort, no correction-rate threshold, no home for the report, and no rule for when a finding is new | `retro` reuses `audit`'s inputs and fetch (no rule suites, so no admin token) and writes `retro/<date>/retro-<cursor>.md` on the ledger with the same header, segment table, and GitHub inputs snapshot (`--replay` likewise). Per story delivered in the window: Effort, checkpoint budget (proposed: XS 1, S 2, M 3, L 5, one checkpoint per footprint layer as WP5.4 plans), checkpoints observed (the largest N of `k/N` in its packs), corrections of its merge PR, cost (`cost_usd` of its records in the segments), and proof. Agent merges, corrected merges, per-skill rates (agent PRs that changed `skills/<skill>/`), and WP5.7 streaks. Findings, each filed once as a Feedback issue (`type:feedback`, `status:backlog`, `intake`, the human-fallback tag on day one; issue-form body) titled `Feedback: retro: <finding>` and matched by title on later runs: a story over its checkpoint budget, a story over `[budget] per_story_usd`, a story merged without proof after the merge gate existed, a floor miss, and a correction rate above 20 % overall or per skill. Pre-gate merges are a founder decision, not a finding. With `retro` implemented, the WP7.1 loops render its weekly job (`aayudha-loops.yml`, `loops/amp/retro.json`, the Cursor automation); in Actions it runs with `--ci` and needs the App token to write (D1), so until the founder creates the App that job exits 4 and the Amp loop (founder identity) is the one that runs. Tested by `TestRetroFilesFeedback` (one new Feedback issue, one already filed, no PR, and a replay that files nothing) |
| D-7.6c | WP7.6 follow-up (founder decisions, 2026-10-12, on the first live audit): a protected-path merge whose "Solo-mode bypass" record came after the merge (#86, rule suite 4473766681) and stories merged after the merge gate without `ship` (#53–#56, #59, #60, #62, #64–#68) | The first live report listed #86 as the only unexplained bypass; the founder verified it (only `.github/workflows/inspect-live.yml` was protected; every other required check was green at the merged head), posted a late "Solo-mode bypass (recorded late …)" comment, and wrote `audit/2026-10-12/incident-pr86-missing-bypass-comment.md` on the ledger. Stories merged after the gate without `ship` have no authoritative pack and stayed `status:ready`; the founder decided they are done but unproven, with no retroactive ship records | A bypass that would be an ordinary solo-mode bypass but has no prior comment is classified "solo-mode bypass, recorded late" (its own class and section, counted apart in the summary, listed only when present so earlier reports replay unchanged) when a later PR comment starting "Solo-mode bypass" or a ledger note `audit/<date>/incident-*.md` names its rule-suite ID; the rule-suite IDs a comment names are read before the comment is cut for the snapshot (`Event.Suites`). Anything else without a prior comment stays unexplained. Stories merged without proof after the gate are "merged without proof (post-gate)" in the report (pre-gate ones keep their label), both uncovered. `audit --mark-merged` now also takes post-gate stories: same status move through `internal/actions`, a distinct replace-in-place comment (`<!-- aayudha:audit-post-gate -->`, "merged after the merge gate existed, but without `aayu ship` … no ship record is made after the fact"), and it refuses covered stories, stories with no merged PR, and stories already released, measured, or done. A report written before this change replays with the binary that wrote it (the post-gate label is new). Tests: `TestAuditBypassRecordedLate` (a late comment naming another suite stays unexplained; one naming the suite, or an incident note, makes it recorded late), `TestClassifyBypasses`, `TestAuditMarkMerged` (post-gate story #60 in the fixture) |
| D-7.7a | WP7.7 `evolve`: quarterly, accepted retro Feedback → first a failing eval case that reproduces it → a skill diff that passes → a PR labelled `agent-authored`, human CODEOWNER review, never auto-merged; ships via `rolling`; `install --pin` rollback; per-skill correction rate tracked before and after; accept: an `evolve` PR without a new eval case is rejected by CI | Tier 1 checks recorded runs, not live skills, so "fails before, passes after" has to be two recorded runs. The plan does not say what marks a PR as an evolve PR, what "accepted" Feedback is, or which CI job rejects it. `internal/evals` replays CLI calls, so it imports `internal/cli` and the CLI cannot import it. A `pull_request` workflow re-runs on new commits, not on label changes, unless it lists those event types | An evolve case is an eval case with `feedback = <issue>` and `reproduce = "<dir>"` (new case keys): `<dir>` holds the current skill's recorded run (the `output/` artifacts under that name) and must fail tier 1; `output/` holds the new skill's run and must pass. `evals.EvolveGate` (the eval-first gate) requires, for the change against its base, at least one added `evals/cases/<skill>/<name>/case.toml` that names its Feedback and reproduce directory, a diff to that skill (`skills/<skill>/` or `evals/skills/<skill>/`), the old run existing and validating against its schemas yet failing another tier-1 check (a missing or malformed run reproduces nothing), and the new run passing tier 1. An evolve PR is one whose head branch starts `evolve/` or that has a commit carrying the `Aayudha-Evolve:` trailer; both change only with a push, which re-runs CI, whereas a label can be added without re-running the required check, so the `aayu:evolve` label is applied for people but never decides (CI fetches the PR's commits to read the trailers). CI: the required `build` job runs `go run ./internal/evals/cmd/eval -evolve-gate -base HEAD^1` on every PR (the merge commit's first parent is the base) and fails an evolve PR the gate refuses, so it cannot merge without a bypass; other PRs pass the step. `aayu evolve` without `--feedback` (the quarterly WP7.1 loop, now rendered for Amp, Cursor, and Actions) lists the open, accepted retro Feedback with the skill each names and writes nothing. `aayu evolve --skill <skill> --feedback <n>` acts only on an open Feedback issue filed by `aayu retro` (title prefix) and accepted: it carries `needs:grill`, triage's existing "accepted Feedback" label, and `evolve` takes it instead of `grill` when it is about a skill. The first run cuts `.aayudha/worktrees/evolve-<skill>-<n>` on `evolve/<skill>-<n>` (keyed by both, so another skill for the same Feedback gets its own worktree and branch) from `origin/dev`; the next runs the same gate (the binary injects it, `cli.Options.EvolveGate`, because of the import cycle), and on a pass commits with DCO sign-off and an `Aayudha-Evolve: #n` trailer, pushes with the verb's token, opens the PR (`Refs #n`, the cases, the rollback command), and labels it `agent-authored` and `aayu:evolve`. It never merges; `skills/` is a protected path, so a human CODEOWNER reviews it. On a refusal nothing is committed or written. The per-skill correction rate before and after is `retro`'s skills table (agent PRs that changed `skills/<skill>/`, and their WP5.7 corrections) in the retro windows on either side of the merge, so `evolve` does not compute its own. "Quarterly" is the WP7.1 loop schedule (day 2 of January, April, July, and October). Tests: `TestEvolveGateCI` (the CI entry point over a scratch git repository: an evolve PR with only a skill diff is rejected, and so is one marked only by an `Aayudha-Evolve:` commit; a case whose old run is missing is rejected; a case whose old run passes, or a case without a skill diff, is rejected; an eval-first PR passes; a non-evolve PR is not checked, whatever its labels) and `TestEvolveVerb` (the verb against the fake GitHub: listing, refusals of unaccepted, non-retro, or closed Feedback, a case for another Feedback, a binary without the gate, an eval-first PR opened once and never merged) |
| D-7.7b | WP7.7 CI gate, found on #138: the "Eval-first gate for evolve PRs" step failed intermittently with `git log HEAD^1..HEAD: exit status 128`; re-runs did not help, a fresh push did | The step read the PR's commits from the checked-out test-merge commit (`HEAD^1..HEAD`, after `git fetch --deepen`). GitHub recomputes `refs/pull/<n>/merge` when the base moves, so a run's checked-out merge can stop being on any ref; deepening it then cannot fetch its parents, and a re-run checks out the same event's merge. `TestEvolveGateAfterTestMergeRecomputed` replays this against a local origin (shallow checkout of the merge, dev moves, the merge is recomputed and the old one pruned) and gets the same error from the old step | The step passes the event's `pull_request.base.sha` and `head.sha` (`eval -evolve-gate -base <sha> -head <sha>`), and the gate fetches whichever of them, or their merge base, the checkout lacks from origin by SHA (with history, so the merge base exists), never using the merge commit. The PR's commits are `base..head` (two dots: only the PR's own commits, so a base commit's `Aayudha-Evolve:` trailer cannot mark it); its change is `base...head` (three dots: from the merge base, so base commits made after the PR branched are not in it). The eval cases are still read from the checked-out tree. The same test checks that the gate passes an eval-first PR and refuses an evolve commit without a case in that replay |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP8.1 (dogfood)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-8.1a | WP5.5: a never-layer footprint ships only with the Engineering Manager's plan-time review on the ledger (`plan --approve`, human-only), whatever the streak; WP8.1 needs stories on L04, L07, and L09, which are `[autonomy] never` layers | One human and no bot App (D1, D2). The agent drafts and checks every plan, so the one human approving it would be the only review, and an agent running `plan --approve` under the founder's account would record an approval that no one gave. In solo mode `plan --approve` records the one human as `as: solo`, which reads as a review. Founder decision (2026-10-12, relayed by the orchestrating thread): a never-layer story proceeds under a solo-mode bypass, recorded as a bypass and never as an approval | `aayu plan <n> --solo-bypass` (new registry input, human-only, refused for agents and when `--approve` is also passed): only in solo mode (no bot identity, no second paired human, the same checks as `ship --solo-bypass`), only for a plan on the ledger that needs a review and has none, and only after the human's own comment on the story starting "Solo-mode bypass:". It writes `em_review.bypass {as: "solo-bypass", reviewer: "none (solo mode)", recorded_by, recorded_at, comment_url, note}` to `specs/<n>/plan.json` and leaves `approved_by` and `as` empty. `ship` accepts a real approval or this bypass for a never layer; the ship record keeps it under `plan_review`, and the Complete section says "Plan-time review: none; … recorded a solo-mode plan-review bypass for layers …". `audit` lists these ships in their own section, "Solo-mode plan-review bypasses (`plan --solo-bypass`)", with their own count in the summary. Neither appears when there are none, so earlier reports replay unchanged. A plan already approved refuses the bypass. Tests: `TestPlan` (refusals, the bypass record, no approval claimed, and the bypass refused after an approval), `TestShipPassRules` (a bypass passes the plan check), `TestShipMobileWaiverAndSoloBypass` (ship record and Complete section), and `TestAuditPlanReviewBypass`. Every WP8.1 story shipped this way is listed in the WP8.1 report for the founder to review after the fact |
| D-8.1b | WP4.5 and WP8.1: the merge gate runs every floor of a story on the PR head, the web floors against `[verify] seed` data; a product repository installs Playwright, axe-core, and Lighthouse itself (packages/floors) | aayudha's merge gate installed only the Go-side floor tools, so a UI story's `a11y` and `perf` floors could only be blocked there. `examples/benefits-qa` lives in aayudha's own checkout, so its verify settings are aayudha's `.aayudha/config.toml`, and nothing starts the app in the gate. `[verify] seed` runs with the network and outside verify's PID namespace | aayudha's own `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` (not the `init` template, which leaves these tools to each product) checks out the base branch (never the PR's lockfile), installs `@aayudha/floors` with the frozen lockfile and denied install scripts, installs Chromium, and sets `NODE_PATH`, which verify already reads. `.aayudha/config.toml` gains `[verify] seed = ["examples/benefits-qa/deploy/serve.sh"]` (the script builds the example and starts it detached on seeded, synthetic data, then waits for `/healthz`), the local target `http://127.0.0.1:8787`, the example's Lighthouse budgets (`min_score` 0.9, LCP 2500 ms, CLS 0.1, TBT 200 ms), and `[measure]` events for its Goal (`benefits_visit` acquires, `answer_grounded` within 7 days activates). Only stories with screenshot or recording criteria run the web floors, so aayudha's own stories are unaffected. The layer table maps the example's directories (`web` L01, `api` and `cmd` L02, `ask` L03, `policy` L04, `answer` L05, `contracts` L06, `store` L07, `analytics` L08, `deploy` L09), which `go generate` renders into LAYERS.md and the seat charters |
| D-8.1c | Ground rule 5: `review` lane A and the `brief` adversarial pass call a model directly from the binary, where the person running the verb holds the key | aayudha's only model key is the `ai-gateway` environment's secret, which admits dev, rolling, and main. Neither the founder's machine nor the agent's orb holds a key, and the orb's other gateway variables belong to another organisation and are not used | `aayudha-model-lanes.yml`, dispatch only, from dev: `verb=review pr=<n>` checks out the PR head, builds the example (no PR code runs as the runner user), runs it as `nobody` so it cannot read the key holder's environment, and runs `aayudha review <n> --ci` (lane A through the gateway, lane B with the base branch's floor tools); the critique and captures are the run's artifact and the PR gets review's replace-in-place comment. `verb=brief subject=<n> register purpose draft=<base64>` runs `aayudha brief … --ci` on dev with the gateway (the adversarial pass) and Playwright (the page's axe check); the HTML, manifest, draft, and output are the run's artifact. Only the step that runs the verb sees the secret |

### Deviations recorded while implementing WP8.2 (docs and installers)

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-8.2a | WP8.2: `tell docs` → GitHub Pages (the plan names no source branch) | `dev` is the default branch, and the `github-pages` environment that Pages creates admits only the default branch until an admin adds a rule. `rolling` moves only on promotions, so docs built from it would trail the code that generates them, and neither branch has an installable artifact: `install.sh` installs only `v*` releases from `main`. Enabling Pages is a repository-admin call | `.github/workflows/pages.yml` builds on every push to `dev` (and on pull requests that touch Markdown, `internal/tell/`, `internal/verbs/`, or `skills/`, without deploying) and deploys `dev`'s build with `actions/deploy-pages`. Each page's header names the commit it was built from. Pages was enabled with `build_type: workflow`. Moving the site to `rolling` later is a trigger change plus a `github-pages` branch rule |
| D-8.2b | WP8.2 with D-7.5a: the docs are `tell docs` copy; claims link only to Features with authoritative packs; `tell publish` is human-only | D-7.5a's `tell docs` checks a draft the host writes; the plan does not say which pages the site carries or how a site reaches Pages. The repository's own Markdown is already reviewed copy, and a Pages deployment from CI writes nothing to the ledger. D-7.5a's check counted `#N` inside inline code (`[#12]` in the brief skill's citation format) as a claim, though GitHub renders no link there | New registry input `tell --site <dir>` (docs only; not with `--from` or `--publish`; the directory must be new or empty). It builds the site from the checkout: README (home, followed by the proven Features list), `docs/getting-started.md` (new: installing, `doctor`, and connecting Amp, Cursor, and Claude Code), CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY, CONSTRAINTS, LAYERS, and `docs/RACI.md` when present, plus a verbs reference generated from the registry and a page per embedded skill. Every page goes through `tell.Check` with the proven Features from the same rule; one problem refuses the whole site (exit 4, each problem named by source file and line) and nothing is written. Links to another page become relative links (heading anchors follow GitHub's rules, so `CONTRIBUTING.md#…` works); every other relative link resolves as GitHub resolves it and points at the built commit on GitHub; every outbound link carries the UTM tags (`utm_medium=docs`, campaign `docs-site` in the workflow). Goldmark (already a dependency) renders GFM with raw HTML omitted; one stylesheet, no scripts. `tell.Check` now skips inline code spans as CommonMark delimits them (a run of n backticks closed by the next run of exactly n), for claims, avoided words, and UTM tags alike. Deploying the site is not `tell --publish`: nothing is written to the ledger, and the pages are Markdown already merged to `dev`. Tests: `TestSiteBuild`, `TestTellDocsSite`, `TestCheckFences` (code spans), `TestCheckLeavesCodeSpanLinks` |
| D-8.2c | WP8.2 accept: `install.sh` on clean macOS/Linux/Windows VMs; `aayu doctor` passes | No `v*` release exists until the orchestrator cuts v0.1.0, and `install.sh` accepts only `release.yml`-signed `v*` releases (WP0.4). The plan does not define "clean VM" or "passes". WP0.4 and D-0.4d name a Scoop manifest, not a PowerShell script, as the Windows package path; `install.sh` already supports Git Bash. GoReleaser cannot give a Scoop manifest or cask a second command name | `.github/workflows/installers.yml` (dispatchable, and run on pull requests that change `install.sh`, `.goreleaser.yaml`, or the workflow) uses fresh GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest`, `macos-latest`, and `windows-latest` runners as the clean machines. It builds a goreleaser snapshot signed keylessly by itself; on each OS `install.sh` must refuse a flipped byte (checksum) and the default `release.yml` identity (signature), then install the snapshot with `AAYU_CERT_IDENTITY_REGEXP` pinned to that run's exact identity, bootstrapping its pinned cosign where none is installed, into the default `~/.local/bin` (with the PATH hint when that directory is not on PATH). A new shell finds `aayu` and `aayudha` on PATH, `aayu version` names the snapshot, and `aayu doctor` in a fresh checkout with git and git-lfs installed exits 0 with no `FAIL` row and `PASS` for both. Windows runs `install.sh` in Git Bash, and the Scoop manifest in PowerShell: Scoop (installer pinned by commit and SHA-256) installs goreleaser's manifest from a local server, refuses the same manifest with a wrong hash, and its `aayu` shim runs `doctor`. When a `v*` release exists, the same three OSes run the documented `install.sh` from `main` with no overrides against the latest release, and Windows installs from the Scoop bucket once it is published. "Passes" means exit 0 and no `FAIL` row: `WARN` and `N/A` rows (no token, no host, optional tools) are what a new machine has. `release.yml`'s `scoop` job adds the `aayu` alias to the manifest with `jq`, the filter the workflow tests. The first Windows runs found two defects, both fixed here: Git Bash rewrote the backslashes of the identity regexp `install.sh` passes to the native `cosign.exe`, so no genuine release could have verified on Windows (`install.sh` now passes native paths with `cygpath -w` and turns MSYS argument conversion off for that call); and Windows has no zoneinfo, so `doctor` failed `config` on the default `[time] tz` (`internal/config` now embeds `time/tzdata`, about 450 KB). The Homebrew cask still links only `aayudha`, and Scoop and winget check only the archive's SHA-256 from their manifests; only `install.sh` checks the cosign signature |
| D-8.2d | WP8.2: first `rolling` snapshot; brew/Scoop/winget after the stable decision | The first rolling snapshots were promoted before WP8.2 (`rolling-2026-10-11`, `rolling-2026-10-12`, `rolling-2026-10-12.2`). Under D-0.4a the stable decision is an explicit stable-release request plus a green stable gate, which the orchestrator owns. `release.yml` already publishes the cask, the Scoop manifest, and the winget PR for a non-prerelease `v*` tag, and fails each job naming the missing secret until its repository and token exist (D-0.4d) | No promotion or `v*` tag is part of WP8.2. The founder creates, before the first stable tag: `rahulnakmol/homebrew-tap` with `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN` in environment `release-homebrew`; `rahulnakmol/scoop-bucket` with `SCOOP_BUCKET_TOKEN` in `release-scoop`; and a fork `rahulnakmol/winget-pkgs` with `WINGET_TOKEN` (classic, `public_repo`) in `release-winget` (CONTRIBUTING.md, Release publishing setup). After v0.1.0, `gh workflow run installers.yml --ref dev` runs the release jobs |

The registry gains a journey `Stage` per verb (`verbs.Stages`, every verb in exactly one, plumbing in none, tested), and `internal/verbs/gen` renders the 32-verb map into `docs/guide/when-to-use-what.md` (region `verb-map`). Diagrams are `docs/guide/img/*.diagram` sources rendered to SVG by brief's diagram renderer through a new `go generate` step (`internal/tell/gen`); the site serves images that exist in the checkout from its own copy (`tell.SiteMedia`). ### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP5.1b (seat personas)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-5.1b | WP5.1 gives each seat a charter (purpose, responsibilities, rights, limits) but no voice; twins differ only by RACI | Founder request (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): give each twin a character and a soul, with the Engineering Manager elevating the concept, breaking issues down with anecdotes, reviewing and nudging toward alignment, and grounding every point in the codebase and architecture when challenged; then (same day) each twin must practise like a senior practitioner with disciplines from large-scale technology companies, adapted to the team's size, with public sources, never claiming to have worked at a named company, imitating real people, or using trademarks as personas | WP5.1b added to M5: personas as data in `seats/<seat>/persona.toml`, rendered everywhere by `go generate`; a practice playbook of three or four entries per seat with sources; `[team] stage` and `size`; the evidence rule as a deterministic check; persona eval cases in tier 1 and tier 2 |
| D-5.1b | WP5.1b: one persona source rendered into charters, SKILL.md bodies, AGENTS.md, and the MCP prompt text; drift-checked; tier 1 on recorded outputs for the grounding rule with a planted uncited claim; one tier-2 EM-led case through `evals.yml` with a judge of another family; the same scenario at startup and enterprise must differ in depth while both stay grounded | Skill bodies are hand-authored and another thread is rewriting `grill`'s; the generator owned only the frontmatter. The MCP server had prompts only for `grill` and `brief`, both built from the skill body. "Grounded" has no deterministic definition. `inspect` runs in the review lane, which is also the lane the tier-2 judge is routed through, so an `inspect` case would judge itself. The `skills` tier-2 group spent about 4.6 of its 6 USD cap. "Inferred by a sensible default" names no rule. `inspect`'s merge-gate model call does not read SKILL.md | `persona.toml` per seat, strictly decoded and validated at load (`seats.LoadPersonas`: every field, two or more values, habits, questions, and nevers, three or more practices, each with a source naming a year and an optional https URL, distinct stage artefacts none of which starts another, unique one-word names, and no company name or employment claim outside sources); `TestPersonas` also applies `design/brand.yaml`'s avoid list. The generator writes the Persona region of each charter, the AGENTS.md seat section (a Twin column plus each twin's purpose and voice, the stage rule, and the evidence rule), and a block between `<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: persona -->` and `<!-- END GENERATED: persona -->` placed after the body's title (replaced in place once present), so text outside the markers, including `grill`'s, stays hand-written. MCP: `grill` and `brief` prompts carry the block through the skill body, and a new `persona` prompt (seat, optional stage; default the repository's effective stage) renders the persona at a stage. `[team] stage` (enum) and `size`; unset, `Config.TeamStage` infers it from the size (≤ 15 startup, ≤ 150 scaleup, else enterprise), else from the paired seats plus the founder, so a solo pod is a startup. The evidence rule (`seats.CheckGrounding`): Markdown splits into paragraphs, list items, and table rows (header rows, headings, code fences, and comments excluded); a unit that matches a code, architecture, risk, or progress cue needs a citation (a path with optional line, a backticked directory, `#n`, `ADR-n`, a backticked `pkg.Symbol`, a backticked commit, `run <id>`, or a backticked config key); questions are exempt; a unit labelled `Analogy:` or `Anecdote:` needs a cited neighbour; an answer with statements but no citation fails; with a grounding text every citation must occur in it. `seats.CheckStage`: the practice's artefact for the stage is a heading prefix, no other stage's is, and a startup answer is at most 400 words. New eval case kind `[persona]`; tier 1 on `retro/em-startup`, `retro/em-enterprise`, `inspect/em-review` (scaleup; with `planted/review.md`, whose uncited claim must fail), `proof/qa-startup`, and `proof/qa-enterprise`, all on one synthetic grounding (`evals/cases/retro/grounding.md`, story #212 of a fixture repository, including an untrusted comment asking a bot to approve). Tier 2 only on the two `retro/` cases, in a new `personas` group (4 USD): the skill answers at the stage, gets the check findings once to revise, else scores 1 without the judge; the judge (`gpt-6.1-sol`, openai, against `retro`'s spec lane, anthropic) scores clarity, nudging, grounding, realism, stage fit, and guardrails. The names are proposals for the founder. `inspect`'s direct model prompt is unchanged (its merge-gate behaviour is out of scope) |

| D-5.1d | WP5.1b accept: tier 2 meets the WP3.4 bar for the EM retro cases and the two decision packs; the `personas` group gates promotion like every enforced group | Not met. Run 38682959916 (035a05f): spend cap 6 USD reached after 6 of 20 runs (6.06 USD); the five judged runs scored 2, 3, 2, 3, 3 (decision packs, means 2.2 Architect and 3.0 EM), one run stopped at a deterministic check, fourteen errored on the cap. Run 38685472745 (b2b5907, after the D-5.1c fix): mean 1.45, pass^5 0, 13.33 USD; case means 1.4 (Architect pack), 1.8 (EM pack), 1.2 (EM retro, enterprise), 1.4 (EM retro, startup); 15 of 20 runs stopped at the deterministic checks and the five judged runs scored 3, 3, 3, 2, 3. Most of those stops were false positives of the checks, not the model's: template paths such as `retro/<date>/` and `ledger/plans/<story>/plan.json` and the twin's own `seats/<seat>/CHARTER.md` were refused as citations missing from the grounding, a bold option heading and a short lead-in ending with a colon were read as uncited claims, and a startup retro of 405 words counted its headings | The checks are fixed (`seats.CheckGroundingWith`): a citation with `<` or `>` is a template placeholder, needs no grounding, and is no evidence, and a unit whose only citations are placeholders is not a claim; a registry seat's `CHARTER.md` or `persona.toml` is always a known source; a unit that is only a bold label, or a lead-in of at most twelve words ending with a colon, is a heading and claims nothing; `seats.Words`, which bounds a startup answer (400) and a decision pack (500), counts body prose only (paragraphs and list items; headings, table rows, code, and comments excluded). `TestCheckGrounding` and `TestCheckStage` hold each fix with its other side (a real ungrounded path, a placeholder beside a real uncited claim, a non-seat charter, another file under a seat, a bold label followed by a claim, a long claim ending with a colon, body prose over the bound).
