The `personas` group is `gate = "advisory"` (as `brief-plain` is, D-7.3h): computed and printed, skipped by `evals-gate`; it becomes gating again when a run passes the bar. What remains after the fix is the model's quality: the judged runs scored 2–3 (claims stretched beyond their sources, packs long for a five-minute decision). One more dispatch was run after this fix and its numbers are in the WP5.1b report; no further iteration in this WP |
| D-5.1e | WP5.1b: each twin has a short name proposed by the implementer, which the founder may veto | The founder vetoed the twelve proposed names (Mira, Tomas, Kiran, Noor, Ines, Pax, Vera, Juno, Hana, Remy, Leo, Aadi) on 2026-10-15 and chose short Greek mythology names | Renamed in `seats/<seat>/persona.toml` only, everything else in the personas unchanged: Product Strategist Metis, Code Architect Daedalus, Engineering Manager Athena, AI Native Engineer (+SRE hat) Talos, UX Researcher & Evolutionist Iris, DevOps & DevEx Tinkerer Hermes, Quality Architect Themis, Mentalist Pythia, Auditor Argus, Marketer & Documenter Clio, Revenue Architect Plutus, Founder Zeus. The persona text refers to each twin as "it", so no pronouns changed. `go generate` re-rendered the charters, the SKILL.md persona blocks, and the AGENTS.md seat section (the MCP `persona` prompt reads the TOML at run time); the charter line and the TOML header no longer call the name a proposal; the decide skill's hand-written text, `seats/README.md`, the eval case descriptions, recorded decision packs, and retro rubric, the tests, and the CLI home goldens use the new names. Earlier Appendix B rows keep the names in use when they were written |
| D-5.6b | WP5.6 with WP5.1b: on demand, a stuck human gets a decision pack from the owning seat's twin: the question restated and checked, grounded facts, two or three options with trade-offs, a recommendation with its door, the cost of waiting, a Mentalist pre-mortem, the questions that would change the answer, and the one thing to decide; read-only and MCP-safe; nothing applied; no new verb | The founder first asked (2026-10-15) for automatic blocker detection, a 4-hour decision pack, 24-hour resolution, new `[decisions]` keys, a tighter V8, and new status, retro, and audit columns, then withdrew it the same day: "I don't need automation. I just need a skill that helps me surface decision-making, coupled with a tight digital twin." None of the automation was built, and V8 (24 h escalate, 48 h provisional) is unchanged. `decide` was CLI and skill only, `ExposeNever`, and its positional was an issue, so a free question could not be passed and MCP could not reach it. Nothing chose a seat by topic | `decide` gains `--pack` (bool; pinned to `true` on MCP while `[mcp] expose_writes` is false, as `ready --check` is), `--seat <slug>`, and `--draft <file>`; its positional becomes a string that must be an issue unless `--pack` is set; it gains the MCP surface with `ExposeRead` (`--accept` stays human-only and never reaches MCP; `--pack` refuses `--accept` and `--waiver`). The verb count stays 32. `decide.OwningSeat` routes by topic words, narrower topics first (Auditor, Revenue Architect for growth metrics, Quality Architect, AI Native Engineer for incidents, DevOps, UX, Code Architect for design, Marketer, Mentalist, Product Strategist, Founder for direction, Engineering Manager for delivery and alignment), then by issue type, then to the Engineering Manager. `aayu decide <issue or "question"> --pack` reads the issue, its parent, up to ten linked items, open Decisions, and `status:blocked` items, wraps the issue body as untrusted data, and prints the owning twin's persona at the team stage and the pack outline; `--draft` checks a pack with `decide.CheckPackWith`: the eight `##` parts in order, a one-sentence question, two or three options (top-level items or `###` headings), a named door, `## Your decision` last and one paragraph, and the evidence rule, with citations resolved against the printed grounding or paths in the checkout (exit 4 on findings). The decide skill body says the owning twin speaks for the pack and the human records the outcome with `--accept`. Every persona's values include "helping a human decide quickly, and never letting a blocker sit" (`TestPersonas`). Eval case kind `[pack]` (routing, the recorded pack, and an incomplete pack that must fail); cases `decide/em-split-or-replan` (startup, with a pack missing its pre-mortem) and `decide/architect-tombstone-kind` (scaleup) on a shared synthetic grounding, both in tier 2 (the `personas` group, cap raised to 6 USD) with a rubric whose first item is "a busy human can decide from this in under five minutes" |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP7.2b (cloud integrations)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-7.2b | WP5.2, WP7.2, and WP4.2 read Azure, AWS, and GCP only as export files, and the media bucket speaks only S3/R2 (D-4.2a, D-5.2a, D-7.2a) | Founder request (2026-10-12, relayed by the orchestrating thread): first-class Azure, AWS, and GCP integrations, so a product on any of the three clouds measures live, satisfies V3 with its own storage, and is set up with least privilege. This extends the plan; it replaces nothing | WP7.2b (WP7.2b.1 read adapters, WP7.2b.2 media buckets, WP7.2b.3 setup and docs) added after WP7.2, built as three PRs to `dev`. No cloud SDK: each API is called with `net/http`, the AWS signer is WP4.2's `internal/proof/sigv4`, and the Google service-account JWT is signed with `crypto/rsa`, so CGO stays off and no dependency is added (THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md is unchanged). No Azure, AWS, or GCP account exists yet, so every fixture is synthetic and labelled so; the founder actions are listed in WP7.2b.3's getting-started pages |
| D-7.2b.1a | WP7.2b.1: live Azure, AWS, GCP reads for `grill` and `measure` returning WP5.2 tuples and the export readers' event shapes; tokens from Entra, SigV4, and Google; local CLI fallbacks; fixture and live tests | The providers name no baseline metrics. Azure has no endpoint that reports a token's scopes, but Azure Resource Manager lists the caller's effective actions on a resource; AWS reports a principal's permissions only through the IAM policy simulator, which the principal must be allowed to call; Google's `testIamPermissions` reports permissions held on a project or table. CloudWatch RUM's `GetAppMonitorData` serves only the last 30 days, and Logs Insights sees RUM events only when the app monitor copies them to CloudWatch Logs. A GA4 export's daily tables are named in the property's time zone. Baseline filters come from issue bodies (untrusted, §0.1). `aws`, `az`, and `gcloud` inherit the environment, which holds aayudha's own tokens | Packages `internal/integrations/azure`, `aws`, `gcp`, and the shared `cloud` (GitHub OIDC request, CLI runner, token cache). New `[integrations.<name>]` keys for the three clouds only: `auth` (`key` default, `oidc`, `cli`), `identity` (Entra client ID, IAM role ARN, or service account), `federation` (GCP workload identity provider), `region`; `org` is the Entra tenant and `project` the Application Insights or Log Analytics resource ID, the RUM app monitor, or `<project>.<dataset>`. With `oidc` or `cli` no secret is stored. Metrics `events` and `users` for each: Azure `AppEvents` (users = `UserAuthenticatedId`, else `UserId`), AWS RUM events counted by Logs Insights on `event_timestamp` (users = `user_details.userId`; the scan runs an hour past the window and the filter decides), GCP `events_*` on `event_timestamp` with the table suffix a day wider each side (users = `user_id`, else `user_pseudo_id`). The filter is a predicate in the provider's language; `;`, line breaks, and the language's pipe (`\|`, `\|>`) are refused before any request, and it ends its own line. Read-only checks before every data request: Azure `GET <resource>/providers/Microsoft.Authorization/permissions` (any action or data action that is not `*/read`, the two Log Analytics query actions, or `Microsoft.Support/*` is write-capable); AWS `sts:GetCallerIdentity` then `iam:SimulatePrincipalPolicy` for the four read actions and nineteen write actions (root credentials are always refused; a denied simulation leaves the credential reachable but unverified, and doctor says the configured scope is trusted); GCP project `testIamPermissions` for three reads and twelve writes, then the dataset's first table for table writes, and minted Google tokens carry only `bigquery.readonly` and `cloud-platform.read-only`. The read-only transport allows only each cloud's token and query POSTs (by host, path, and for AWS `X-Amz-Target`). `measure --live azure,aws,gcp` (new registry input) writes each pull as JSON lines in its export format next to the snapshot, reads it back through `internal/integrations/exports`, and records the cloud and exact query with its SHA-256 in the Result's sources; a RUM window older than 30 days is refused before any request. The CLI fallbacks run with only `PATH`, `HOME`, and the CLI's own configuration variables. Tests: `TestBaselinesGolden` (Azure, AWS, GCP recordings with the per-request bearer or a recomputed SigV4 signature; tuples written by hand), `TestCloudAuthModes` (key, oidc, cli for each cloud, with every token exchange checked parameter by parameter by `fixture.Clouds`, including the RS256 signature of the service-account JWT), `TestCloudWriteCapableRefused`, `TestCloudRefusedBeforeAnyCall`, `TestAWSSimulationDenied`, `TestMeasureLiveMatchesExports` (the export files' rows as API responses give the WP7.2 cohorts and North Star), `TestLiveCloud` (`AAYU_LIVE=1`, skips each cloud without its settings). Binary size grows by about 0.2 MB (linux/amd64, stripped) |
| D-7.2b.2a | WP7.2b.2: Azure Blob Storage (SAS or Entra token) and Google Cloud Storage (JSON API with a token, or the XML API with HMAC keys) as `[proof] media`, with WP4.2's purge, tombstones, and lifecycle | Azure's lifecycle rules live in the storage account's management policy, which only Azure Resource Manager serves; a SAS token reaches the blob endpoint only, and Resource Manager needs the subscription and resource group. Azure rule names are alphanumeric; GCS lifecycle rules carry no ID at all. The GCS XML API takes HMAC keys for objects but its lifecycle document differs from S3's, so the S3 code cannot manage it; HMAC keys are long-lived, which the GitHub OIDC setup avoids | `proof.Media` is the store interface (the S3 `Bucket`, `AzureBlob`, `GCS`), opened by `proof.OpenMedia` from the `[proof] media` value; credentials stay in `[proof] media_secret`, and the non-secret settings are URL parameters, so no config key is added. `azblob://<account>/<container>[/prefix]` with `auth=sas` (default; the secret is a SAS token), `key` (`<client-id>:<client-secret>`, `tenant=`), `oidc` (`tenant=`, `client=`; GitHub Actions federation), or `cli` (`az`); with an Entra credential plus `subscription=` and `group=`, aayudha keeps one rule `aayudhaproofretention<12 hex of the repository's SHA-256>` (delete block blobs under `<container>/<prefix>/proof/<owner>/<repo>/` after `retention_days`) in the account's management policy, merged with the account's other rules (a PUT replaces the policy; the last rule removed deletes it). With SAS, or without `subscription`/`group`, the push succeeds and reports `retention: lifecycle not managed by aayudha …` naming the rule to apply from `.aayudha/setup/azure/` (WP7.2b.3); `proof purge` still enforces retention. `gs://<bucket>[/prefix]` uses the JSON API with `auth=key` (default; a service account JSON key, RS256 JWT grant), `oidc` (`provider=`, `sa=`; STS and impersonation), or `cli` (`gcloud`), with the `devstorage.full_control` scope (buckets.patch needs it); aayudha's rule is the `Delete` rule whose `matchesPrefix` is exactly the repository's prefix, merged with the bucket's other rules. The HMAC XML API is not built: the token modes cover CI without long-lived keys, and an HMAC key would need a second lifecycle format. Both take `endpoint=` for emulators. The Entra and Google token exchanges moved into `internal/integrations/cloud` so the read adapters (WP7.2b.1) and the stores share them. `init --integrations` accepts `azblob://` and `gs://` bucket URLs. Tests: `TestPushVerifyPurgeBucket` now runs WP4.2's push, idempotent re-push, verify, tamper, purge, tombstone, and rule removal against S3, Azure Blob with an Entra app registration, Azure Blob with SAS, and GCS with a service account key (`internal/proof/fakecloud` checks every request's SAS or bearer; `fixture.Clouds` serves the Entra and Google token exchanges); `TestOpenMedia` covers the URL forms and refusals; `TestLiveBucket` takes any of the four schemes behind `AAYU_LIVE=1` and passed against Azurite 3 (SAS: push, get, verify, purge; lifecycle reported unmanaged) on 2026-10-12 |
| D-7.2b.3a | WP7.2b.3: `init --integrations` offers azure, aws, gcp with a cost/scope summary and `y/N`, writes `[integrations.<cloud>]`, prints and writes the least-privilege setup and the GitHub Actions OIDC federation under `.aayudha/setup/<cloud>/`; `doctor` verifies reachability and read-only scope, refusing write-capable credentials; a getting-started page per cloud through `tell docs --site` | Creating the cloud identities needs the founder's cloud accounts, which init does not hold and the plan does not give it (V1: init is the human's, and cloud admin rights are not GitHub rights). The identifiers the config needs (tenant, role ARN, workload identity provider) exist only after those steps. A committed config serves both GitHub Actions and the founder's machine, which have different credentials | init writes the section with `auth = "oidc"` and empty identifiers (doctor names each missing one), plus `.aayudha/setup/<cloud>/`, rendered for the repository and its default branch from templates embedded in the binary (`initrepo.SetupFiles`), and prints the cloud's `README.md`: Azure `federated-credential.json` (subject `repo:<owner>/<name>:ref:refs/heads/<default branch>`), `lifecycle-policy.json` (the proof-media rule for a SAS-only container), and `az` steps assigning Monitoring Reader on the component and Log Analytics Reader on its workspace; AWS `trust-policy.json` (the GitHub OIDC provider, audience `sts.amazonaws.com`, the same subject), `read-policy.json` (`logs:StartQuery` on the RUM log group, `logs:GetQueryResults`, `rum:GetAppMonitor` and `rum:GetAppMonitorData` on the monitor, and `iam:SimulatePrincipalPolicy` on the role itself), and `media-policy.json`; GCP `gcloud`/`bq` steps for `roles/bigquery.dataViewer` on the dataset, `roles/bigquery.jobUser`, a workload identity pool and provider whose attribute condition admits only the repository and branch, and `roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser`; every cloud a `workflow.yml` with `id-token: write`. Existing setup files are kept on a rerun, so init stays idempotent. Locally the founder overrides auth for one run (`AAYU_INTEGRATIONS__<CLOUD>__AUTH=cli`); the CLI login must itself be read-only (GCP impersonates the service account; AWS uses a profile that assumes the role; an Azure Owner login is refused). doctor needs no new check: its integrations row lists the three adapters and reports the WP7.2b.1 scope checks. Pages: `docs/getting-started-azure.md`, `-aws.md`, `-gcp.md` in a new "Clouds" sidebar group, each with the founder actions; `docs/getting-started.md` links them. Tests: `TestInitExtras` (golden setup files under `internal/cli/testdata/setup/`, JSON validity, printed steps, the config validating, idempotent rerun, `uninit --external` listing the three integrations). Founder actions (none can be done from here; no cloud account exists): Azure, an app registration with the federated credential and the two reader roles, plus optionally a storage account with a private container and a SAS token or an Entra identity with Storage Blob Data Contributor; AWS, the GitHub OIDC provider, the role with the trust and read policies, the app monitor's CloudWatch Logs copy, plus optionally an S3 bucket and an IAM user with the media policy; GCP, the read-only service account with its two roles, the workload identity pool and provider with the binding, plus optionally a bucket with public access prevention and a media service account; then fill each section's identifiers and add `id-token: write` to the jobs that run `aayu` |
| D-8.1d | WP4.8: `inspect` re-entry: `fix` is fixed in the PR, and after two `fix`/`rebuild` cycles the next run pages without a model call; the orchestrator's budget for WP8.1: one `inspect` per PR plus at most one fix cycle | `inspect` found a new P1 on each review of three stories: #130's seed check (plan and section fields, seed metadata and address literals, quoted local parts, Unicode domains), #132's request decoding (trailing input, case-insensitive and duplicate keys, a null `user_id`), and #133's simulation (the CLI truncating its output before validating, HR answers after the window). A PR that reaches the two-cycle cap can never pass `aayudha/inspect`, so `ship` cannot merge it | The capped PRs (#135 and #140 for #130, #151 for #132, #156 for #133) were closed unmerged with a comment naming the cap and their branches deleted; each story was rebuilt with `aayu build` from the then-current dev as a new PR (#142, #155, #158), with every finding fixed and a regression test for each. Story #131 (#148) and #134 (#160) each used one fix cycle. In all, the five stories took 17 `inspect` runs instead of the budgeted 10; the closed PRs stay on GitHub as the record. #156 also carried a justified `nosemgrep` on `math/rand/v2` (seeded, reproducible synthetic data), first written by another thread (e63db62) and kept in #158 |
| D-8.1e | WP8.1: the example is measured "from `grill goal` to `release`"; WP7.2 `measure` writes the Goal's Result from exports; WP7.8 `release` stamps stories released and schedules `measure` 28 days later; V3: synthetic data only | No real employees or analytics exist, and the orchestrator owns this repository's promotions, so `release` runs after this WP reports and its 28-day `measure` due date is in the future | Both exports are synthetic and reproducible from code: `examples/benefits-qa/analytics/simulate` drives 120 fictional employees per run (seeded) through the HR-ticket channel (baseline, 2026-08-10 to 2026-09-07) and through the product's real ask workflow (2026-09-14 to 2026-10-12); both files are committed and `TestExportsReproducible` regenerates them byte for byte. Goal #125 and Initiative #126 are measured on the product-channel export before the rolling snapshot, so their Result is the simulated outcome of the merged code, not a post-release window; the release's own `measure/due` record stays for a later run. When #133's rebuild left out two HR answers that fell after the baseline window, the export's SHA-256 changed but not the measured baseline (31 of 120, 0.258, since `measure` reads the half-open window); the Goal and Initiative bodies were updated to the new hashes and re-snapshotted with `aayu ready`. The Release issue #144 includes Features #127–#129 for the orchestrator's `aayu release` |
| D-8.1f | WP4.5: the web floor runs axe on every Completion Goal state; WP8.1: the example sends `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'` | axe-core is injected with `page.addScriptTag`, which a page's own CSP blocks, so the `a11y` floor could only be `blocked` on a page with a strict CSP | Fixed through #124 before the UI story: the web floor and review lane B set Playwright's `bypassCSP: true`; `TestWebFloorsWithBrowser` gained a strict-CSP fixture page (fails without the fix). A stale local `aayu` binary built before #124 later blocked #134's per-checkpoint verify twice on L09 (a never layer), which set `status:blocked`; the merge gate (authoritative) had passed every web floor. After rebuilding the binary, `aayu ready 134` (agent lane) restarted the budget, as `build` prescribes |
| D-8.1g | AGENTS.md: every GitHub write goes through `aayu`; the orchestrator: "Refs #70" in PR bodies | Story PRs are opened by `aayu build` with `Refs #<story>` first (`StoryOf` takes the first reference, so the gate and `ship` still prove the story). Closing a capped PR, deleting its branch, posting "Solo-mode bypass:" comments, editing PR titles and bodies and a Ready Goal's Baseline, and dispatching a workflow have no `aayu` verb | These writes were made with `gh` under the founder's account and are listed here for `audit` to reconcile: "Solo-mode bypass:" comments on the protected-path PRs (#121, #122) and on every story PR before `ship --solo-bypass`, and on stories #130, #131, #134 before `plan --solo-bypass` (D-8.1a); "Part of the WP8.1 dogfood. Refs #70" appended to each story PR's body; PR titles `pOST` corrected to `POST` (#151, #155, the build verb capitalises the first letter of the story title); #135, #140, #151, #156 closed with a comment and their branches deleted (D-8.1d); the Baseline of #125 and #126 (D-8.1e); and the `aayudha-model-lanes.yml` dispatches (D-8.1c) |
| D-8.1h | WP6.6: `review` lane A and lane B on a UI story, `polish` applies the critique; WP8.1: a UI story runs the `review` lane | `review` and `polish` need the `ai-gateway` key (D-8.1c), and `polish` re-reviews on the machine that holds the critique | Story #134 (PR #160) was reviewed twice through `aayudha-model-lanes.yml`. Run 38259017355 at a00dc9d: `fix`, lane B's impeccable `side-tab` (a coloured left border on a rounded card) and lane A's `target-size` (it estimated 38 px controls; the `targets` floor measured none under 44 px). The fix, made as `polish` would (the host applies the critique): tinted outcome cards with a full border, and controls at `--ay-size-row-height` (48 px). Run 38261559511 at 8e01a42: lane B `ship`; lane A reported one `contrast` finding (footer text "about 4.2:1"), which the deterministic evidence contradicts: `--ay-color-text-label` #4f5a6b on `--ay-color-background` #f5f6f8 is 6.46:1, and the merge gate's axe run on the same head found 0 serious `color-contrast` violations. `review` is not a ship gate, so #160 shipped on its green merge gate and `inspect` `merge`; this lane A finding is left on the PR's review comment for the founder |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP5.3b (grill as a conversation)

Rows are named by WP; `D-5.3b` was already taken by WP5.3's second row, so this WP's rows are `D-5.3b.a`, `D-5.3b.b`, ….

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-5.3b | WP5.3: question sets per type, asked in schema order; D-3.4c and D-5.3a–c: a skill must not invent requirements | Founder request (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): grilling should feel like discussing, ideating, and brainstorming with a colleague, asking as many questions as a vague, high-level idea needs and assessing the codebase, so "the bakery app needs high-quality UX" becomes a concrete, grounded, realistic spec agents deliver without redo. This extends WP5.3 in place; it replaces nothing | WP5.3b added after WP5.3 (M). The host's model reasons and converses; the binary supplies the facts (`--assess`) and the checks (grill, ready, evals). The scope guard is kept and made auditable instead of loosened: proposals are free in conversation, criteria need the human's confirmation, recorded per criterion |
| D-5.3b.a | WP5.3b: a deterministic, read-only fact pass the host consumes | No convention tells a binary which code an idea touches, and "touched" cannot be decided by rule. GitHub search would be a second network dependency; MCP tools must not write | New registry inputs `grill --assess` (bool) and `--idea` (string); `internal/grill.Assess` reads only the checkout (skipping `.git`, `node_modules`, `vendor`, `testdata`, build output; at most 20 000 files, 512 KiB each) and, when a client exists, one page of the repository's `type:story` issues. Relevance is the idea's own words (stop words and the qualifiers removed, stemmed) against route paths, file paths, event names, and story text; when no route matches, every route is listed and the scope line says so. Routes: Next.js `app/**/page.*` and `route.*` (route groups and slots dropped) and `pages/**`, SvelteKit `routes/**/+page.svelte` and `+server.*`. A screen has a state when a convention file (`loading.*`, `error.*`, `empty.*`, `offline.*`) is in or above its directory, or a marker (`isLoading`, `error`, `isEmpty`, `navigator.onLine`, …) is in its own files; a missing state is a `gap:<state>:<flow>` fact listing the screens. Other facts: `flow`, `route`, `component` (used on the matched screens or in a `ui`/design-system directory), `ds` (UI libraries in `package.json`, a local design-system package), `tokens` (CSS custom properties in token files, Tailwind config, `design/brand.yaml`), `test`, `event` (`capture`, `track`, `logEvent`, and `gtag('event', …)` calls), `code`, `story:#N` with its first criteria, `floor` (a11y, Lighthouse, k6, platforms from config), and `layer`. Facts are sorted and carry no timestamps, so the same checkout gives the same report. Every text is one cleaned line; the text report puts all facts in one `<untrusted source="repo">` block and the JSON carries an `untrusted` note. No GitHub client or no answer is a note, not a failure. `grill` is a read verb, so `--assess` is exposed over MCP; the `grill` prompt takes `idea` and, for a story or bug, embeds the same report as untrusted data. These are heuristics: a fact names its evidence (file, marker rule) so the human can correct it in the conversation |
| D-5.3b.b | WP5.3b: nothing enters the spec unless the human confirmed it; record confirmations so it is auditable which criteria came from whom | A work item has no field for open questions, parked ideas, or sources, and an unknown section fails Parse. The binary cannot see the conversation, so it cannot verify a confirmation it was not shown | Story and Bug gain four optional Ready fields (schema v1, additive, so older bodies still parse): Assumptions, Open questions (`answered:` or `deferred:` per line), Parked ideas (`<name>: …`, out of scope), and Provenance: `Request: "<the human's words>"`, `Vague: "<qualifier>" → criteria N, M` or `→ deferred: <why>`, and one `N. stated\|confirmed\|default (<dimension>): "<the human's words>"; facts: <id>, …` per criterion. The issue forms regenerate (and this repository's copies in `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` are refreshed). `grill.Check` (grill and `ready`): when Provenance is present, every criterion has exactly one source with a quote, every qualifier of the Request is resolved to existing criteria or deferred with a `deferred:` open question, Assumptions is not empty, every open question is answered or deferred, and no parked idea's name appears in the Completion Goal. `grill --idea` requires Provenance, its Request must quote the idea, and every cited fact must be in the checkout's assessment. Whether the human really said the quoted words is checked where the conversation is visible: in the evals (D-5.3b.d); in a host the readback the human confirms is the check, and the issue body (hashed at Ready) is the audit record. Case scope rules no longer read the spec record (they guard criteria; the record is where unconfirmed ideas belong). The guard (`ready --guard`) and the Ready checks inside `build`, `verify`, `plan`, and `release` do not apply the new rules, so Ready already granted is never withdrawn by them |
| D-5.3b.c | WP5.3b: Ready validation flags remaining vague words in criteria; catalogues as data that grow per domain | "Resolved" is a judgement; a rule can only approximate it | Six catalogues (`ux`, `perf`, `simple`, `sec`, `scale`, `rel`), parsed strictly by `grill.LoadCatalog` (a heading with the domain ID, a `Qualifiers:` list, and `### <Name> (<domain>.<id>)` dimension headings (the ID in backticks) each with Ask, Default, Evidence, and Facts); a malformed catalogue is an error. Qualifiers match whole words, case-insensitively, hyphen or space ("high-quality" = "high quality"), longest first, never inside quotes or backticks (UI copy and paths are literal). The rule: a criterion (state and expected) or Threshold that contains a qualifier and no digit outside quotes is refused with the catalogue to use; "fast: p95 under 200 ms" and "clean at 390 px and 1280 px" pass. The UX catalogue keeps accessibility "beyond the floors only if asked" and performance budgets as a confirmation, not a default criterion, so the D-5.3c floor rule stands |
| D-5.3b.d | WP5.3b evals: a bakery "high-quality UX" case on a synthetic repository fixture and a non-UX case; tier 1 deterministic; tier 2 rubric on question quality, concreteness, and fidelity with a scripted human; group `skills` | WP3.4's tier 2 is one skill reply per run. A scripted human cannot follow free-form questions by their wording. The `skills` group's 6 USD cap and the job's 60 minutes fit single replies only | Fixture `evals/cases/grill/testdata/bakery` (a never-built Next.js ordering app: a five-screen `/order` flow whose pay screen has no error state, `/menu/search` over a 40-product seed, tokens, components, events, an e2e test, LAYERS.md, Lighthouse budgets, no k6 script). Cases `grill/bakery-ux` and `grill/search-fast` record the conversation (`output/transcript.json`) and replay `grill story --assess`, `grill story --idea … --create`, and `ready --check` in a copy of the fixture (new case keys `repo`, `transcript`, `cite`; `[tier2] human`). The scripted human (`human.toml`) answers by the catalogue dimension the skill tags each question with (`confirm`, `default`, `park`, or `defer`); any other topic gets a fallback that parks it, so an unscripted idea can never become a requirement. Tier 2 runs the skill turn by turn (JSON turns: `say`, up to five tagged `questions`, `done`, `draft`) with the request and the fixture's assessment as untrusted data; after `max_rounds` the human asks for the spec and a skill still asking scores 1. Deterministic floors before the judge (rule 7), shared with tier 1 (`evals.Concrete`): Ready, `grill.Check`, cited facts exist, at least `cite` criteria cite one, the Request is the human's, every criterion's quote occurs in the request or a reply, its dimension was answered `confirm` or `default` (a `default` source only where the human accepted one), and the case's scope rules. The judge (gpt-6.1-sol; the skill is claude-opus-5-5) sees the whole conversation with each reply's kind and the spec. Results keep each conversation run's exchanges and final criteria (`transcript`). `skills` `max_usd` 6 → 20 and the tier-2 job's timeout 60 → 90 minutes. TestTier1GrillConversation (each defect class fails with its reason), TestTier2GrillConversation (turns, answers, untrusted wrapping, a parked topic scored 1 without the judge, a skill that never finishes) |
| D-5.3b.e | WP5.3b: works in the CLI TUI, the MCP prompt, and the host skill | The TUI runs without a model, so it cannot reflect, propose, or brainstorm | The TUI asks the idea first, runs the assessment, and inserts one question per dimension of each qualifier with its default and up to three facts; an answer may be `default`, `park`, or `defer`. The Completion Goal question shows what the human said. The human writes every criterion, so each is recorded `stated (interview)`; accepted defaults become Assumptions, `defer` an open question, `park` a parked idea, and an unsettled qualifier is deferred with an open question. The review screen is the readback ("Here is what done looks like …", also returned as `readback` by `grill --json` for the host). Brainstorming is the host skill's job (its "How a grill goes" and "Voice"); the MCP prompt carries the same instructions and, with `idea`, the assessment |
| D-5.3b.f | WP5.3b accept: tier 2 on both conversation cases meets the WP3.4 bar; the founder's instruction: dispatch tier 2 once after merge, fix once if it fails, then stop | The dispatch (run 38681664301) was cancelled unrun: `evals.yml`'s concurrency group keeps one pending run per ref, and later pushes and another thread's dispatch replaced it. The push run on the merge commit, 38681630586 on 30fb832 (every `skills` case, 16.18 USD), is the tier-2 result: `grill/bakery-ux` 2, 2, 3, 5, 3 (mean 3.0) and `grill/search-fast` 1, 1, 5, 5, 1 (mean 2.6); the five older cases passed (suite pass^5 0.71, mean 4.26). The three search floors were the harness's, not the skill's: the skill quoted the human exactly but joined two replies (`"…" / "…"`, `"… …"`) or added a second source on the line, and the provenance parser read one quote only. The bakery scores came from the fixture: the scripted human never said how paying works, so the skill's good question ("does `/order/review` take payment today, or only submit the order?") got the fallback and payment was parked against the confirmed "review and pay"; and `ux.consistency` suggested `screenshot` evidence for "only existing tokens and components", which the judge rightly said a screenshot cannot show. The judge found the questions grounded, prioritized, and within five per round in every bakery run | The one fix: a Provenance source line may quote several replies, each quoted segment (split at an ellipsis) checked on its own against what the human said; the skill says so. `ux.consistency` evidence is `metric` (0 values outside the tokens and 0 new components over the changed files). The fixture's pay screen charges a card through `lib/payments.ts` (a seeded test card is declined, `event:payment_declined`) and the bakery human's task answer says they pay by card as today; the fallback reads "That is not part of what I asked for; park it." The recorded run follows (criterion 2 quotes two replies; criterion 6 is a metric). The results file `evals/results/30fb832…-skills.json` is committed as the record of that run, with each conversation's exchanges; it is not a baseline. The tier-2 run after this fix is reported to the orchestrating thread; per the instruction there is no further fix loop |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP1.1b (CLI experience)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-1.1b | WP1.1: help prints `aayu`, output is plain text through an `io.Writer`; V7 fixes the plumbing list at 15 commands | Founder request (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): "The CLI commands should be fun to use", with a home screen, `aayu next`, seat voices, colour, live progress, celebrations, a guided first run, and shell completions, without costing correctness or accessibility. This extends the plan; it replaces nothing | WP1.1b, one PR to `dev`. `aayu` with no command is the home screen (`aayu --help` and `aayu help` still print the usage), and the registry gains three plumbing commands after V7's fifteen: `next` (Engineering Manager), `tour` (Founder), and `completion` (DevOps & DevEx Tinkerer), so the RACI, README, and charter regions regenerate (the charters are a protected path). One dependency: `charm.land/lipgloss/v2` v2.0.6 (MIT), which moves ultraviolet, `x/ansi`, go-colorful, and go-runewidth to the versions in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md; the stripped linux/amd64 binary grows from 29.8 MB to 30.2 MB |
| D-1.1b | WP1.1b: "fun never costs correctness"; persona data "from the parallel persona work, not duplicated"; "every error ending with the exact next command"; golden tests per mode | The persona work (WP5.1b, merged while this was built) gives each twin a name, a purpose, and signature questions, but no intro or sign-off line. A celebration needs to know what is a first. The status table has 13 columns, wider than a terminal. Hosts cannot afford a GitHub call on every status-line refresh | `internal/ui` resolves each stream's level: Machine (a pipe, `--json`, `AAYU_AGENT=1` and so MCP, `--ci` or `CI`) adds nothing to a command's existing text; Plain (`--plain`, `[ui] plain`, `AAYU_PLAIN=1`, `TERM=dumb`) is ASCII words only; Rich adds symbols, motion, and colour (none with `NO_COLOR`; themes `auto`, `saffron`, `contrast`, `mono` under `[ui] theme`). Results go to stdout; the seat's intro and sign-off, live progress, and celebrations go to stderr, so `aayu status > file` holds only the result. A status keeps its word (PASS, WARN, FAIL, N/A) at every level. The seat voice reads the seat's `persona.toml` (WP5.1b, `seats.Persona`) from the embedded assets: the twin's name, its purpose up to the first colon as the intro, and one of its signature questions, picked by a hash of the command name so it is stable, as the sign-off; a seat without a persona falls back to the first clause of its charter's `## Purpose` and has no sign-off. Milestones are recorded once per checkout in `.aayudha/state/milestones.json` (first Ready story, first proven ship, Level 1); every release celebrates. Errors print `next:` with the command the error itself names in backticks, else one for its exit code (`<command> --help` for usage, `aayu doctor` for external errors, `aayu next` when blocked, `aayu verify` for a floor miss, `aayu status` for budget), and `--json` errors gain `next`; `ready`, `build`, and `tour` name their own. `status` and `doctor` show a person a seven-column table and a status table with a tally; machines keep the markdown table and the tab-aligned rows. The home screen reads GitHub with the human token (Ready, in-progress, and blocked story labels; up to ten open story PRs, their reviews, and their check runs, using `ship`'s gate checks), local packs, and `measure/due/` on the local `origin/ledger`, and caches itself in `.aayudha/state/journey.json` for host status lines. `verify` runs its floors one at a time to show each (the report is the one `verify.Execute` builds). The effective config gains `[ui]`, so the audit fixture's config hash changed. Tests: `TestHomeModes` (nine goldens), `TestNextForAgentsAndScripts`, `TestHomeOutsideARepoAndWithoutGitHub`, `TestErrorsEndWithNext`, `TestVoiceReadsThePersona`, `TestTour`, `TestCompletionScripts`, `internal/ui` level, mark, progress, and table tests, and `internal/journey` `TestNext` |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP3.2b (native host plugins)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. Formats were read from each host's own
documentation on 2026-10-15 and checked against the host CLIs named in the rows.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-3.2b | WP3.2: three hosts (Amp, Cursor, Claude Code), project-level copies and adapters, and an Amp plugin stub that only runs the hooks | Founder request (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): plugins that feel native in Claude Code, OpenAI Codex, and Amp, with Cursor still working, under the same safety rules. This extends the plan; it replaces nothing | WP3.2b, one PR to `dev`: Codex as a fourth host in the registry (`codex`, "OpenAI Codex"), a marketplace plugin for Claude Code and Codex at the repository root, a real Amp plugin, and the Claude Code status line. The delegation thread's host-neutral slash commands go in `hosts/commands/` beside `home.md` and `next.md`, which every plugin loads |
| D-3.2b | WP3.2: every hook and MCP registration runs the absolute, version-checked binary; "look up each host's current extension format" | Sources: Claude Code [plugins reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins-reference), [marketplaces](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugin-marketplaces), [skills and commands](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/slash-commands), [status line](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/statusline); Codex [skills](https://developers.openai.com/codex/skills), [MCP](https://developers.openai.com/codex/mcp), [hooks](https://developers.openai.com/codex/hooks), [plugins](https://developers.openai.com/plugins/build/plugins); Amp [plugin API](https://ampcode.com/docs/plugin-api) and `amp plugins show-docs`. A marketplace plugin is the same files on every machine, so it cannot carry a machine's binary path. Plugins cannot set Claude Code's main `statusLine` (only `subagentStatusLine`). Codex reads `.agents/skills/` and `AGENTS.md`, loads a project's `.codex/config.toml` only when the project is trusted, and runs `.codex/hooks.json` hooks only after the user trusts them (`/hooks`); its repo marketplace path, `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json`, is a protected path here. Codex 0.162.1 also reads `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` and installs a Claude-format plugin from it, loading its skills, commands (as `source-command-<name>`), and MCP server. Codex exports `CODEX_THREAD_ID` to the commands it runs (found in the 0.162.1 binary, not documented). No host sets `AAYU_AGENT=1` for the shell commands its agent runs, so a human-only verb's refusal could not rely on it | The marketplace plugin's root is the repository (`"source": "./"`), so its skills are `skills/` itself with no copy; its hooks run `aayu hook <name> --skills <version>` and its MCP server `aayu mcp --skills <version>`, from `PATH`: the server refuses to start (exit 5, `next: aayu self-update`) and `session-start` reports when this binary carries other skills, and a hook without `--host` detects the host (`CLAUDECODE`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`). `aayu install` keeps the absolute, version-checked path for every file it writes; the Amp plugin also checks at load that the binary reports the version install recorded and disables its commands otherwise. Use the plugin or `install` for a host, not both. `install --host codex` writes a block between `# BEGIN aayudha` and `# END aayudha` in `.codex/config.toml` (text outside it is kept, a product-owned `[mcp_servers.aayudha]` is left alone with a note) and `.codex/hooks.json` (`SessionStart`, `PreToolUse`; Codex blocks on exit 2 like Claude Code). `install --host claude` sets `statusLine` to `aayu hook status-line --host claude` unless the product has its own; `status-line` prints one line from the cached home screen (`.aayudha/state/journey.json`, D-1.1b) and never calls GitHub. `pre-tool` now reads the event the host sends (Claude Code and Codex `tool_input.command`, Cursor `command`) and blocks a shell command that runs a verb the MCP server never exposes (`decide`, `ship`, `release`, `respond`, `init`, `install --apply`), any human-only input, or `resume`, `self-update`, `tour`, `seat`, `uninit`; Amp's palette offers none of them. The Amp plugin registers Home, Next step, Status, Doctor, and six agent verbs (grill, ready, plan, build, verify, inspect) that ask the thread to run the skill, plus every shared command but home and next, which install writes into the plugin. Codex model lanes: build, spec, and plan GPT-6.1 Sol (escalation GPT-6 Astra at high effort), review Claude Opus 5.5 (another family than the builder) with Grok 4.7 as the alternative, ui-review Gemini 3.1 Pro, chore Haiku 5.5. Uncertain: Codex's handling of `disable-model-invocation` in a Claude-format skill, and whether the Codex IDE extension loads repo marketplaces as the CLI does; Cursor has no headless way to list what it loaded, so its smoke test is the golden tree. Tests: `TestGoldenTrees` (codex-unix, codex-windows), `TestCodexConfigKeepsTheProductsConfig`, `TestHumanOnlyCommand`, `TestPreToolBlocksHumanOnlyShell`, `TestPluginManifests`, `TestPluginHooksAndStatusLine`, and `TestHostPluginsLoad` (passed with Claude Code 2.1.300, Codex 0.162.1, and Amp 0.0.1792022479 on 2026-10-15; skips a host whose CLI is absent, as in CI) |
| D-5.3b.g | WP5.3b: after D-5.3b.f, the founder (2026-10-15) accepted the results and asked for one more round, then stop: per-topic scripted answers, a skill that never decides an unconfirmed detail, a narrower caching rule, the conversation cases in their own advisory group, and dispatches that a push cannot replace | Run 38687921977 (after D-5.3b.f) showed three causes. The scripted human answered by dimension, so a follow-up on the same dimension got the same reply and every `request` or `other` clarification ("depend on #14?", "split this L story?") was parked. The skill decided details the human left open (the confirmation on `/order/review`) and once tagged a criterion with a dimension it never asked about. The search case's `\bcach` rule also matched "cold cache", a test condition. And `evals.yml`'s one concurrency group per ref let later pushes replace a pending dispatch, twice | Topics: every catalogue dimension lists `Topics` (kebab keys, parsed strictly), and the skill tags each question `<dimension>/<topic>`, a request topic (`request/dependency`, `request/size`, `request/existing-behaviour`, `request/parent`, `request/scope`), or `other/<key>`; the turn schema requires the topic. `human.toml` has `[topics."<dimension>/<key>"]` answers, `[dimensions."<dimension>"]` fallbacks, and the global fallback (park or defer); the bakery human now answers where the confirmation goes, where options go, the #14 dependency, the story's size, and existing payment, and the search human a cold cache. Provenance names the topic (`(ux.steps/screen-count)`); a criterion from a topic or dimension never asked fails tier 1 and the tier-2 floor. Placements: `grill.Check` (so `grill`, `ready`, and both tiers) refuses a grilled criterion whose expected result places something (a backticked route, "on the review screen", "above", "below", "next to", "at the top of", …) unless that word is in the criterion's quoted human words or the request; the skill says never to decide where something appears, what it is called, its order, or a number without the human, and to defer or park it instead. The search rule now matches result caching only (`cache … results`, `results cach…`, `cache per query`, memoizing); the recorded search run measures with a cold cache and passes (TestGrillSearchScopeCaching). Group `conversations` (the two cases, 20 USD, `gate = "advisory"` until a run meets the bar; like brief, not on pushes to `dev`); `skills` back to 6 USD and no longer holds them. `evals.yml`: a dispatch's concurrency group carries its run ID, so a push or schedule cannot replace it; pushes and the schedule keep the shared per-ref group and `cancel-in-progress: false`, so a running promotion run is never cut short. Tests: TestTier1GrillConversation (unasked topic, unasked dimension, unconfirmed placement), TestTier2GrillConversation (answers by topic, dimension, fallback), TestTopicsAndPlacements, TestCheck, TestGroups. The founder's instruction is one round and then stop, whatever the dispatched result |
| D-5.3b.h | WP5.3b and D-5.3b.g: placement checks refuse a criterion whose place word is not in its quoted human words; WP3.4: tier 2 runs nightly, after relevant merges to `dev`, on promotion, and on dispatch | Run 38692692002 scored search-fast 4.8 and bakery-ux 2.8: both 1s were placement refusals of criteria that said "on that screen", "on the same screen", or "on the review screen" without quoting a reply naming it. The founder (2026-10-15) kept `grill.Check` strict and asked the skill to quote the reply instead, and asked that the advisory `conversations` and `personas` groups (about 13 and 20 USD a run) stop running nightly: on demand, and on pushes to `dev` or pull requests that change grill or persona files. Tier 2 needs the `ai-gateway` environment, which only `dev`, `rolling`, and `main` may use, so it cannot run on a pull request | Skill only: `skills/grill/SKILL.md` (outside the persona block) gains "Placement quotes": a criterion that says where something is quotes every human reply naming that place, even one given to another question (`"… on the same screen" / "… on the review screen"`), names the place instead of "that screen" or "this page", and leaves an unconfirmed place out with a `deferred:` open question; `references/story.md` and `bug.md` say the same. `TestCheck` adds a criterion whose placements come from two quoted replies (accepted) and one that says "on that screen" (refused). `conversations` and `personas` get `events = ["workflow_dispatch", "push"]`, so the schedule never runs them; `evals.yml` runs them on a push only to `dev` and only when the push changes their files: for conversations `skills/grill/`, `evals/cases/grill/`, `internal/grill/`, `internal/evals/conversation.go`; for personas `seats/*/persona.toml`, `internal/seats/persona.go` (it renders the persona blocks), `evals/cases/retro/` and `decide/`, `internal/evals/persona.go`. Pushes to `rolling` and `main` skip them too (neither gates). A pull request gets tier 1 in `go test`; its tier 2 runs on the push to `dev` that merges it. `brief`, `brief-plain`, and `skills` keep their schedule. Test: TestGroups (events of both groups) |

### Deviations recorded while fixing Bugs #170–#173, #177, and #183 (limits found while writing How it works)

Rows are named by the WP whose behaviour each Bug corrects.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-7.6d | WP7.6 accept: re-running audit with the same recorded inputs produces a byte-identical report; WP1.9: files an earlier binary wrote stay readable | Bug #183: the inputs snapshot recorded `config_hash` over the whole effective configuration, and `--replay` refused any difference, so every new key ([team] in WP5.1b, [ui] in WP1.1b) made every earlier report unreplayable (exit 4), though audit reads none of them. A whole-configuration hash cannot be recomputed by a binary with another configuration schema | New reports record `audit-config:sha256:<…>`, the hash of only what `audit` and `retro` read: `[identity] bot_logins`, `[decisions] sla_hours`, `[time]`, `[autonomy] correction_days`, `never`, `streak`, `[proof] media`, `media_secret`, `retention_days`, `[budget] per_story_usd`, and the dev branch; a replay refuses (exit 4) when that differs. A legacy `sha256:` hash is not recomputed: the replay renders the recorded hash, warns on stderr that it replays with the current values of those sections, and the byte comparison with the report is the check. Tested by `TestAuditReplaysRecordedReports` (the 12 October 06:35 and 15 October 04:35 UTC reports replay byte-identically from `internal/cli/testdata/audit-replay`: this repository's ledger up to the 15 October report as a 300 kB git bundle, with the config and `LAYERS.md` the replay reads) and `TestAuditRerunByteIdentical` (a `[ui] theme` change leaves the replay byte-identical; a `[decisions] sla_hours` change refuses it). A key audit starts reading must join `auditConfigHash` |
| D-1.6b | WP1.6 and D-1.6a: the `[budget]` breaker trips at `per_story_usd` or `per_day_usd` | Bug #177: `Breaker.Seed` was never called, so every `aayu` process started at zero and the limits bounded one process's spend; only the pause file carried a trip across processes. The actions log is per clone (`.aayudha/telemetry/actions.jsonl`, shared by its worktrees), and a CI run starts from a fresh clone | `Env.Gateway` seeds the breaker from the clone's actions log through `Breaker.SeedLog` before any call: every logged record with a cost counts toward its story and its local day in `[time] tz`, so per-day counts today's records and per-story the story's. A CI run's breaker still sees only that run's spend, and a host's own model spend inside a skill stays invisible to it (docs/guide/how-models.md). Tested by `TestBudgetBreakerAcrossProcesses` (two `aayu triage` child processes, each the test binary run as the CLI, sharing one state directory and calling a fake model endpoint through `[models.gateway]` that reports each call's cost: 0.60 + 0.60 USD on one day trips `per_day_usd = 1` with exit 6, the pause file, and one page in the `file` outbox; on one story it trips `per_story_usd = 1`; 0.90 USD yesterday does not count today) and the `TestBudgetBreaker` cases for a missing log, a malformed one, and one that cannot be read (a directory) |
| D-4.8c | WP4.8, D-4.8a, and WP4.8b: `inspect` reviews with a model whose family ≠ the builder's; the builder's family from the delegation record, else the pack's `model` (the `Aayudha-Model` trailer), else the build lane of the pack's host | Bug #171: the trailer is written into the PR's own commits, so for every PR not delegated the different-family rule rested on what the builder said about itself, and a hand-made commit can carry any trailer. The actions log is per clone and absent from the merge gate's fresh checkout. A host-managed lane (Amp) records no model. The first fix, written before inspect v2 (#186) merged, was reworked onto it | `build` writes each checkpoint's model into its ledger record (`CheckpointRecord.Model`). `inspect` lists the PR's commits and calls `inspect.BuilderOf` with the delegation record and the checkpoint models: the family is known only when a delegation covers the PR, or a checkpoint names a model family for every commit, the records name one family, and the trailer, if any, agrees. Otherwise it is unknown, and the records', the trailer's, and (with no record) the build lane's families are candidates; `reviewerModels` (WP4.8b) takes the candidates, so the first reviewer and the peer are each of none of them, or inspect exits 4. The verdict's first note names the family and its source, marks the trailer "self-reported, unverified", and calls a family from a build record "attributed, not verified": the record holds what `build` resolved (`AAYU_MODEL` or the lane), and without a bot App the founder's token, which agents use here, can write the ledger. inspect's first review of this fix (cycle 1) found both: an agreeing builder-controlled record presented as verified, and a checkpoint without a model counted as covering its commit. Tested by `TestInspectBuilderFamily` (a trailer naming anthropic over a record naming openai is reviewed by `grok-4.7`; no record names the lane's family; an honest PR is reviewed by `gpt-6.1-sol` with the record as source), `TestBuilderOf` (partial and model-less records, two families, delegation, a trailer contradicting a delegation), and the checkpoint model assertion in `TestBuildTextDryRunPlanAndBudget` |
| D-5.7b | WP5.7 and D-5.7a: `aayu halt` sets a repository variable every workflow checks; every job in every aayudha workflow starts with the halt step | Bug #173: `floors.yml`, `inspect-live.yml`, and `screen-reader.yml` had no halt step, and neither had the merge gate's `aayudha/inspect` and `aayudha/pack` jobs (in this repository and in the `init --gates` template), which D-5.7a counted as covered; nothing checked the rule, so a new workflow could drop it unnoticed | Those six jobs (and the template's two) start with the same halt step as the rest. `TestEveryWorkflowJobStartsWithHaltCheck` (repository root, run by CI's `build` job) parses every `.github/workflows/*.yml` and `internal/initrepo/*.yml` and fails naming each workflow and job whose first step is not the halt check (name `Halt check…`, `if: vars.AAYUDHA_HALT != ''`, a script that exits), then runs each halt script in bash with the variable set and requires a failing exit and the halt message. It covers `.yaml` files too. Live: on 2026-10-15 the three workflows from this branch ran on a scratch repository (`rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-halt-1792045632`, created and deleted with the admin token, since the default token cannot create repositories): with `AAYUDHA_HALT` set every job (floors-web, floors-dast, inspect-live, both screen-reader readers) failed at the halt check and skipped every later step; after it was deleted the halt check was skipped and every job succeeded. The jobs API records are in `testdata/workflow-halt`, and `TestHaltedWorkflowRuns` checks them against the current workflows (inspect's first review asked for this evidence) |
| D-7.1b | WP4.2: retention TTL from config and `aayu proof purge`; WP7.1 and D-7.1a: loops honour `halt` and `[budget]`, and `init --gates` renders a product's loops when it has `loops/` | Bug #172: no loop, workflow, or template ran `proof purge`, so media retention (V3, personal data) depended on a person. A product without `loops/` got no loops at all. Purging writes the ledger, so in Actions it needs the bot App (D1), and a bucket purge needs the media credential, which no loop job received | A default loop, `loops.Defaults["proof-purge"]` (`command = ["proof", "purge"]`, daily at 03:40 in `[time] tz`, `bot = true`): `init --gates` writes `loops/proof-purge.toml` into a checkout that lacks it and renders it with the checkout's loops, so every product gets the Actions job, the Amp schedule, and the Cursor automation; aayudha commits the same file, so `go generate` renders it here. A new loop key `secrets` names aayu secrets the Actions job passes as `AAYU_SECRET_<NAME>` (the default passes `proof-media`; a product whose `[proof] media_secret` differs edits it). Each run starts with `aayu hook loop:proof-purge` (halt exits 4, the pause file and the minutes budget exit 6) and `proof purge` refuses while halted too; retention is `[proof] retention_days`. Without an App the Actions job skips with a notice, as the `decisions` loop does, so on this repository only a host schedule can run it. Media behind an `oidc` bucket credential (D-7.2b.2a) cannot be purged by the Actions job, which has no `id-token: write`; a host schedule or the bucket's lifecycle rule covers it. Tested by `TestInitGatesRendersProofPurge` (a scratch checkout without `loops/`, and this repository's generated workflow) and `TestLoopProofPurge` (the job's guard and command as the bot against a fake S3: a June pack's screenshot deleted and its pack tombstoned, an October pack untouched, and, halted, exit 4 at the guard and the command with nothing purged) |

### Deviations recorded while fixing the stable gate's cross-OS smoke tests (before v0.1.0)

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-0.4g | WP0.4 and D-0.4a: the stable gate's `smoke` job runs every unit test on Ubuntu, macOS, and Windows | Stable gate run 38742897657 on `dev` failed on macOS and Windows. Three product defects: `status --work` compared `git worktree list`'s resolved, forward-slash paths with the unresolved checkout, so it listed no worktree on macOS (`/var` is `/private/var`) or Windows (a WP5.5b regression); the floor sandbox's `lookPath` accepted only `.exe` on Windows, so a tool installed as a `.cmd` shim (npm's `lighthouse` and `impeccable`) never resolved, and `node_modules/.bin/<tool>` resolved to the POSIX script; and the ledger lock treated Windows' "access denied" on a lock file another writer is deleting as an error, so concurrent writers failed. The rest were tests that assumed Unix: `syscall.Kill`, shell stand-in tools without a Windows extension, Unix permission bits, `/`-separated or drive-less paths, LF in a `core.autocrlf` worktree, a Windows path in a TOML basic string, and case-sensitive variable names. `internal/cli` is not hung on Windows: it takes about 880 s (`TestReleaseFailsClosed` 336 s), since git starts about ten times slower than on Linux, past `go test`'s 10-minute default | Product: `status --work` resolves both sides (`filepath.EvalSymlinks`, which also expands 8.3 names such as `RUNNER~1`); floor tools resolve through `exec.LookPath` per PATH entry, so PATHEXT applies on Windows; the ledger lock retries on "access denied" on Windows until its timeout. The smoke job runs `go test -timeout 30m`. Tests take the OS's paths and line endings, and Windows stand-in tools are `.cmd` files that run the shell script with Git for Windows' `sh`. Not supported on Windows, by test and here: `examples/benefits-qa/deploy/serve.sh` is POSIX `sh` that tracks the app by Unix pid, so its tests are `//go:build unix`; the benefits-qa interrupt test sends itself SIGINT, which Windows cannot (Ctrl+C reaches the same `signal.NotifyContext`), so it is Unix only. Different on Windows, asserted only on Unix: the age secrets file and identity have no `0600` mode, they take their directory's ACL (under the user profile: the user, SYSTEM, and Administrators), and aayu sets no ACL, so a `HOME` or `XDG_*` directory outside the profile shares its access; a floor's child sees one of `HTTPS_PROXY` and `https_proxy`, since names are case-insensitive; and an actions log path under a file reads as missing (Windows reports the path not found), as it does when the log is absent |
| D-0.4h | D-0.4f: with the `live-tests` token, the stable gate's `extended-integration` job runs every live test green | Run 38754333645 on `dev` failed two live tests that pass from a workstation with the same classic token; re-running only that job passed both, so neither was the CI credential. `TestLive` (internal/github) counted on GitHub's default labels for a second page of five, and a new repository sometimes has none yet. `TestReleaseLive`'s `release --to main` landed `main`, then GitHub refused the `v0.1.0` tag push with a bare `[remote rejected] v0.1.0 -> v0.1.0 (failed)`. The "Bypassed rule violations … creations being restricted" lines above it are GitHub's notice that the admin role's bypass was used, not the refusal. `(failed)` carries no reason; it is the answer GitHub gives for a lost race on a ref, which the ledger writer already retries (D-2.4a), and the re-run's tag push succeeded, so it was transient. This repository's `release-tags` ruleset matches the test's (creation, update, deletion, non-fast-forward; bypass: the admin role, always), and the founder's token reports `current_user_can_bypass: always`, so v0.1.0's tag push is admitted the same way | `TestLive` creates six labels, more than a page without defaults (`testdata/smoke.json` re-recorded). Every `release` push (candidate, landing, tag, candidate deletion; each idempotent) is retried up to three times, a poll interval apart, when the remote answers `(failed)` or `cannot lock ref`; any other refusal fails at once as before. `TestReleaseRetriesALockedRef`: a pre-receive hook leaves the tag's ref locked for the first push only, and `release --to rolling` still lands and tags |

### Scope change and deviations recorded while implementing WP4.2b (least-friction proof, 0.2)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-4.2b | V1 and §0.1: CI acts as a bot (App token) and writes packs to `ledger`; V3: proof media in Git LFS on private repositories, in a private bucket on public ones; M4: "ledger ops with LFS"; WP1.7: LFS quota; WP2.5 (V3 enforcement): `proof push` refuses media on a public repository until a bucket is set; WP2.6: `--integrations` offers the bucket for V3; WP4.1: artifacts `lfs\|bucket`; WP4.2: LFS by extension, `proof purge`, tombstones, CI packs authoritative; WP4.8: the merge gate "writes the authoritative pack to `ledger`"; WP7.2b.2: buckets with purge and tombstones; R5, R11, J2-1, J3-4; D1, D-2.5a, D-4.1a, D-4.2a, D-4.8a, D-4.10a, D-M4.5a, D-7.1b, D-7.2b.2a, D-7.2b.3a, as far as they describe the bot App writing proof, LFS media, purge, or tombstones | Founder decision (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): proof must be the least-friction design that still keeps the rule that the builder cannot certify its own work. A product repository needs no bot App, no media bucket, no secrets, and no purge job for proof. The plan's design needed an App installation token in CI (D1: the workflow token can never write `ledger`), media storage the team pays for and purges, and a daily purge loop that itself needed the App (D-7.1b) | The merge gate (`pull_request_target`, base-branch copy) builds the pack from what CI ran on the PR head, as before, and keeps `pack.json` with every artifact as the run artifact `aayudha-pack` for `[proof] retention_days`, clamped to the repository's Actions limit (`GITHUB_RETENTION_DAYS`, else 90). Job `aayudha/pack` (workflow token, `contents: read`, `pull-requests: write`, no App, no secret) runs the new `aayudha proof comment <story> --ci`: it re-checks the pack against the PR head and the Ready snapshot and upserts one `<!-- aayudha:proof -->` PR comment with the criteria verdict table, the floor table, the SHA-256 of `pack.json`, a link to the artifact, and the date it expires; `aayudha/pack` fails with it. CI writes nothing to `ledger`. `aayu ship` (a human) finds the pack through the Actions API (D-4.2b.a), checks every artifact's bytes against `pack.json`, and only after the human pass, just before the merge, writes `proof/<story>/<head>/authoritative/pack.json` and its data artifacts (text, never media) plus the ship record (`gate_run`, `artifact`, `pack_sha256`) under the human's identity. A refused ship writes nothing. Agent previews (`proof push`, any host conversation) stay advisory (`<!-- aayudha:proof-advisory -->`), `proof push --ci` is refused, and no preview satisfies ship. Media never go to `ledger` or Git LFS; LFS stays only for design manifests and review baselines on private repositories (WP6). V1's bot identity remains for agent lanes; it is no longer needed for proof. V3's intent (no media in git, bounded retention) holds, by a different store: the run artifact. The V-table rows are unchanged in text; this row records where V1's CI column, V3's storage column, §0.1's CI row, and M4 no longer apply |
| D-4.2b.a | S-4.2b: ship verifies that the artifact came from the merge-gate run on the exact head | The workflow-runs API gives a run's `path`, `event`, `head_sha`, `head_branch`, and conclusion, but nothing that binds it to the pull request's base branch: `pull_requests` lists the open pull requests whose head matches (PR 3 into `dev` on the scratch run 38762241501) and is empty once they close (run 38752748439 of this repository), so a second PR from the same head into another base would appear there too. Artifacts cannot be attached to a run from outside it; only the run's own jobs upload them | `gatePack` (`internal/cli/ship.go`) lists runs of `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` for the PR's head branch, newest first, and accepts the first with path `.github/workflows/aayudha-merge-gate.yml`, event `pull_request_target` (so the workflow file is the base branch's, never the PR's), `head_sha` = the PR head, and `completed/success`, whose `aayudha-pack` artifact is unexpired, unzips without escaping paths, verifies every hash, and is for this story, PR, and head. A refusal (exit 4) names every run it passed over and why. `aayudha/pack` joins ship's required checks. Residuals, in the threat model (T4): the base-branch copy is trusted, so a change to the gate itself must land through review first; PR code runs inside the gate job (sandboxed, D-4.8a) before the upload |
| D-4.2b.b | S-4.2b: retention from `[proof] retention_days` clamped to GitHub's limit | GitHub caps artifact retention at the repository's (or organisation's) setting, at most 90 days for public repositories and 400 for private ones, and exposes it to a run as `GITHUB_RETENTION_DAYS`. An artifact of a public repository can be downloaded by any signed-in GitHub user | `aayu verify --ci` reports `retention_days` = `proof.ArtifactRetention(retention_days, $GITHUB_RETENTION_DAYS)` (0 or above the limit → the limit; no limit known → 90); the gate passes it to `actions/upload-artifact` and the PR comment shows the expiry date. A ship after the artifact expired is refused: re-run the gate. On a public repository media are as visible as the PR for that window, which V3 did not allow; captures use `[verify] seed` data and the redaction pass (WP4.5) as before. The founder accepted this (D-4.2b.g) |
| D-4.2b.c | WP4.2, D-4.2a, D-7.1b, D-7.2b.2a: `proof purge`, tombstones, the `proof-purge` default loop, and media delete | Nothing writes media that need purging any more: the artifact expires on its own, and the ledger copy holds text only. A bucket costs little to keep (push, get, verify, lifecycle are separate from purge) | Removed: `proof purge`, `proof.Purge`, `Media.Delete` (S3, Azure Blob, GCS), `loops.Defaults` and the `secrets` loop key, `loops/proof-purge.toml` and its Amp and Cursor renders, tombstones on new packs, the LFS media path of `proof push`. Kept: `[proof] media` as opt-in for longer retention (`proof push --media` without a bucket now exits 4 on every repository); `proof verify`, `get`, and `list` still read 0.1 packs, including tombstoned ones and `store: lfs` (`TestVerifyReadsPacksFromBefore02`); `audit` reads the same `[proof]` keys, so `TestAuditReplaysRecordedReports` stays byte-identical. New `proof.VerifyLocal` checks a local pack's hashes (a local pack has `store: none`, which plain `Verify` skipped) |
| D-4.2b.d | D1 and WP1.4: `init --identity` replaces the ledger ruleset's admin bypass with the bot App; WP2.5: `.lfsconfig` for media on private ledgers | Ship writes the ledger under the human's identity, so replacing the admin bypass would lock ship out | `ledgerBypassApp` keeps the repository admin role and adds the App beside it. Only a repository admin could ship (the ledger push was refused otherwise) until D-4.2b.h widened it to the write role. `init` still writes the LFS attributes on a private ledger, now for design and baseline media only; the public-repository config scaffold describes the bucket as optional |
| D-4.2b.e | WP1.7: doctor checks the LFS quota | LFS no longer holds proof media | `doctor` gains a `proof` row: the effective artifact retention, a warning for a leftover `loops/proof-purge.toml` (runs a removed verb), and a warning when the merge gate is a 0.1 copy without `aayudha proof comment` (re-run `aayu init --gates`). The LFS quota row now names design and baseline media |
| D-4.2b.f | S-4.2b: prove it end to end on a scratch repository: a PR gets the artifact and the comment, ship refuses a pack from another SHA or a hand-uploaded artifact, and ship succeeds on the real one | GitHub has no API to attach an artifact to a run from outside it, so "hand-uploaded" means an artifact uploaded by a workflow the PR controls: the PR's own copy of the gate file, run on `pull_request`. The scratch repository has no model key, so `aayudha/inspect` cannot review | `TestLiveShipFromGateArtifact` (`internal/cli/proof_live_test.go`; `AAYU_LIVE=1`, `AAYU_LIVE_VERSION` = the commit the scratch gate installs; `AAYU_LIVE_KEEP=1` keeps the repository) on a public aayu-scratch-* repository with the `init --gates` merge gate and no App, bucket, or secret; the inspect step alone is an `echo`. Run on 2026-10-15 against commit 93f54c6, kept as `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-1792079559982603691`: (1) run 38764241082 on 90707dc kept artifact 11988095989 (expires 2027-01-13, the public limit of 90 days) and one PR comment (issuecomment-6196401723, all floors pass, pack hash, artifact link); (2) after a failing commit, run 38765027029 failed and ship exited 4: "run 38765027029: is failure on the head; run 38764241082: proves 90707dc, not the head 801b5da"; (3) the PR added `pull_request` to the gate file and its copy restored the passing code before `verify`: run 38765900626 (`pull_request`) went green with an `aayudha-pack` claiming every floor passes on f3a7658, while the base copy's run 38765896563 failed; ship exited 4: "run 38765900626: a pull_request run, so the workflow file came from the pull request, not the base branch"; nothing reached the ledger; (4) on 294142d, run 38766675139 passed, ship without a human pass exited 4, and after a `Solo-mode bypass:` comment `ship --solo-bypass` merged PR 3 as 47cecd8 and wrote `proof/2/294142d…/authoritative/` (pack.json, ship.json, and text artifacts: coverage, evidence, gitleaks, osv-scanner, semgrep reports) under the founder's account. The founder's token stood in for the human. The pack held no media (no web criterion); `TestBuildAndShip` covers a screenshot that ship hash-checks but never lands |
| D-4.2b.g | V3: media never public; D-4.2b.b: a public repository's run artifact can be downloaded by any signed-in GitHub user | Founder decision (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread): accepted | On a public repository, proof media in the merge gate's `aayudha-pack` artifact are readable by any signed-in GitHub user until the artifact expires, at most 90 days. This is where V3's "no public media" no longer holds; its other intent (no media in git, bounded retention) stands. No code change: captures still use `[verify] seed` data and the redaction pass (WP4.5), and a team that needs less exposure deletes the artifact on GitHub or keeps the repository private. Recorded in the threat model (T14) |
| D-4.2b.h | D1, D-2.5a, D-4.2b.d: the ledger ruleset lets only the admin role (and the bot App) update `ledger`, so only an admin can ship | Founder decision (2026-10-15): anyone with write access may run ship, which merges into `dev` only (promotion to `rolling` and `main` stays a separate, admin-only release step); force pushes and deletion stay blocked. A ruleset bypass actor bypasses every rule of its ruleset (live: with `update` and `non_fast_forward` in one ruleset, the write-role bypass let the admin force-push `ledger`, "Bypassed rule violations … Cannot force-push"), so one ruleset cannot say "writers may update but nobody may rewrite". GitHub accepts `RepositoryRole` 4 (write) as a bypass actor on a personal repository, and it covers higher roles: with only that actor listed, `current_user_can_bypass` is `always` for the admin and the admin's push is a bypass. The Actions workflow token still cannot be a bypass actor (D1) | `aayu init` creates two rulesets on `refs/heads/ledger`: `aayudha-ledger` (`update`; bypass: the write role, plus the bot App after `init --identity`) and `aayudha-ledger-history` (`deletion`, `non_fast_forward`; no bypass actors). On a repository from before 0.2, a rerun creates the history ruleset first and then narrows the old one to `update`, adding the write role and keeping its other actors; an adopted ruleset is left alone. `uninit` deletes the rulesets before the ledger, since the history ruleset now blocks the ledger's deletion for admins too. This repository's own `ledger-writers` gets the same shape, with a `ledger-history` beside it. Live (`TestLiveLedgerWriters`, 2026-10-15, `rahulnakmol/aayu-scratch-1792086941983397991`, kept): `init` created rulesets 25184190 and 25184186; `uninit` removed both and the untouched ledger; after a second `init`, the admin fast-forwarded `ledger` and its force push and deletion were refused ("Cannot force-push to this branch", "Cannot delete this branch"); a `workflow_dispatch` run with `contents: write` was refused updating it (run 38781954189, "Cannot update this protected ref"). Not shown live: a push by a real write collaborator. The only second account available is an Enterprise Managed User, which GitHub refuses as a collaborator on a personal repository; `TestLiveLedgerWriters` runs that case when `AAYU_LIVE_WRITER_TOKEN` names a personal account. `audit` still labels each ledger bypass "founder-identity ledger write by" followed by the pusher's login, whoever pushed; the wording is kept so recorded reports replay byte-identically |

### Scope addition and deviations recorded while implementing WP4.5b (feature maps and behaviour checks, 0.2)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-4.5b | WP4.5: the web floors load each screenshot or recording criterion's URL and photograph it, and behaviour criteria are evidenced only by Go tests named in `.aayudha/evidence/<story>.toml`; WP4.6: k6 thresholds for API and CLI contracts; WP3.2: `install` copies only aayudha's own skills; WP7.1: the loops are the digest, decisions, retro, seat review, and evolve; the `verify` skill body is a stub | Founder decisions (2026-10-15, relayed by the orchestrating thread), after the research in docs/research/verification-skills-2026-10.md: (1) generic, not UI-only: a feature map lists a repository's capabilities and each has a behaviour check of the kind that fits (a UI flow, a CLI command, an API call, a library or function call), with no assumption of user journeys or a browser; (2) the control tool uses the product's own stack (Node and Playwright for web apps, shell or a PTY driver for CLIs, plain HTTP for APIs, the product's test framework for libraries) and adds no runtime the product lacks; (3) behaviour checks block the merge gate at once when a mapped check fails, and run in CI deterministically with no model; (4) a free deterministic drift check runs on every PR; (5) no AI keys in a product repository's CI: the model-driven maintain pass runs weekly and on drift by @-mentioning an agent app the user installed (@claude, @cursor, @amp, @codex) with the maintain brief, and its result is at most one PR of map corrections through the normal gate; (6) the verify skill gains create and maintain procedures, `aayu init --verify-skill` creates it, `install` mirrors it to hosts, grill, plan, and inspect read the map, and a nightly loop sweeps every check. This extends the plan; WP4.5's static captures and WP4.6 stay | WP4.5b (five parts, WP4.5b.1–5), one small PR each to `dev`. The research's journeys become generic behaviour checks (`kind` ui, cli, api, or lib), its control CLI a contract (`<control> check`, JSON in, result and artifacts out) that each product implements in its own stack, and its source pass `aayu verify --map`. Per-PR drift is reported, not blocking (WP4.5b.1), so that the maintain pass's own PR can correct it; the behaviour checks themselves block |

### Scope additions and deviations recorded while implementing the October 2026 research decisions (P1, P4, P7)

Rows are named by WP so parallel threads do not collide on numbers. The research is
[docs/research/video-learnings-2026-10.md](research/video-learnings-2026-10.md); the founder decided its §8 questions
on 2026-10-15 (relayed by the orchestrating thread): measure first (P1), a stricter `evolve` (P4, Q3), ratchets in
this repository only for 0.2 (P7, Q5), never score host agent transcripts (Q6), and no AI keys in a product
repository's CI. Everything below runs with no model.

| # | Plan assumption | Finding | Implemented instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-7.6e | WP7.6 `retro`: estimate against observed checkpoints, corrections, streaks, floor misses, cost per story | Founder decision 1 (P1): retro also reports, from data aayu already has and deterministically, the time per stage, the human wait from gate-green to ship, touches per story, the share of inspect findings acted on, the escape rate (Bugs traced to shipped stories), and the change mix. This extends WP7.6; it replaces nothing | A "Flow (reported, not gated)" section in every retro report, after the estimate table, and `flow` in `retro --json`. No finding, no Feedback issue, no config key, and no `status` line come from it: the numbers are a baseline, and the skill tells the twin not to change a story, a split, or a review to move one (R1 of the research) |
| D-7.6e | S-7.6e; P1 of the research: stage times from "ledger timestamps", touches from "human-only actions in the actions log", acted-on from posted finding IDs, escapes as PRs `inspect` passed that got a correction | The actions log records the GitHub write, not the verb, and in solo mode almost every write carries identity `human` (agents run under the founder's token), so it cannot tell a person's touch from an agent's. Only stories built with `aayu build` have a first checkpoint (`build/<n>/state.json`): 6 of the 46 stories this repository delivered from 2026-10-01. A workflow run has no completion time in the API; `updated_at` is the last change. inspect keeps its state, including the IDs it posted inline (WP4.8b), in its replace-in-place PR comment, so PRs reviewed before inline comments record only their fix cycles. The founder named Bugs, not corrections, for escapes | `internal/audit/flow.go`, run by `aayu retro` only (`Source.Flow`). Per story delivered in the window: Ready is its first `status:ready` label (timeline); the first checkpoint the earliest `at` in its build record on the ledger; gate green the `updated_at` of the earliest successful `pull_request_target` run of `aayudha-merge-gate.yml` on the merge PR's final head SHA that completed by the merge; ship the merge (with or without `aayu ship`). Stages: queue (Ready → first checkpoint), build (→ gate green), human wait (→ ship), total (Ready → ship); each counts only stories with both ends and reports P50 and P90 by nearest rank, with "n of N stories". Touches: comments and reviews by a person on the story or its merge PR between Ready and ship (not a `…[bot]` or `[identity] bot_logins` login, not a comment carrying an `<!-- aayudha:` marker, not an approving review, which is ship's human pass), plus each later `status:ready` label and the PR's WP5.7 corrections other than changes-requested (already counted as a review); an agent writing as the founder without a marker still counts as a person. inspect: over PRs merged into dev in the window with an inspect comment, fix cycles, and each posted ID: dismissed when `inspect/dismissed.jsonl` on the ledger names it for that PR, open when the latest inspection still lists it, else acted on; the share is acted on ÷ posted. Escape rate: stories with a Bug (from the WP5.7 fetch: `type:bug` issues updated since the oldest of the 100 newest merged PRs) opened after the merge, before the cursor and within `[autonomy] correction_days`, whose body links the story or its merge PR (`#n`, `/pull/n`, `/issues/n`), and which that PR does not itself fix (a Bug filed first and fixed by the PR is not an escape). Change mix: the Conventional Commit type of every PR title merged into dev in the window, `other` when it has none. The GitHub inputs snapshot gains `gate_runs`, `bugs`, PR `head_sha`, a PR item's `inspect` state, and a comment's `aayu` flag, written by retro only, so audit's snapshots and golden are unchanged; the build records and dismissals come from the ledger at the recorded commit, so `retro --replay` is byte-identical. A retro report written before this change replays with the binary that wrote it (it has no Flow section). Test: `TestRetroFlow` (hand-computed and asymmetric: a 24-hour human wait beside a 1-hour one, three touches against a bot comment, a marker comment, and an approval that do not count, an escape against a Bug filed before the merge and one the PR fixes, a dismissal of the same finding ID on another PR, and a PR into `main` outside the mix; the numbers survive a snapshot round trip). First run on this repository (dry run, 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-16T00:00Z, 46 stories and bugs, 135 PRs): total Ready to ship P50 2.5 h, P90 6.4 h (45 stories); queue P50 1.6 h and build P50 1.2 h (6 stories); human wait P50 0.0 h, P90 0.1 h (12 stories: in solo mode the agent ships as soon as the gate is green); touches P50 1, P90 2; inspect reviewed 25 PRs with 22 fix cycles and recorded 1 posted finding, still open at merge (0 % acted on); escape rate 0 of 46; change mix feat 52 %, fix 27 %, docs 10 %, chore 4 %, test 4 %, ci 2 %, refactor 1 % |
